# THE CROSSING CAMPAIGN — the window theorem as a campaign of stages, each with its instrument, its decider and its falsifier, oriented for days or years; preregistered before any stage's engine is written (Edwin Boston III, 2026-11-01 03:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat iii-43; the operator's orders of 03:1x — no scope minimization, no time limits, the goal the complete mass gap — and of 03:2x — the scale-uniform inequality as one lemma, its constant attacked)

> **CORRECTION OF RECORD, 2026-11-05 03:41 EST (read from `date`): THE BOUND OF 4 ON EVERY CONTACT LEAF IS FALSE, AND EVERY THRESHOLD ABOVE 0.3650 ON THIS PAGE FALLS WITH IT.** A Z₂-minimal contact leaf of 46 plaquettes in ℤ⁴ (eight four-fold contact links, two unit squares sharing a vertex; a sphere sheet) reads S = 20/3 on four roads — the sum over its 6,561 resolutions and the cut formula in `boxenum_wff.c`, the word integral 5/6 in `wordsys4.c` and on the 120 icosians in `wordsys_icosian_check.c` — where Lemma S12’s staked half allows 4; by Schur’s lemma the word systems of k such blocks read 4(5/3)^k, so no constant bounds them. The thresholds 0.4764, 0.4816, 0.5050, 0.5140, 0.5100 and 0.5198 were readings of the certificate with its tail at that bound, and no theorem stands on them. WHAT STANDS: the lattice gap at β ≤ 0.2452 on proved lemmas, and the flux-surface gas’s certificate at β ≤ 0.3650 (0.3656 in the tree dressing; 0.3588 on two roads) with the tail at the all-spin bound, under the four inputs of THE PROOF’s Step 17 (e2ce30b). A coupling above 0.3650 needs a proved bound 4θ^F on the contact leaves of the tail. Every sentence below that states 0.4764 or a larger coupling is read under this notice.

> Status: a CHARTER, the page before every organ of it (the house order). It consumes THE FAR SHORE (OPEN/the-far-shore.md: the chain in hand, the walls measured, the roads refuted, §10's lemma and its exit budget) and THE CROSSING (OPEN/the-crossing.md: the open part at its sharpest), and it names the stages from the lemma's exact form to the continuum gap for every compact simple group. Each stage lands under its own page line, its gate and its teeth; a stage's cost is a counted state space or a measured run, never an hour; the abelian control runs first at every stage; nothing above β = 0.4764 is claimed until the assembly stage closes; no stage is deferred because it is long. The campaign has no end date and no checkpoint that reads as an end.

## §0 — THE GOAL IN THE EXACT WORDS, AND THE SHAPE OF THE PROOF

**The prize.** For every compact simple gauge group G, a non-trivial quantum Yang–Mills theory exists on ℝ⁴ (the Wightman/Osterwalder–Schrader axioms with the theory's local gauge-invariant observables) and has a mass gap Δ > 0.

**The shape in hand (THE GROUND §7–8, THE FAR SHORE §2).** The continuum gap of every limit point of a construction of the corpus's kind follows from two finite statements at one fixed physical scale a_c (Theorem 8.5): Hypothesis I, the blocked measure's coarse variables under a Dobrushin-type condition with an exponential weight, uniformly in the cutoff; Hypothesis II, the field inside a block clusters at the block scale (exact under the perfect decimation). The chain MassGapChain.lean closes the gap with one rate for every cutoff from the UV interface (every effective weight at a_c a tilt of one base weight) and the crossover budget. The flux-surface gas (Theorem 8.9) is a convergent expansion for β ≤ β* = 0.4764 with a certified margin.

**The one open link, stated at its sharpest (THE FAR SHORE §4.1, §10).** The window theorem: the blocked theory crosses the couplings of order one, [β_D, β_P], with its correlation length finite at the exit. In the lemma's form: at an exit scale where the blocked Wilson coupling is β_c ≤ β*, the blocked action's non-Wilson content c(β_c) lies under the gas's exit budget ε(β_c), uniformly in the cutoff. ε is certified (§10 RESULTS 1: 2.32 at 0.05, 0.68 at 0.25, 0.00 at β*). c is unread. The campaign is the proof that c ≤ ε at some exit, for SU(2) first, for every compact simple group last.

**The two controls that bind every stage.** (F1) four-dimensional U(1) at weak coupling has no gap and no exit scale: every stage must REFUSE it — a stage whose instrument or theorem treats U(1) as SU(2) is withdrawn at that stage. (F2) the free massless Gaussian field: algebraic correlations with no frustration; any criterion monotone in averaged per-site influences certifies it falsely.

## §2 — THE STANDARDS OF THE CAMPAIGN (beside the house's)

1. **The page before the organ**, at every stage and sub-stage; a RESULTS note dated from `date` after every run; no stake edited after the fact.
2. **The abelian control first.** U(1) at 1.2 (the Coulomb phase) and at 0.9 (the confining phase, the sighted control) run before SU(2) at every instrument; a reading that does not part them is not a reading of the crossing.
3. **The datum is a variable, not a choice.** Every finite-object reading at the ordered, the Haar-random and the cancelling datum; the measure-weighted datum named as the quantity a theorem needs.
4. **Costs are counted or measured.** A state space is counted by the fusion rule before an engine is written; a run's seconds are its record's; no cost is a session's length.
5. **A proxy is withdrawn the hour its object is found to differ** (§10 RESULTS 2: a fine correlation is not a coarse coupling; a single-plaquette correction is not content).
6. **Nothing above β = 0.4764 is claimed until W6 closes**; every number above it carries its stage and its conditions.
7. **The strong end is kept clean while the campaign runs**: S12's remainder (W1), the exact table at k = 15, 16 and the five classes (the engines in flight), the kernel record's refresh after every GREEN of the big gate.

## §3 — THE ORDER OF WORK, FROM HERE

1. Land this charter (both repositories) — before W1's text and before W2's count.
2. W1: the lemma written out with its proof and its content defined; S1 §27's road for S12 continued (the resolution sum on the lattice's local laws, the even-star table its alphabet); the certificate's budget mode re-read against the lemma's own a(X).
3. W2's first act: the state-space count of the two-face object's cut at spins ≤ 2 (a fusion count, a page line with the number); then the engine, its known answers, its gate arm, its three data, its U(1) column.
4. W3 on the same engine; the map's U(1) refusal first.
5. The big gate's landings and the kernel record's refresh as they come; W5's lemmas landed one at a time beside W2–W4, since they do not wait on the crossing.
6. W4 when W2–W3 have given the theorem its known answers and the shape of the decay; W6–W8 thereafter.

## §4 — WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED, SAID ONCE

The continuum gap; the window theorem; any coupling above β = 0.4764; the existence of the exit for SU(2) beyond the certified descent's reading; the content's decay before it is read. This page claims the ORDER and the STANDARDS of the work, and nothing of its result.

## §5 — W1 BEGUN: THE LEMMA'S EXACT FORM, ENTERED WHERE THE BUDGET LIVES (2026-11-01 03:4x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a dated note, the rows above unedited)

The lemma's mathematics is ONE COPY, at OPEN/the-far-shore.md §10 RESULTS 3, beside the budget it compares against; this section points at it and records what it changes on this page.

1. **W1's row read "fine polymers" and defined the content by a sup norm.** RESULTS 3 (a) shows the sup-norm content does not pair with the exact-pricing budget of §10 RESULTS 1: a bond inside the Haar integral prices every surface it touches crudely. The row's words stand as written (no stake is edited); the correction is dated there and here. The polymers at the exit scale are the WILSON polymers of the exit gas.
2. **The proof's quantity is the RELATIVE FOURIER CONTENT** c_rel of RESULTS 3 (c): each bond's Peter–Weyl modes in units of the Wilson coefficients they create or modify, summed through a plaquette with the weight e^{α|L(C)|}. The two structural facts (b1), (b2) put the decorated gas inside the gas's own alphabet; the owed PRICING-COMPARISON LEMMA (that a mode's coefficient enters A2's crossing law and A3's patch law as a scalar) is W1's second item, and the resolution-sum road for S12 its third.
3. **The amplification is a number before it is a hope**: a mode that creates spin-½ flux is charged 1/(2a_½(β)), from 40 at β = 0.05 to 4.2 at 0.4764; a spin-1 mode 1/(3a_1), from 3200 to 36; a mode that modifies an existing plaquette is charged once (RESULTS 3 (d)).
4. **W2's output, sharpened (a dated note under its row, the row unedited):** the two-face object's moment table E[χ_a(A)χ_c(B)] is the Fourier data the lemma consumes; W2 reports the pair coupling's modes IN WILSON UNITS, their sum r_C, and c_rel at a plaquette by the twenty adjacent pairs, with the relative-orientation mode (the dependence on tr(U_A U_B^{±1})) read beside the class-function part — never a sup norm. The count that is W2's first act is unchanged by this.
5. **Owed under W1, by name** (RESULTS 3 (h)): the pricing-comparison lemma; a DRESS=crude mode of the certificate under the existing tilt, for the sup-norm form's own budget (W1(a), the Osterwalder–Seiler regime); the coefficient-supplied certificate mode, since the exit gas's one-plaquette coefficients are not Bessel ratios (RESULTS 3 (f)); the second seat's read of (b)–(c); S12's remainder.

The next act is W2's count (§3, item 3), with §1's W2 row read together with note 4 above.

## §6 — W2'S FIRST ACT: THE COUNT (2026-11-01 03:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk act on g41's index structure, STDLIB/build/pentapylon/g41_cube.c, before a line of the engine is written)

**The object, exactly.** Face A: the 2×2 block of fine plaquettes in the xy-plane, x, y ∈ [0, 2], z = 0; face B: the 2×2 block in the yz-plane, y, z ∈ [0, 2], x = 0. They share the coarse edge x = z = 0, y ∈ [0, 2] — the two fine links y ∈ [0, 1] and [1, 2]. Plaquettes: eight (p1, p2 A's far column x ∈ [1, 2]; p3, p4 A's near column; p5, p6 B's near column z ∈ [0, 1]; p7, p8 B's far column). Links: twenty-two — fourteen edge links (twelve private, two shared) and eight cross links (the four through each face's centre). Coarse loops: A = the product of the eight edge links of face A, B = of face B; both traverse the two shared links. Every object link also lies in plaquettes outside the object (six per link in ℤ⁴): five staples on a private edge link, four on a shared edge link, four on a cross link; the staples' other links are THE DATUM (ordered, Haar-random, cancelling, as the W2 row says).

**The representation content.** Each object plaquette carries a spin s ≤ 4 in g41's integer label (spin 2), weight (s + 1) a_s χ_{s/2}; each staple likewise; the loop representations a (on A's eight edge links) and c (on B's) are carried as g41 carries a resolved face's: as a chain of matrices D^a(U_ℓ^{±1}) along the loop, so that the insertion is χ_a(A) for the class moments and, for the relative-orientation modes, the contraction of the two chains' ends through the channel k ∈ a ⊗ a ∩ c ⊗ c at the basepoint (0, 0, 0). A link's tensor is g41's: the Haar quadrature of the representation matrices on it against the product of its staples' weights with the datum inside — blocks (s, a) of size d_s² d_a² on a private edge link, (s, s′) of size d_s² d_{s′}² on a cross link, (s, s′, a, c) of size d_s² d_{s′}² d_a² d_c² on a shared link.

**THE COUNT, scheme Σ (the column sweep).** The strip is swept column by column — A-far, A-near, B-near, B-far — the column's two spins as outer sectors, the cut between columns crossing two links. The state at a cut is the open index pairs of the two cut links' tensors on the unprocessed side (d_s² each, the two plaquettes' spins being the sector) times the open ends of every loop chain opened and not yet closed (d_a² for A, d_c² for B):
 • the A-face cut (between A's columns): Σ_{s, s′ ≤ 4} d_s² d_{s′}² · d_a² = 55² · d_a² = 3025 d_a² states in all; the largest sector d_s² d_{s′}² d_a² = 625 d_a²;
 • the shared cut and the B-face cut: both chains open, 3025 d_a² d_c² in all; the largest sector 625 d_a² d_c².
 At a, c ≤ 2 (the modes to spin 1, d = 3): 27,225 states at the A-face cut, 245,025 at the shared and B-face cuts, the largest sector 50,625. At a, c ≤ 4 (d = 5): 75,625 and 1,890,625, the largest sector 390,625.
 THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE ENGINE: g41 forms its ring's transfer matrices DENSE (state 2,310 at WMAX = 4; the three products) — here the shared cut's largest sector squared is 2.6 × 10⁹ entries at a, c ≤ 2 (41 GB complex) and 1.5 × 10¹¹ at a, c ≤ 4, so THE SWEEP MUST BE MATRIX-FREE: a state vector from the A-far end to the B-far end, each link's tensor contracted into it in turn (the object has two ends, not a ring; nothing is traced). Its cost is then the link tensors' quadrature — (Σ_s d_s²)⁴ · N_pt ≈ 9.2 × 10⁶ · N_pt at a shared link, (Σ_s d_s²)³ · N_pt ≈ 1.7 × 10⁵ · N_pt elsewhere, N_pt ≈ 3,600 at g41's rule for this degree, so ≈ 3 × 10¹⁰ multiply-adds at the two shared links and ≈ 10⁹ at the twenty others — and the sweep itself, Σ over links of the state times the link's contracted pair, ≲ 10⁸. MINUTES per (β, datum, (a, c) cap) on this machine, not the afternoon the charter's row estimated for the whole stage; the whole W2 table (four β, three data, a, c ≤ 4, the k-channels) is of the order of an hour of machine time. The count supersedes the estimate (standard 4).

**The known answers the gate arm will hold, sharpened by the count (dated notes under the W2 row).**
 (K1) THE FREE OBJECT HAS CONTENT ZERO — EXACTLY, AND THE BENT ONE TOO: with the staples off (g41's FREE), the object's 2-complex is contractible, so after gauge fixing its eight plaquette holonomies are independent with the weights w; A is the product of four of them and B of the other four (with transports that integrate away), hence W(A, B) = W(A) W(B) with W(A) = Σ_s d_s a_s⁴ χ_{s/2}(A), the two-dimensional law — for the bent object as for the planar one. The row's "planar 2×4 control (content zero, Migdal)" is this statement and holds only with the staples off: WITH the staples the coplanar pair's content is a reading, not a zero. K1 is therefore two KATs: the planar free object and the bent free object, both to the closed form, every mode of the joint law equal to the product of the marginals' to rounding.
 (K2) THE PARTITION FUNCTION OF THE FREE OBJECT: Z = a_0⁸ = 1 in g41's normalisation (∫ w = 1), the chains closed at a = c = 0.
 (K3) THE ORDERED DATUM BY A SECOND ROAD: with every outside link the identity, each link's external weight is w(U)^k, a class function, and the 1×1 object (two fine plaquettes sharing one link, the cube's "top × side0" geometry with the cube's other faces as staples) is a two-plaquette spin network with link weights — its joint law a finite character sum computable in awk with no quadrature; the engine at 1×1 faces and the ordered datum must meet it (law 6: no shared line). g41's own pair reading at the unit cube is NOT this object (its other four faces are object plaquettes, not staples) and is not a known answer here.
 (K4) THE TEETH, named now: the fusion rule (a bent Clebsch–Gordan range in the chain contraction — K1 must leave); the datum (a bent staple sign — the Haar-random reading must move while the ordered one does not at the identity); the chain (a bent orientation on one loop link — K2 must leave).

**What W2 reports (the row read with §5 note 4):** at each (β, datum): the joint law of (A, B) mod conjugation as its modes (a, c, k), a, c ≤ 4; the marginals' modes; the pair coupling's modes b̂(a, c, k) of e^{δ₂} − 1 by the log on the class grid; each mode IN WILSON UNITS by RESULTS 3 (c) — a mode (1, 0), (0, 1), (1, 1) that modifies a spin-½ plaquette charged once, a mode that creates flux charged against the unit (2a′_½)² or (3a′_1)² — and r_C, c_rel by the twenty adjacent pairs; the U(1) column beside.

**The next act:** the engine on g41's bones under this count — matrix-free, two ends, the chains as g41's resolved-face indices; K1–K3 first, the gate arm with K4's teeth, then the three data at β = 2.2.

## §7 — W2'S FIRST LIGHT: THE ENGINE, THE FOUR DATA, THE DATUM AS THE EFFECT (2026-11-01 04:4x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; RESULTS after the runs, no stake above edited)

**The engine** (STDLIB/build/aperanton/surface-gas/w2_strip.c, landed 0d69fcc50; the planar object added 04:3x): the §6 design built as written, with two repairs found by its own records — the first link order opened one plaquette on four wires at face B's far column (a state of 1.85 × 10⁹ entries at a = c = 4; the order now keeps every cycle at two open wires, 1.04 × 10⁸) and the loops' junction wires open from the chain's first link (the §6 count had d_a² at the A-face cut; the engine carries d_a² there and d_a² d_c² from the shared cut on, as counted). Known answers: the free object (bent and planar) Z = 1, E[χ_a(A)] = d_a a_a⁴, every connected pair zero to 10⁻⁹, Σ_k f_k = the class moment and Σ_k P_k = 1 to 10⁻¹⁵; the ordered datum swept forward and reversed to twelve equal digits; the representation and projector identities at load. Cost at WMAX = LMAX = 4 (spins to 2, loop representations to 2): the link tensors 61–88 s, the twenty-five sweeps 60–100 s, 143–186 s per (β, datum) at 12–16 threads; the W2 table of the row is machine-hours, as §6 counted.

**The post-processor** (w2_modes.awk): the truncated joint class density on a 256² Chebyshev-U grid, the ratio to the marginals, the modes of e^{δ₂} − 1 and of δ₂, each mode's kind (modifying, creating) and share, and the certificate's three inputs of the far shore's RESULTS 4 (c) as one W2-CERT-CALL line. ITS FIRST RUN REFUSED UNANIMOUSLY — 65,536 of 65,536 cells non-positive — and the operator read the unanimity as a pattern or a test flaw: it was the flaw (the record's eighth field is the literal "(imag", every moment read zero), fixed, and the instrument now refuses by name before any grid when the normalised (0,0) moment is not 1 — a dead table is unanimous, a truncation is not.

**THE READINGS at β = 2.2, L = 4 (bent object; records w2_strip.2026-11-01a.<datum>.txt, w2_modes.2026-11-01a.<datum>.txt):**

| datum | E[χ_½(A)] | E[χ_1(A)] | ⟨χ_½χ_½⟩_c | sup\|δ₂\| | r_mod | s_half / s_one per pair | half factor 1 + 16 r_mod | wall floor a′_1 + 16 s_one/3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ordered | 0.815 | 0.277 | 0.0207 | 0.895 | 0.0355 | 1.02 / 0.53 | 1.567 | 2.91 |
| cancelling | 0.561 | 0.111 | 0.105 | 1.474 | 0.0759 | 1.64 / 0.84 | 2.215 | 4.53 |
| random:1 | 0.038 | −0.0018 | 0.00089 | 0.0054 | 1.2·10⁻⁵ | 0.060 / 0.026 | 1.0002 | 0.139 |
| random:2 | −0.012 | −0.0038 | 0.0020 | 0.0124 | 5.2·10⁻⁵ | 0.087 / 0.033 | 1.0008 | 0.176 |

**What the table says.** (1) THE DATUM IS THE EFFECT: the coarse loop's expectation runs from 0.81 (the ordered outside) to ±0.01–0.04 (a Haar-random outside), the pair coupling's sup norm from 0.9 to 0.005, the modifying share across three orders of magnitude — at a 2×2 block of the β = 2.2 Wilson measure, the outside decides the inside. The W2 row's falsifier's second clause has fired: a datum share of the order of the effect, with no measure-weighted reading possible on this object — so W3 CARRIES THE DATUM AS A VARIABLE, and the measure-weighted datum is W4's object (the chessboard estimate as a thermodynamic integral, THE FAR SHORE §5), not a number this instrument produces. (2) THE ORDERED DATUM ERASES THE GEOMETRY: the planar and the bent object give the same nine class moments at the ordered datum to twelve digits, because every staple then contributes w(U_ℓ) and only the staple census (12 five, 10 four) survives — the two objects part only at a non-trivial datum. (3) THE FIRST STEP IS FAR FROM ANY EXIT, as expected and now measured: at the ordered and cancelling data the certificate's inputs (a′_½ = 0.41 and 0.28; the half factor 1.6 and 2.2; the wall floor 2.9 and 4.5 against an exit's a_1 ≈ 0.003) refuse the gas outright; at the random data the one-plaquette coefficients are those of a gas near β ≈ 0.15 with a wall floor still 0.14–0.18 — the created-flux share, not the modifying one, is what the recursion must bring down. (4) The created-flux ratios of RESULTS 3's c_rel grew with the mode (12 at (4, 4) ordered, larger at the random data), which is why RESULTS 4 prices the created plaquette at the bond's own coefficient and the ratio is retired. ## §8 — W3 RE-DESIGNED BY W2'S FINDING: THE TWO-STEP OBJECT, COUNTED (2026-11-01 04:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk act under standards 4 and 5; the W3 row's words stand, this section corrects their object)

**The proxy withdrawn.** The W3 row defined the content's recursion as the one-step map (β, δ₂) ↦ (β′, δ₂′) at a FIXED fine datum. §7's table makes that map a proxy for the wrong object: at the ordered datum the blocked one-plaquette coefficient a′_½ = 0.41 is Wilson's at β ≈ 3.5, above the fine coupling; at a Haar-random datum it is Wilson's at β ≈ 0.08 to 0.15; the "map" runs up or down by the datum's choice, and neither datum is the measure's. (And the typical outside at β = 2.2 is nearer the ordered datum than the random one: the plaquette expectation there is about 0.57.) The RG map's datum is the measure-weighted one, and the only way to carry it exactly is to keep it INSIDE THE OBJECT: a larger exact object whose fine fluctuations are integrated with the outside fixed one scale further out. The recursion's first honest reading is therefore THE TWO-STEP OBJECT, not a map iterated at a fixed datum.

**The two-step object.** Face A the 4×4 block of fine plaquettes in the xy-plane, face B the 4×4 block in the yz-plane, sharing the coarse-coarse edge x = z = 0, y ∈ [0, 4] (four fine links); the loops A and B of sixteen fine links each; thirty-two plaquettes, seventy-six links (forty per face, four shared), the staples five on a private edge link, four on a shared or cross link, as before. Its joint law of (A, B) is the TWO-STEP blocked pair coupling at the fine datum — the bonds of the first step are inside the object, exact, no mode truncation, no bond order: the content at the second scale read directly. THE COUNT (scheme Σ, the column sweep, one column = four plaquettes, the cut four links): the cut state Σ over the four cut plaquettes' spins of Π d² = (Σ_{s≤WMAX} d_s²)⁴ times the open loop chains — 55⁴ · d_a² = 9.2 × 10⁶ d_a² at WMAX = 4 on the A-face cuts, 55⁴ · d_a² d_c² from the shared cut on. At a, c ≤ 1 (the spin-½ moments, the dominant (½, ½) mode of δ₂ and the marginal E[χ_½]): 1.5 × 10⁸ states, 2.3 GB complex, two vectors in flight — feasible; at a, c ≤ 2: 7.4 × 10⁸, 12 GB, feasible on this machine (the engine has held 1.85 × 10⁹ once, §7); at a, c ≤ 4: 5.7 × 10⁹, 90 GB, out of reach. The link tensors are the 2×2 object's (two plaquette slots and two loop slots at most: 55² · d_a² d_c² entries), cheap. The sweep's cost is the state times the contracted pairs times the links: ≈ 1.5 × 10⁸ · 25 · 76 ≈ 3 × 10¹¹ per sweep at a, c ≤ 1, four sweeps; hours per (β, datum), overnight for the three data. The THREE-STEP object (8×8 faces) has a cut of eight links, 55⁸ ≈ 8 × 10¹³ states: OUT OF REACH, by the count, for any truncation that keeps spin 2 — exact objects reach two steps and no further, and past them the recursion is W4's expansion or nothing.

**CORRECTED 07:4x (a side reader's catch, read against the engine's own refusal; the trunk seat):** the feasibility line above counts the COLUMN sweep, whose loop chain is d_a² d_c², and the engine runs no column sweep: its generated column order deferred the shared links and opened eight plaquettes at once (§10's 23 GB runaway), the row order seven, and the SLAB sweep that holds four plaquettes open carries face B's chain at d_c⁴ — §10's refusal measured it, 30⁴ · 3² · 3⁴ = 5.9 × 10⁸ entries at spins to 3/2 and loop representations to 1, 9.4 GB a vector. At spins to 2 and loop representations to 1 the slab sweep's state is 55⁴ · 9 · 81 = 6.7 × 10⁹ entries, 107 GB a vector — not 7.4 × 10⁸ and 12 GB, and feasible on no host of this size, quiet or not. The full-truncation two-step reading therefore needs a sweep that keeps B's chain at d_c² without opening the shared links' plaquettes early (none in hand: the shared links lie on both faces' near columns), a factorisation of the loop chain, or a cap below spins to 2; §10's contraction ×0.1–0.23 is read at spins to 1 and loop representations to 1 (W2L2), the crude cap, and the owed run of §10 is the spins-to-3/2 one at 9.4 GB a vector, nothing above it.

**What it reads, and what it does not.** The content at the second scale at each of the three fine data, beside §7's first-scale content at the same data: THE FIRST MEASURED STEP OF THE DECAY, bond-exact. It does not read the measure-weighted datum (the outside of the 4×4 object is still a fine datum two scales out) — it moves the datum one scale further away, which is what a convergent recursion would do at every step and what the object can do twice.

**The truncation check that must precede it (owed first, cheap):** the 2×2 object at WMAX = 6 against WMAX = 4 at β = 2.2 (S1's RESULTS 16.32 read a 40 % move of the square's blocked coupling between spin 1 and spin 2; the spin-2-to-3 move is unread; the cut state at WMAX = 6 is 140² · 625 ≈ 1.2 × 10⁷, minutes).

**The engine's change:** the face size n ∈ {2, 4} as a parameter of w2_strip.c (the plaquettes, the loops' steps, the column order from the geometry; the known answers unchanged: the free object's content zero, the forward and reversed sweeps equal, the representation and projector identities); the staple census re-derived by the code, not pinned by hand (the 4×4 object's private edge links are twelve per face... the count is the code's to print and the gate's to pin after a run, never before).

**The order of work from here:** the WMAX = 6 check; the engine at n = 4 and its free known answers; the two-step object at a, c ≤ 1 on the three data; §9 the reading. W4's formulation (the environment as an expansion in the coarse variables, the window's content datum-uniform or weighed) proceeds on the page beside it, since no exact object reaches it.

## §9 — W4'S FORMULATION, AND ITS FIRST INSTRUMENT: THE INFLUENCE LADDER (2026-11-01 06:2x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk act under the charter, the W4 row's words standing; the two-step object's reading follows as §10 when its matched-cap runs end)

**What the exact objects can and cannot do, after §7–§8 and the two-step object's first light (its free known answer met at n = 4 at spins to 1 and 3/2 with four plaquettes open; its readings at matched caps in §10).** They read the blocked pair coupling as a function of the fine datum one and two scales out, exactly, and no further (§8's count: the three-step object has 55⁸ states). They cannot integrate the datum: the measure-weighted outside is the whole lattice. So W4 cannot be read off them; it must be a theorem whose HYPOTHESES the objects read. The theorem's shape is fixed by THE GROUND's own chain: Hypothesis I (Theorem 8.5) is a Dobrushin-type condition on the blocked measure's coarse variables with an exponential weight — the influence of one coarse variable's value on another's conditional law, summed with the weight, below one. The window theorem is then: the influence row sum of the blocked measure at the scale a_c, read uniformly in the cutoff, is below one. That hypothesis is EXACTLY what a finite object reads when its outside is varied one link at a time: the two-face object's loop law under a rotation of one outside link is the influence of that link on the coarse variable A — THE GROUND's Hypothesis I at the fine datum, link by link.

**THE INFLUENCE LADDER (the instrument, counted).** For the one-step and the two-step objects at a coupling β and a datum, rotate ONE outside link ℓ by an angle θ (about a fixed axis; three angles: π/2, π, and a small one for the derivative) and read the coarse loop's class law (its moments E[χ_a(A)], a ≤ 2, and the total variation of the truncated class density against the unrotated one); the INFLUENCE of ℓ on A at angle θ is that total variation; the ROW SUM at scale s is Σ_ℓ sup_θ influence — the Dobrushin quantity for the coarse variable A against the fine outside. The outside links fall into SYMMETRY CLASSES under the object's own symmetries (reflections of the faces, the exchange of the two faces where the object admits it) and by distance; one reading per class times the class's multiplicity gives the row sum. Cost: one sweep set per (class, angle): the one-step object at spins to 3/2 under a second; the two-step object 2 minutes per set (§9), so for ~30 classes and three angles about three hours per (β, datum) — machine time, not an estimate. g41's `resp` mode is the one-step instrument's ancestor (the cube's 14 link classes against a grid of angles, 16.20 S2); the engine gains a `resp` mode of its own: the datum rotated at one link, the class law read, the TV printed beside the unrotated law.

**What the ladder decides.** (i) THE ROW SUM BY SCALE: if the row sum FALLS from one step to two at the ordered and cancelling data (the typical outside at 2.2 being nearer them than the Haar-random one), the influence of the fine outside on the coarse variable contracts with the scale, which is the direction Hypothesis I needs; if it does not fall, the window theorem is not a Dobrushin theorem at these scales and says so. (ii) THE DISTANCE PROFILE: the influence by distance of the link from the loop — an exponential profile is the weight Hypothesis I carries; a flat one refutes the weight. (iii) THE U(1) REFUSAL: the same ladder on the U(1) object at β = 1.2 (the Coulomb phase, no gap) must NOT contract — if it does, the ladder is group-blind and is withdrawn as a decider (the charter's F1); at β = 0.9 it may. Two scales are two rungs; the ladder's decay law is not a reading at two rungs, and the page says so where any ratio is quoted.

**CORRECTED 07:4x (the quantity named exactly, after a reviewer's read; the trunk seat):** the ladder's first reader took the TOTAL VARIATION of the truncated class density, and the name is wrong twice. The truncated density is SIGNED where the moments are large — the ordered one-step base at W2L1 has E[χ_½] = 0.62 and 1 + 2E·χ_½ < 0 on 51 of 256 grid cells — so its L¹ distance is no total variation there; and a rotation by π is ONE POINT of the sup over a link's values that a Dobrushin row takes, read on a truncated law besides, so no reading here is licensed against a threshold of one, and the page says "above one" nowhere. THE LADDER'S QUANTITY IS THE MOMENT INFLUENCE I(ℓ) = max_{a ≤ L} |ΔE[χ_a(A)]| / d_a — for U(1) |ΔE[e^{iΦ_A}]|, the first moment, the only one the lines kept, exact there since both laws are real (the ordered datum and the angle π are their own conjugates) and |ΔE| ≤ 0.074 < E_base = 0.46–0.58 at every link — the change of a bounded observable of the coarse loop under the rotation of ℓ, a Lipschitz-type influence at one angle, a LOWER BOUND on the row's sup, compared only across scales and groups at MATCHED caps. The readers w2_inf.awk and w2_u1_inf.awk compute it from two records and print the L¹ distance beside it only where both truncated densities are positive, refusing it by name otherwise; w2_ladder.awk reads a ladder whole, with the profile by distance (the least L¹ distance from either endpoint of the link to a vertex of loop A). THE ONE-STEP LADDERS RE-READ (row sums over the 174 outside links, the largest link in brackets): SU(2) ordered at spins to 3/2 and loop representations to 1 (W3L2) 1.190 [0.019] (the L¹ 1.194, the base density positive); SU(2) ordered W2L1 0.712 [0.016] (the L¹ 0.605 withdrawn as a TV, the density signed); SU(2) cancelling W3L2 1.828 [0.176] (L¹ 1.613); U(1) ordered at β = 1.2 2.616 [0.054] (L¹ 1.937), at 0.9 3.407 [0.074] (L¹ 2.264). The profile by distance stands in shape: the mean influence 0.011 / 0.0057 / 4 × 10⁻⁵ / 1 × 10⁻⁵ at distances 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 on the SU(2) ordered W3L2 ladder, a hundredfold fall past the loop's neighbours. §11 reads the two-step ladders with this quantity.

**What W4 still needs beyond the ladder (the theorem proper, not an instrument).** The ladder reads the influence of the FINE outside on the COARSE variable; Hypothesis I is about the influence of coarse variables on coarse variables under the blocked measure, with the fine fluctuations integrated — the ladder's reading is the integrand at a fine datum, and the theorem must bound the integral over the datum by the fine theory's own measure. That is the step no exact object makes, and it is where the classical constructions (Balaban's small-field expansion from the weak-coupling side; the gas from the strong one) would be the tools if either reached the window; neither does. The page keeps this as the open link it is; the ladder is what can be read now.

**The order of work from here:** the `resp` mode on w2_strip_n.c (one link rotated, the class law and its TV read; the symmetry classes of the outside links enumerated by the code); the one-step ladder at β = 2.2 on the three data (minutes); the two-step ladder at the ordered and cancelling data (hours); the U(1) ladder at 1.2 and 0.9 (minutes); §11 the reading.

At spins to 3/2 (W3L1, loop representations to ½; the one-step at that cap unreadable at the ordered and cancelling data, the truncated density not positive, so those cells cite W3L4): the two-step (½,½) mode 1.79·10⁻² / 2.87·10⁻² / 1.18·10⁻⁴ and sup|δ₂| 0.19 / 0.20 / 4.8·10⁻⁴ against the one-step's 7.08·10⁻² / 1.66·10⁻¹ / 3.01·10⁻⁴ and 0.79 / 1.24 / 1.5·10⁻³ at W3L4 — the same direction and size as the matched pair, with the caps' difference said.

**What it says, and what it does not.** (1) The pair coupling of the blocked action CONTRACTS from the first scale to the second at every datum read: the leading mode by ×0.1–0.23, its sup norm by ×0.08–0.27, the modifying share (the spin-1 modes that touch a spin-½ surface's coefficient) by ×0.009–0.07, at the two outsides nearest the measure's typical one. This is W3's first measured step of the decay, bond-exact, and it is the direction the window theorem needs. (2) It is TWO POINTS: a ratio, not a law; no third exact scale exists (§8); nothing is staked on its continuation. (3) The U(1) control at matched caps (§7's correction, 06:2x) contracts too — ×0.38 / ×0.29 at β = 1.2 and ×0.29 / ×0.24 at 0.9 at the two data — so a two-scale contraction of the pair coupling does NOT distinguish the gapless phase from the gapped one, and the charter's F1 is not decided by this reading in either direction; what parts the groups here is only the size of the contraction (SU(2) at 2.2 stronger than U(1) at either coupling), which two points cannot turn into a statement. (4) The truncation at spin 1 is crude at 2.2 (RESULTS 6 (c): spin 2 to 3 moved the one-step moments 1–7 %, the modifying share 17 %); the matched pair shares it, the W3L1 column confirms the direction one spin higher. (5) The datum remains the effect: the two outsides differ in the first-scale content by a factor 3–8 and in the second-scale content by 1.5; the measure-weighted datum is W4's object (§9).

**Owed:** the two-step at spins to 3/2 and loop representations to 1 when the host is quiet (two vectors of 9.4 GB; or a sweep order that keeps the loop chain at d_c², which §8's count showed no slab sweep can); the influence ladders (§9) at both scales, in flight; the planar object at two steps.

## §11 — THE TWO-STEP LADDERS READ (2026-11-01 08:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the quantity of the 07:4x correction, THE MOMENT INFLUENCE, at MATCHED caps; records `ladder1/` and `ladder2/` under STDLIB/build/aperanton/surface-gas and records/surface-gas here, the reader `w2_ladder.awk`)

**The protocol, as §9 staked it before either number was read.** The decider is THE ROW SUM over the object's outside links of the moment influence I(ℓ) = max_{a ≤ L} |ΔE[χ_a(A)]| / d_a under the rotation of ℓ by π at the ordered datum, one step against two at the same caps: (i) it must FALL for the window theorem to be a Dobrushin theorem at these scales; (iii) at U(1), β = 1.2, it must NOT fall, or the ladder is group-blind and is withdrawn as a decider.

**THE READINGS** (row sum [the largest link]; one step 174 outside links, two steps 532; the base densities positive on the grid at both two-step objects):
- SU(2), β = 2.2, ordered, W2L1 (plaquette spins to 1, loop representations to ½): **0.712 [0.0164] → 0.395 [0.0046] — FALLS, ×0.55**, while the outside triples.
- U(1), β = 1.2, ordered, NMAX 6, L 2 (the one-step ladder re-run at the two-step's cap; its NMAX 8 row sum 2.616016 against 2.615992 at NMAX 6, converged to five digits): **2.616 [0.054] → 3.231 [0.034] — RISES, ×1.24.**

**THE VERDICT ON THE STAKED QUANTITY: (i) holds at SU(2), β = 2.2, and (iii) holds at U(1), β = 1.2 — THE LADDER PARTS THE TWO GROUPS AT TWO RUNGS, at the crude cap.** Two rungs are two points: no decay law is read here, and the SU(2) contraction is the DIRECTION Hypothesis I needs, not Hypothesis I — which quantifies over the whole fine outside, every angle of every link, and every block, where this reads one angle, one block, two scales.

**THE CAP ASYMMETRY, named.** The U(1) pair is converged in its cap. The SU(2) pair is at W2L1, where the one-step row sum moves 0.712 → 1.190 under W3L2 (×1.7): the verdict carries that asymmetry on its face. The owed instrument is the two-step W3L1 ladder (the object ran in §10 at ≈ 5 × 10⁷ entries; 532 links at minutes each, a day of this host at the ordered datum), and the W3L2 one behind the 9.4 GB wall (§8's correction).

**Post-hoc columns — added after both results, none the decider.** (a) Per link: the touching mean 0.0054 → 0.00135 (×0.25) at SU(2), 0.027 → 0.016 (×0.60) at U(1). (b) THE RELATIVE INFLUENCE, the row sum divided by the coarse loop's own expectation (E[χ_½(A)] 0.621 → 0.146 at SU(2); E[e^{iΦ_A}] 0.578 → 0.301 at U(1)): 1.15 → 2.70 at SU(2), 4.53 → 10.7 at U(1) — it RISES for both, the coarse loop's expectation falling faster than one link's absolute influence on it. A Dobrushin condition is a statement about LAWS (total variation, bounded observables), hence about the absolute column; the relative one is printed because a reader will ask, and it reads the other way. (c) The pair-coupling column, Σ_ℓ |ΔE[χ_½(A)χ_½(B)]|: 1.79 → 0.28 at SU(2) (E[χ_½χ_½] 0.403 → 0.029).

**THE DISTANCE PROFILE, split by position** (the reviewer's point, taken: at n = 4 the staples over the face's interior sit at distance ≥ 2 from the loop's vertices and couple through the face, so one distance class held two populations; the split is by the link's projection — OVER-A, both endpoints over the block's footprint; OVER-B, beside face B; BESIDE, the rest). OVER-A carries 424 of the 532 two-step links and 0.294 of the 0.395 (SU(2)); its mean influence by distance 0..5: 1.27e-3 / 8.6e-4 / 6.2e-5 / 3.2e-7 / 1.2e-8 / 3.9e-9 at SU(2), 1.54e-2 / 3.5e-3 / 1.6e-4 / 1.0e-6 / 3.3e-8 / 7.2e-9 at U(1); at one step (130 of 174): 4.6e-3 / 4.3e-3 / 6.1e-6 / 9.9e-6 at SU(2), 2.6e-2 / 8.4e-3 / 2.1e-5 / 1.3e-5 at U(1). Both groups fall by ten to a hundred per unit beyond the loop's neighbours: THE PROFILE DOES NOT PART THE GROUPS; THE ROW SUM DOES. OVER-B and BESIDE carry 0.053 and 0.047 at two steps (SU(2)).

**What this does not decide, and the next instrument.** The cancelling datum at two steps (owed, W2L1, hours); the sharper cap (the two-step W3L1 ladder, a day); three rungs (the three-step object is out of reach, §8). The next instrument is the two-step W3L1 ladder at the ordered datum; if its row sum also falls against the one-step W3L1, the parting survives one cap step, and the cancelling datum follows.

**Note (2026-11-01 08:5x EST; the trunk seat) — the U(1) column's max, and which cap moves the SU(2) number.** The influence is a max over a ≤ L and the U(1) ladder lines kept the first moment. At one step the 174 rotated records (ladder1/n6/) run through w2_u1_inf.awk show |ΔE₂| > |ΔE₁| at NO link, so 2.616 is the quantity as defined; at two steps no rotated records were kept, so 3.231 is the first-moment row sum, a LOWER BOUND on the defined quantity — and a lower bound rising above an exact value is still RISES. The SU(2) lines kept both moments. And the loop-representation cap does not enter the first moment at all: the one-step W3L1 ladder (ladder1/tv_ordered_n2_W3L1_pi.txt) reads 1.190408, the W3L2 one's digits, so the "×1.7" of the cap asymmetry above is the plaquette cap's alone, W2 → W3; the two-step W3L1 ladder in flight (three minutes a link, 532 links) is the test of it.

**§11 ADDENDUM — THE ABELIAN CONTROL'S SECOND COUPLING, OWED BY §2 AND LAUNCHED (2026-11-01 10:0x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the stake written and committed before the number is read).** A side reader's catch, true against the records: §2's standard 2 names U(1) at 1.2 AND at 0.9 the abelian control "at every instrument", and §11 read the two-step ladder at 1.2 alone — the two-step record set holds `ladder2/u1_base_ordered_b1.2.txt` with its tv record and nothing at 0.9, and §11's owed list left the 0.9 two-step out. A standard of the campaign's own, missed by its own seat; corrected here, dated. THE RUN: the two-step object (32 plaquettes, 76 links, 532 outside links) at β = 0.9, NMAX 6 (the dropped weight I₇/I₀ = 6.27 × 10⁻⁷, below 1.2's 4.17 × 10⁻⁶), LMAX 2, the ordered datum, every outside link's phase shifted by π; launched 09:59:31 by a frozen script that first remade the 1.2 two-step base byte for byte with the same exe (`w2_u1r.exe`; the control line in the run's own log) and refuses a landed record's name; four links at a time (the engine is single-threaded); every rotated record kept under `ladder2/u1_n4_b0.9/` (the 1.2 two-step kept none — it is reproducible from its base and the control); the tv record assembled afterwards in index order from the kept records, so its bytes do not depend on the scheduling. THE MATCHED CAP: §9's one-step at 0.9 was read at NMAX 8 (row sum 3.406963); the verdict standard of §11 is matched caps, so the one-step was re-run at NMAX 6 (174 links, 10:03, `ladder1/u1_*_b0.9_N6*`): row sum 3.406962, largest link 0.073898, six digits with NMAX 8, as 1.2's pair (2.615992 at NMAX 6 against 2.616016 at 8). THE THRESHOLD of this stake is therefore the matched one-step row sum 3.406962.

THE STAKE, the two outcomes weighed alike, the direction UNGUESSED (the one-step row sums do not order as a gap would order them — 3.407 at 0.9 against 2.616 at 1.2, the stronger coupling the larger influence — so no direction is implied): (a) THE TWO-STEP ROW SUM FALLS below 3.406962: the fall from one step to two is not SU(2)'s alone; the abelian object falls at 0.9 and rises at 1.2 (3.231 against 2.616), so at these two abelian points the ladder's direction follows the coupling's side of the infinite-volume β_c ≈ 1.01, and "the group's arithmetic" is struck as the sole account of SU(2)'s fall at 2.2 — §11's "the ladder parts the groups at two rungs" narrows to "parts by the coupling's side, U(1) at 0.9 falling with SU(2) at 2.2" — and a fall at 0.9 is CONSISTENT with the gap reading, no proof of it: the run asks only whether the abelian direction depends on the coupling, and the SU(2) ladder at the sharper cap in flight is still what decides whether SU(2)'s own fall survives. (b) IT RISES above 3.406962: U(1) rises at both couplings read and SU(2) falls at the one coupling read; the parting is by group at these points, SU(2)'s fall may be the non-abelian character arithmetic as much as anything a gap does, and the SU(2) ladder at a second coupling (a β on the other side of its crossover, with the same object) becomes the next owed instrument. Either way three points on the abelian column, no law, nothing staked on their continuation. WHAT THE OBJECT CANNOT CARRY: a 32-plaquette object has a coupling, not a phase — its moments are analytic in β and mark no transition — so "0.9, on the confining side of the infinite-volume β_c" is the only sense in which this control is sighted, and the page says it in those words rather than §2's "the confining phase". The side reader's framing (that 1.2 "tested only the gapless half") is carried as the reason the run was owed, not as a reading of the object.

**RESULTS OF THE §11 ADDENDUM (2026-11-01 10:1x EST, read from `date`; the run ended 10:11:06, the stake's commit b7f88fa at 10:05:52, five minutes before it).** THE TWO-STEP ROW SUM AT β = 0.9 READS 3.162885 (532 links, largest link 0.034182) AGAINST THE MATCHED ONE-STEP 3.406962: IT FALLS, ×0.928 — outcome (a). Beside it: U(1) at 1.2 rose ×1.235 (2.615992 → 3.231184); SU(2) at 2.2 fell ×0.55 (0.712 → 0.395). So at the two abelian points read the ladder's direction follows the coupling's side of the infinite-volume β_c, and the group's arithmetic is struck as the sole account of SU(2)'s fall — consistent with the gap reading and no proof of it, as staked. WHAT THE NUMBERS ADD: the two two-step row sums are 2% apart (3.163 at 0.9, 3.231 at 1.2) where the one-step pair is 30% apart (3.407, 2.616) — the direction of each step is set by the one-step value's dependence on the coupling, the two-step's being nearly flat between the two couplings; U(1)'s fall at 0.9 (×0.93) is mild beside SU(2)'s (×0.55); the distance profiles at the two couplings have one shape — per unit of distance from d1 on, the sums fall ×48, ×129, ×36, ×37 at 0.9 and ×55, ×133, ×38, ×36 at 1.2 (d0 2.429 / 2.509; d1 0.718 / 0.710; d2 0.0151 / 0.0128; d3 1.17 × 10⁻⁴ / 9.6 × 10⁻⁵; d4 3.2 × 10⁻⁶ / 2.5 × 10⁻⁶) — and the position split likewise (OVER-A 2.532 / 2.573, OVER-B 0.339 / 0.349, BESIDE 0.291 / 0.309; 0.9 / 1.2). THE RECORDS: `ladder2/u1_base_ordered_b0.9.txt`, `ladder2/u1_tv_ordered_b0.9_pi.txt`, `ladder2/u1_n4_b0.9/` (the 532 rotated records), `ladder1/u1_base_ordered_b0.9_N6.txt`, `ladder1/u1_tv_ordered_b0.9_N6_pi.txt`, `ladder1/u1_n2_b0.9_N6/` (the 174), copied under records/surface-gas. THE NEXT INSTRUMENT, named by the stake's (a) and (b) alike: THE SU(2) LADDER AT A SECOND COUPLING — the two-step object at β = 1.5 and at 3.0 beside 2.2 (W2L1, the ordered datum, 532 links at about twelve seconds each, two hours a coupling; the one-step pair at each, a minute), so that SU(2)'s direction is read on both sides of its crossover as U(1)'s now is on both sides of its β_c; launched when the cancelling two-step in flight ends. Beside it the W3L1 two-step in flight (the cap), and §8's memory wall, which the sliced sweep is moving as this is written (the engine's W2_SLICE mode, built and controlled 10:1x: unsliced output byte-identical below the timings, the sliced output digit for digit on the landed one-step records through the 81-slice class; its account and the refused W3L2 object's reading follow as §12).

## §12 — THE SLICED SWEEP: THE MEMORY WALL MOVED BY AN EXACT REARRANGEMENT, AND THE REFUSED OBJECT STAKED (2026-11-01 10:2x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the account and the stake written and committed before the refused object's record is read)

**The weld.** S1 §27 (m‴)'s fixed-leg split (cup_box.c's CUP_FIX: an open matrix-index pair of a leg clamped in an outer loop of slices, multiplying the link weight as a scalar instead of riding the state) was read onto §8's wall by two side readers and by iii-24, who verified the principle — a clamped index summed outside is an exact rearrangement of the contraction for any order — and left two questions to the trunk: whether the pair is carried through the peak, and at what price. Verified in the engine: the four junction wires wAin, wAout, wBin, wBout each belong to ONE chain step (a loop's wires are WBASE + i, consecutive steps sharing one), are born open at that step's link (wAout at face A's column 0, wAin and wBout at the shared links, wBin at face B's first slab), are never contracted, and are closed only by the final trace (the class moment, F's diagonal) or the channel projection (the whole F) — so a clamped junction wire is an exact slice of F, and all four ride the state through the peak. iii-73's structural reading, carried as he gave it: the group-theoretic weight of any configuration of glued fundamental sheets is d^χ per connected sheet, so the slicing is exact by the Haar calculus — the wires are never integrated inside a slice — and the digit-for-digit identities below are what that calculus predicts, not a numerical coincidence; and any refusal clause written in links or faces is a χ-clause in disguise, the two-step object's new contributions being the sheets whose χ changes when the second step is attached.

**The mode (w2_strip_n.c, `W2_SLICE=full|diag`; the exe w2_strip_s.exe).** Face A's part of the order (its columns, before the shared links: index NA = 2n² + n, found as the first shared link's position) is swept once per class and kept as a snapshot; each slice (q, r, q′, r′) of the junction tensor is gathered from it — a strided gather on the junction wires already open, a clamp at birth (FIXV, the value entering the link tensor as an offset) for those not yet born — then the shared links and face B's slabs are swept on the slice; F is assembled entry by entry and the class, channel and connected moments are computed by the unchanged code. Under full the B-part's multiply-adds are unchanged in total (d_a² d_c² slices, each 1/(d_a² d_c²) the state); under diag (the d_a d_c diagonal slices) the sweep is faster than the unsliced one by d_a d_c, because the unsliced sweep computes the whole junction tensor where the trace uses its diagonal — the channels are then refused by name. Unset, every byte of the output is the unsliced engine's.

**The controls (10:1x–10:2x, all MET).** (i) Unset: byte-identical below the timings to the landed one-step W3L1 record (ladder1/base_ordered_n2_W3L1.txt). (ii) full on that object: 12 line pairs of class, channel, connected and Z values, max difference 0. (iii) full on the one-step W3L2 cancelling object (ladder1/base_cancel_n2_W3L2.txt): 32 line pairs through the 81-slice (1,1) class, max difference 0; its (1,1) peak 24,300 against the unsliced 656,100. (iv) full on the TWO-STEP W2L2 ordered object (w2n4_W2L2.2026-11-01a.ordered.txt, 126 s unsliced): 32 line pairs, max difference 0, the (1,1) peak 1,037,232 against the unsliced 28,005,264 (÷27), 120 s. (v) diag: the class moments of (i), the channels refused.

**§8's wall, moved.** The slab sweep's d_c⁴ is carried at d_c² and face A's junction pair leaves the state: the W3L2 two-step object refused at 5.9 × 10⁸ entries (9.4 GB) runs at 30⁴ · 27 = 2.19 × 10⁷ entries at its peak (350 MB; the peak is during the shared links — face A's end moving up the shared edge beside face B's two ends — not during the slabs, where the state is 30⁴ · 9); the spins-to-2 object counted at 107 GB would run at 55⁴ · 27 = 2.5 × 10⁸ entries, 4 GB, on this host. §8's CORRECTED paragraph stands as the unsliced count; this section is its sequel, and §10's "Owed" line ("a sweep order that keeps the loop chain at d_c², which §8's count showed no slab sweep can") is answered not by an order but by a rearrangement.

**THE STAKE for the refused object (W3L2 — plaquette spins to 3/2, loop representations to 1 — at §10's three data, records w2n4_W3L2.2026-11-01b.<datum>.txt, in flight under full since 10:22; the one-step W3L2 pair landed: ladder1/base_ordered.txt and ladder1/base_cancel_n2_W3L2.txt, both readable by w2_modes.awk).** The one-step at W3L2 reads, ordered: (½,½) mode 4.79 × 10⁻², (1,1) mode 5.78 × 10⁻³, sup|δ₂| 0.359, r_mod 2.22 × 10⁻²; cancelling: 1.63 × 10⁻¹, 2.08 × 10⁻², 1.209, 5.22 × 10⁻²; against the W2L2 one-step's 3.63 × 10⁻², 1.19 × 10⁻³, 0.183, 4.4 × 10⁻³ and 1.25 × 10⁻¹, 1.17 × 10⁻², 0.874, 3.6 × 10⁻²: the sharper plaquette cap raises the one-step modes by ×1.3 to ×5. (S1) THE DIRECTION: at W3L2 the two-step's (½,½) mode, sup|δ₂| and r_mod lie below the one-step's at the same datum, ordered and cancelling, as at W2L2 and W3L1; the falsifier: any of the three above its one-step value at either datum, on which §10 (1)'s "contracts at every datum read" is withdrawn. (S2) THE SIZE AGAINST THE CAP: the W3L2 ratios (two-step over one-step) lie within a factor of 2 of the W2L2 ratios at the same datum (ordered ×0.23, ×0.07, ×0.27, ×0.07 for the four columns in that order; cancelling ×0.10, ×0.008, ×0.08, ×0.009); outside by more than 2 at any column, §10 (1)'s ranges are restated at the sharper cap as cap-dependent — which side, UNGUESSED. (S3) THE TWO-STEP'S CAP DEPENDENCE: the move of the two-step modes from W2L2 to W3L2 beside the one-step's ×1.3–5 — UNGUESSED; a smaller move at two steps than at one is the direction a window theorem's coarse variable would show (its law converging faster in the truncation), a larger one the contrary; no direction staked. (S4) THE PEAK, the mechanism's own check: the record's max state at (2,2) reads 21,870,000 and at (1,1) 6,480,000; a different number corrects the account of the peak above, never the number. The Haar-random datum's cells give magnitudes, as in §10. RESULTS follow the three runs, every value copied whole from its record.

**Next, and its instruments:** (a) the spins-to-2 two-step object (W4L2) under full, 4 GB at its peak, after the three W3L2 runs — the cap test one spin higher; (b) the planar two-step object under full; (c) the two-step influence ladders under diag (faster by d_a: the W3L1 ladder in flight at three minutes a link would take half), the next ladders launched on it once a one-step ladder under diag reproduces its landed record line for line; (d) the SU(2) ladder at a second coupling (§11 ADDENDUM RESULTS).


**§9 ADDENDUM — THE REDUCTION, STATED AS AN INEQUALITY (2026-11-01 10:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk note written while the ladders return, so that "what W4 still needs" is an object and not a sentence).** Let the coarse variables of the blocked measure at the scale a_c be the loop variables A, B, …, and for two coarse outsides ω, ω′ that differ at the block B alone let b_{AB} = sup ‖μ_A(·|ω) − μ_A(·|ω′)‖_TV; THE GROUND's Hypothesis I is Σ_B w(d(A, B)) b_{AB} < 1 with its exponential weight. For a fine configuration of every link but ℓ, let β_{Aℓ} = sup_{U, U′} ‖μ_A(·| ℓ = U) − μ_A(·| ℓ = U′)‖_TV be the fine influence of the link ℓ on the coarse variable A at that configuration. THEN b_{AB} ≤ Σ_{ℓ ∈ B} sup_{configurations} β_{Aℓ}. Proof: the law of A given ω is the fine conditional law of A averaged over the fine fields consistent with ω; couple the fine fields under ω and ω′ so that they agree off B; the total-variation difference of the two averages is at most the coupling's expectation of the difference between the fine conditional laws at two fine outsides that differ on B's links alone, and that difference is at most the telescoping sum over B's links of the single-link influences, each at some configuration, hence at most the sum of their suprema. ∎ W = 0 (the block form of Dobrushin's condition by telescoping; textbook). WHAT IT SAYS ABOUT THE LADDER: the moment influence of §9–§11 is a LOWER bound on β_{Aℓ} at the datum (one configuration, one rotation, a truncated law), and Hypothesis I needs the SUPREMUM over the fine configuration of every other link — a supremum over SU(2)^{532} for the two-step object — summed over ℓ with the weight. The exact objects do not take that supremum; it is bounded in strong coupling by the gas's own expansion (the influence of a link falls exponentially with its distance from the loop, which is the lattice gap at strong coupling restated) and in weak coupling by the small-field expansion with its large-field bound; at β = 2.2 neither converges, and the crossover theorem is exactly a supremum bound on the single-link influence of the coarse loop at a_c, uniform in the fine configuration, at such a coupling. The datum-dependence read in §7 (the first-scale content differs by a factor 3–8 between the ordered and the cancelling outsides, the second-scale content by 1.5) is an indication of the distance between a typical reading and that supremum, not its measure. THE INSTRUMENT THIS NAMES, in two steps: first, at a fixed datum, the OSCILLATION of the coarse loop's moments over ONE link's value — the sup over U, U′ of the link at the others fixed, a polynomial of bounded degree on SU(2) whose range is computable — which the π rotation of the ladder samples at one point; second, over the datum, which is the theorem. The first is W4's next instrument and is cheap (one link's oscillation is a sweep set per quadrature point of the link's value); the second is the theorem, and the charter's W4 row names its mechanism — the frustrated data weighed by their measure through the chessboard estimate (THE FAR SHORE §5), the typical-data criterion on gauge-invariant coarse variables — not an instrument.

The profile by distance (sums over the links at each distance): 0.386, 0.188, 0.0057, 6.7 × 10⁻⁵, 1.9 × 10⁻⁶, 7.2 × 10⁻⁸ at d = 0..5 — ×33, ×86, ×35, ×27 per unit of distance from d1, the shape of the ordered datum's (§11) and of U(1)'s at both couplings (§11 ADDENDUM); by position OVER-A 0.403, OVER-B 0.140, BESIDE 0.037 against the one-step's 1.066, 0.486, 0.078. **§9 ADDENDUM RESULTS — THE OSCILLATION READ AT THE THREE STRONGEST LINKS, AND THE CHAIN OF INEQUALITIES THAT PLACES THE LADDER (2026-11-01 12:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; two passes of the instrument W2_SET, every value from its records).** THE CHAIN, in THE GROUND's own symbols (Hypothesis I verbatim: "there are numbers κ > 0 and α < 1 and a rung j₀ such that at every rung j ≥ j₀, on every lattice of the construction, Σ_{y′} C^λ_j(y, y′) e^{κ|y−y′|} ≤ α for every coarse point y", C^λ_j(y, y′) the interdependence coefficient of the blocked measure, and §8's reading C^λ_j(y, y′) ≤ 1 − e^{−Δ_{yy′}(A)} with Δ the mixed second difference of the effective action): at a fixed fine configuration of every link but ℓ, (i) the ladder's moment influence |ΔE[χ_a(A)]|/d_a at the angle π is at most the total-variation distance of the loop's two laws at that angle; (ii) that distance is at most the link's OSCILLATION, the sup over the link's two values; (iii) the oscillation at one configuration is at most β_{Aℓ}, its sup over configurations; (iv) by the ADDENDUM's inequality Σ_{ℓ∈B} sup β_{Aℓ} ≥ b_{AB} = C^λ(A, B). The ladder reads (i); this instrument reads a lower bound on (ii) — the range of E[χ_½(A)] over a grid of the link's values at the ordered datum. THE READINGS (pass 1: 391 unit quaternions, fifteen half-angles by twenty-six axes; the π rotation about x is the ladder's point): link 56 (the one-step object's strongest, the x-link (−1, 2, 0, 0)): base E₁ 0.621039, the π point moves it by 0.0329, the grid's largest deviation 0.0590 and its oscillation (max − min) 0.0703; link 60 (the y-link (0, 1, 0, −1)): 0.0282, 0.0535, 0.0645; link 78 (the two-step object's strongest, the y-link (0, 2, 0, −1)): base 0.145567, 0.0091, 0.0173, 0.0209. So THE LADDER'S ONE-ANGLE READING IS 44–47 % OF THE ONE-LINK OSCILLATION AT EVERY LINK READ, at both scales; the grid's extremal values sit at the same two quaternions for all three links (a rotation of 337.5° about an axis in the xz-plane at the maximum, of 247.5° about another at the minimum), and the two scales' strongest links oscillate in the ratio 0.0209/0.0703 = 0.30, as their π readings do (0.28). THE SECOND PASS FOUND THE STRUCTURE (osc2_links.sh: the half-turn about 203 axes, the coordinate axes exact, and the angle scan about x): AT THE ORDERED DATUM THE MOMENT DEPENDS ON THE VARIED LINK THROUGH ITS CLASS ALONE — at the half-turn, all 203 axes read one value to twelve digits at every link (0.588140843 at link 56, 0.592802956 at 60, 0.136422132 at 78) — because every other outside link is the identity, the plaquette weights and the loop's character are class functions and the Haar measure is conjugation-invariant, so a global conjugation carries the object with link ℓ = U into the object with ℓ = gUg⁻¹: the single-link oscillation at this datum is a function of one variable, w = cos(angle/2) on [−1, 1], and the π rotation is its value at w = 0. THE EXACT OSCILLATION (wscan_links.sh: 181 rotation angles 0..360° about x, the extremes bracketed to two degrees; records wscan_*): link 56 — base 0.621039, maximum 0.635430 at 360° (the CENTRE FLIP, U = −1, which raises the loop's expectation above the aligned datum's), minimum 0.560878 at 240°, oscillation 0.07455, the π point 0.441 of it; link 60 — 0.635055 at 360°, 0.566766 at 240°, oscillation 0.06829, the π point 0.413 of it; link 78 (two steps) — base 0.145567, 0.150147 at 360°, 0.128034 at 240°, oscillation 0.02211, the π point 0.414 of it. So the ladder's one-angle reading is 41–44 % of the exact one-link oscillation at the three strongest links, the extremes lie on the centre-flipped side of SU(2) at both scales, and the two scales' oscillations stand in the ratio 0.0221/0.0746 = 0.30 as their π readings do (0.28). WHAT THIS PLACES: a row sum read at the oscillation instead of the π point would be about twice the ladder's at the strongest links (0.712 → of order 1.5 at one step, 0.395 → of order 0.9 at two, if the ratio held across the row, which is not read); it is still (iii)'s lower bound at ONE configuration, with the sup over SU(2)^{532} untaken, and §9 ADDENDUM's conclusion stands unmoved: the direction by scale (×0.55, ×0.36 at the two data) is what the objects read, and the theorem is the sup. THE RECORDS: osc_n2_W2L1_link56.txt, osc_n2_W2L1_link60.txt, osc_n4_W2L1_link78.txt, osc2_*.txt, the two frozen scripts, under records/surface-gas/oscillation.

## §13 — THE LADDER MODE: A LADDER IN MINUTES, EXACTLY, AND SU(2)'s DIRECTION ON BOTH SIDES OF ITS CROSSOVER (2026-11-01 12:2x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; on the operator's order that the runs' shape, not their core count, was wrong)

**The shape that was wrong.** A ladder read one outside link at a time re-swept every one of the object's 76 steps for each of its 532 links, though a flipped link changes the tensors of the few object links whose staples hold it — at two steps 4.2 steps on average, 22 at most. Two hours a ladder at the crude cap, a day at the sharper one, and twelve hours on the host once this seat's own runs had saturated it (trap 47).

**The instrument (w2_strip_l.c, a fork of the engine; W2_LADDER=1; the records lad_*).** Per class (a, 0), a ≤ LMAX: (i) ONE FORWARD SWEEP with every intermediate state stored (the state after each step; 162 MB in all at W2L1 two steps); (ii) the final trace carried BACKWARD through the order as a functional on the open wires, by process() itself run from the far end — the wires the forward sweep opened it contracts and the ones it contracted it opens, a closed plaquette cycle's spin sum falling at the cycle's first step instead of its last, so that at any cut the forward state and the backward functional hold the same open wires and the same open cycles and their contraction, with each open cycle's weight applied once there, is the full value — THE CUT IDENTITY, checked at all 77 cuts of every run and printed; (iii) for each outside link, a MINI-SWEEP: the stored state before its first affected step carried through its affected steps with that link's tensors rebuilt at the flipped datum (an override beside the base tensors, freed after), contracted against the backward functional at the cut after its last affected step — the flipped value exactly, N_ℓ and Z_ℓ from the (1,0) and (0,0) classes, ΔE = N_ℓ/Z_ℓ − N/Z. Every quantity is the link-by-link ladder's; nothing is approximated; the mini-sweep's steps total 2,236 against 40,432 for the 532 sweeps.

**The controls (11:5x–12:1x).** Unset, the fork's output is byte-identical below the timings to the landed one-step base (ladder1/base_ordered_n2_W3L1.txt). The one-step W2L1 ordered ladder: 174 of 174 links to the landed record's seven digits, in 8 s where the link-by-link ladder took a minute. The two-step W2L1 ordered ladder: 532 of 532, the row sum 0.3948683 identical to seven digits and the largest link identical, in 537 s where the link-by-link run took two hours; the cancelling ladder 532 of 532, 0.5800709 against the landed 0.5800708 (the two records' seventh digit), 268 s. The per-link differences between the two roads are at most 5 × 10⁻¹⁰ absolute, at links whose |ΔE| is 10⁻¹⁰ — the two contraction orders' roundoff on the engine's unnormalised magnitudes (10¹¹³ at two steps; trap 44: a running normalisation is the cure, not yet built) — so the profile's d = 4 sums carry four digits and its d = 5 sums one, on both roads alike. At β = 1.5 the one-step 0.8573339 and the two-step 0.2997453 are the link-by-link run's to every printed digit (17 s and 528 s against a minute and forty).

**SU(2)'s DIRECTION ON BOTH SIDES OF ITS CROSSOVER (the ordered datum, W2L1, matched caps; the one-step rungs link by link, the two-step at 1.5 both ways, at 3.0 the ladder mode, 149 s):**

| β | one step (174) | two steps (532) | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.5 | 0.857 | 0.300 | ×0.35 |
| 2.2 | 0.712 | 0.395 | ×0.55 |
| 3.0 | 1.230 | 0.950 | ×0.77 |

The contraction from one step to two is monotone in the coupling — strongest on the strong-coupling side, weakest on the weak-coupling side — and present at all three; with §11 ADDENDUM 3's withdrawal of the ladder as a decider this is a reading of the finite object's coupling dependence, consistent with the gap reading and no proof of it. The W3L1 two-step ladder at 2.2 (the cap, §11's note) is in flight under the ladder mode as this is written; its reading follows as RESULTS.

**What the mode makes possible, at their counted costs:** every ladder of §9–§11 at any coupling and datum in minutes (the U(1) engine keeps its own road, seven seconds a link); the ladder at the sharper caps (W3L1 in the hour, W3L2 under the slice mode's diag with the junction wires clamped); the OSCILLATION ladder — every link's exact one-variable oscillation at the ordered datum (181 angles) at the cost of 181 ladders of the old shape, now 181 mini-sweeps per link: one hour at W2L1 two steps — the row sum of the exact single-link oscillations, which §9 ADDENDUM names as the next quantity under the sup. Not possible by it: the supremum over the fine configuration of the other links (the theorem).

## §14 — W4'S FIRST INSTRUMENT: HYPOTHESIS I's OWN QUANTITY READ ON THE OBJECT — THE INTERDEPENDENCE COEFFICIENT (8.5) BETWEEN THE TWO COARSE LOOPS AT A FINE DATUM, STAKED BEFORE ANY NUMBER (2026-11-01 17:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; every direction UNGUESSED where it is unguessed, each side's meaning written here first)

**A correction first.** The trunk seat's report of 16:5x named as the next instrument "W3's pair-coupled sweep on the two-face engine" — the one-step map (β, δ₂) ↦ (β′, δ₂′) at a fixed fine datum with a two-body coupling added to the fine action. That is the proxy §8 withdrew in its first paragraph ("a map iterated at a fixed datum" runs up or down by the datum's choice; the recursion's honest reading is the two-step object, which §10 and §12 have read; past two steps the recursion is W4's or nothing). The report's instrument is struck, dated; the next instrument is this section's.

**The quantity, in THE GROUND's own words.** §8.3's (8.5): C^λ_j(y, y′) = sup ‖λ_j(· | v) − λ_j(· | v′)‖_TV, the supremum over pairs v, v′ of coarse configurations that differ only at y′, of the total variation distance of the two conditional laws of v_y; Hypothesis I: Σ_{y′} C^λ_j(y, y′) e^{κ|y−y′|} ≤ α < 1 for every coarse point y; and its reading on the effective action, C ≤ 1 − e^{−Δ} with Δ_{yy′}(A) the mixed second difference (Proposition 8.4's first inequality). Every instrument of §9–§13 read a cousin of this: the ladders the influence of a FINE link on the coarse loop, §10 and §12 the pair coupling's modes and sup norm. None read (8.5) itself, whose variables are both COARSE. The two-face object reads it directly: at a fine datum ω the object's joint law p_ω(U, V) of the two coarse loops is in hand (the engine's junction tensor), and (8.5) for y = A, y′ = B with every other variable fixed by the datum is

C_ω(A, B) = sup_{V, V′ ∈ SU(2)} ‖p_ω(· | B = V) − p_ω(· | B = V′)‖_TV,  ‖·‖_TV = sup_E |·| = ½ ‖·‖₁,

beside the tilt bound's own quantity Δ_ω(A, B) = sup_{V, V′} osc_U [log p_ω(U | V) − log p_ω(U | V′)] and the bound 1 − e^{−Δ_ω} ≥ C_ω it implies. The readable part of the row sum: the twenty coarse plaquettes sharing a coarse link with A (sixteen bent, four coplanar; the coplanar pair's law equals the bent's at the two named data by §12 (b)'s lemma and parts from it at the random datum, where the planar W2L2 record is read for it), S₂₀(ω) = Σ_B C_ω(A, B), at κ → 0, the weakest form — any κ > 0 weighs each adjacent term by more than one. Pairs sharing a vertex or farther are unread and positive: S₂₀(ω) is a LOWER bound on the datum's row sum at every κ.

**What it is, and what it is not, said before any number.** (i) It is THE INTEGRAND AT ONE FINE DATUM: (8.5) integrates the fine fields outside the blocks and takes the supremum over coarse outsides; the object fixes the fine outside and varies two coarse variables. No number below is (8.5)'s row sum, and every number is written "at the datum ω". (ii) Its variables are the two coarse LOOP holonomies with a common basepoint, the W4 row's choice ("gauge-invariant coarse variables"); (8.5) is stated on the coarse LINK holonomies of the block map in a fixed gauge ("one SU(2) element per coarse link"). The relation between the two Dobrushin conditions is the theorem's to supply; it is an open link, named here and not papered. (iii) The law is the TRUNCATED one — loop representations to spin 1 at L2 (a, c ≤ 2), to spin ½ at L1 — so C_ω is a total variation between truncated laws, which is a total variation only where the truncated joint density and the conditioning marginal p_B(V) are positive on the grid: THE POSITIVITY CENSUS IS PRINTED FIRST for every (object, datum, cap) and the TV is REFUSED BY NAME where it fails (§9's CORRECTED 07:4x, the ladder reader's rule, applied here before a number exists; the one-step SU(2) object at the ordered datum is the likely refusal — E[χ_½(A)] = 0.62 there). (iv) The suprema are taken on GRIDS with local refinement, and a grid's sup is at most the group's: C_ω and Δ_ω are read as LOWER bounds, so "S₂₀ exceeds one" survives the grid and "S₂₀ is below one" does not — the refinement's movement is printed beside each value.

**The instrument.** The engine (w2_strip_n.c) gains `W2_DUMPF=1`: for every class pair (a, c) the junction tensor's entries F^{ac}(r, q, r′, q′) = E[D^a(A)_{rq} D^c(B)_{r′q′}] (unnormalised, with Z, the four strides and GENINV on the line), under the unsliced and the full slice modes alike; unset, the output is byte-identical. The reader (w2_dob.c, a new file; the engine's own Dsub/rep/quadrature copied verbatim so that the convention is the engine's and not a cousin of it) reconstructs p(U, V) = (1/Z) Σ_{a,c ≤ L} d_a d_c Σ F^{ac}(r, q, r′, q′) conj D^a(U)_{rq} conj D^c(V)_{r′q′} (Peter–Weyl) on the engine's quadrature family, the marginals, the conditionals, the positivity census, C_ω and Δ_ω over a (V, V′) grid refined locally about the maximiser, and prints S₂₀ with the planar column where it differs. For U(1) the joint moments E[e^{iaΦ_A} e^{icΦ_B}] of the ladder bases are the Fourier coefficients of the joint density on the torus; the same quantities follow on a grid of the two phases (w2_u1_dob.c).

**The controls, all MET before any TV is read, or the reader is not trusted.** (K1) THE FREE DATUM: p = w_A ⊗ w_B (W(A) = Σ_a d_a a_a^{n²} χ_a), C = 0 and Δ = 0 to roundoff at every pair. (K2) THE CLASS MOMENTS recomputed from the dump (the trace over F's two index pairs) equal the record's nine E[χ_a(A)χ_c(B)] to the printed digit. (K3) THE CHANNELS f_k recomputed from the dump with the reader's own projector equal the record's. (K4) THE SCHUR IDENTITY AT THE ORDERED DATUM: the global conjugation by g fixes the ordered datum and carries (A, B) to (gAg⁻¹, gBg⁻¹), the plaquette weights and the Haar measure being invariant, so F^{ac} is an intertwiner of a ⊗ c and F^{ac}/Z = Σ_k (f_k/d_k) P_k there; the dump must meet it to roundoff — a KAT at the ordered datum, and at the cancelling and random data a READING of the invariance's breaking (the cancelling datum's staple values ±1, ±i leave a U(1) of global conjugations; the random datum none), printed, not pinned. K4 met, the W3L2 two-step ordered datum's F is recoverable from the landed record b (its channels) with no re-run; the cancelling and random data need dump runs under the full slice mode (about an hour a datum, §12). (K5) THE QUADRATURE CHECK: the reader at rule degree 30 and at 60 must agree on C_ω to three digits; where they do not, the finer is the reading and the disagreement is printed.

**The runs, the abelian column first (standard 2).** U(1) at β = 1.2 and 0.9, one step and two, the ordered and the cancelling data: the joint moments in hand at NMAX 6, LMAX 2 (the ladder bases), and re-run at LMAX 4 and 6 so that the charge-cap convergence of C is read before any value is quoted. Then SU(2) at β = 2.2, bent: one step and two at W2L2 at the three data (minutes); W3L2 one step at the three data with the dump (minutes); W3L2 two steps from record b at the ordered datum (K4) and from dump runs at the cancelling and random data (hours, in the background).

**THE STAKE.** (D1) S₂₀(ω) at SU(2), β = 2.2, two steps, W3L2, at the ordered and the cancelling data — above or below one: UNGUESSED. Above one: Hypothesis I's adjacent sum is not met at the object's reach at that integrand, and the number of further scales at which it would be, were the measured per-scale contraction of §10 and §12 (×0.17–0.34 at the leading mode) to continue, is arithmetic written beside the number and no claim; below one: the adjacent sum is met at that integrand, nothing follows for (8.5) (the supremum over coarse outsides and the integral over the fine fields untaken), and the grid cannot certify "below". (D2) THE SCALE DIRECTION: C_ω at two steps lies below C_ω at one step at every datum and cap where both are readable — the pair coupling's contraction of §10 and §12 read in (8.5)'s currency; a reversal is a finding against §10 (1) in that currency and is printed as one. (D3) THE TILT BOUND's LOOSENESS, C_ω/(1 − e^{−Δ_ω}): UNGUESSED; a ratio near one says THE GROUND's reading "C ≤ 1 − e^{−Δ}" is tight on this object, a small ratio that the mixed-difference route to Hypothesis I overprices the coefficient by that factor. (D4) U(1) at β = 1.2 (the Coulomb phase, no gap), S₂₀ at two steps: UNGUESSED. For the true blocked measure (8.5)'s row sum cannot lie below one at any scale in a gapless phase (Dobrushin's condition implies exponential decay); the one-datum truncated reading can land either side. Above one at both scales: consistent, and no parting from SU(2) unless SU(2)'s own S₂₀ lies below. Below one: a one-datum reading passes where the theory has no gap, which places this instrument exactly where §11 ADDENDUM 3 placed the ladder — an integrand, never a decider of F1 — and the page says so now, in advance, so that no later reading can be read as a verdict on the gap. (D5) THE CHARGE-CAP CONVERGENCE at U(1): the move of C from LMAX 4 to LMAX 6 below five per cent of C at every cell, else the LMAX 2 column is struck and only the converged cells are quoted. (D6) At the two named data the planar column equals the bent column to the digit (the §12 (b) lemma in (8.5)'s currency); at the random datum it parts.

**What this instrument cannot do, said once.** Integrate the datum (the theorem); take the supremum over coarse outsides (the theorem); read pairs beyond the adjacent ones (a three-loop object, out of reach by §8's count); relate the loop-variable condition to (8.5)'s link-variable one (the theorem). It reads Hypothesis I's integrand in Hypothesis I's own currency on the only exact objects there are, at one and two scales, and it tells the window theorem how far its hypothesis stands from the readings at the reach the objects have. RESULTS follow the runs, every value copied whole from its record.
