## §15 — THE WINDOW THEOREM'S SHAPE AFTER §14: THE BLOCKED COEFFICIENT AS A FIBRE AVERAGE OF TWO QUANTITIES THE OBJECTS READ, AND WHERE THE OBJECTS' REACH ENDS AGAINST THE HYPOTHESIS (2026-11-01 18:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk act under the W4 row, written while the W3L2 random cell and the gate's run e are in flight; no number of §14 edited)

**The decomposition (a lemma, W = 0: the coupling inequality for mixtures and the triangle inequality).** Let v be a coarse configuration and v′ the same with the coarse loop B's value changed from V to V′; let ω be the fine outside of the two-face object (every link not in its two faces) and ν(· | v) its conditional law given v under the Wilson measure. The conditional law of A given v is the mixture λ(dU | v) = ∫ p_ω(dU | B = V) ν(dω | v), because given ω and B's value the law of A is the object's own. Couple ν(· | v) and ν(· | v′) optimally; on the coupled mass the two components share ω and differ by C_ω(A, B) at most, on the rest by at most one. Hence

C^λ(A, B)(v) ≤ ∫ C_ω(A, B) ν(dω | v) + ‖ν(· | v) − ν(· | v′)‖_TV,

and the second term, the fine outside's sensitivity to B's value, splits again by coupling the two outside laws to agree away from B's neighbourhood: at most the fibre average of the influence of B's neighbourhood links on A's law — Σ_{ℓ near B} β_{Aℓ}(ω), the §9 ADDENDUM's single-link influences, whose one-angle lower bound the ladders read in the OVER-B position class — plus the far outside's sensitivity, which is Hypothesis II's clustering at a distance. So, at every coarse configuration,

C^λ(A, B)(v) ≤ ∫ [ C_ω(A, B) + Σ_{ℓ ∈ N(B)} β_{Aℓ}(ω) ] ν(dω | v) + (the far term, II),

THE INTEGRAND BEING EXACTLY THE TWO QUANTITIES THE OBJECTS READ POINTWISE — §14's C_ω and §11's ladder restricted to the OVER-B links — at one point ω of the fibre each. Nothing in this inequality is new; it places the campaign's readings inside THE GROUND's Hypothesis I without a proxy between them, and it says what a proof of the window theorem must do with them.

**What the readings say against the hypothesis at the objects' reach.** At β = 2.2, two steps, the ordered datum (the point of the fibre nearest the typical outside at this coupling, §8): C_ω = 0.056 at W3L2 (§14), the OVER-B ladder 0.105 at W3L1 (§13 RESULTS; a one-angle lower bound) — the integrand's adjacent value at that point is at least 0.16, and twenty adjacent coarse plaquettes make 3.2 against Hypothesis I's one, before any κ-weight and before the pairs beyond the adjacent ones; at the cancelling datum C_ω alone reads 0.066 (§14 Part 2 in flight for its OVER-B column); at the Haar-random datum both quantities are two orders smaller, but that datum's coarse loops are far from any typical coarse configuration at 2.2 and it is not a point of the fibre over such a v. So, as a reading and not a theorem: THE INTEGRAND OF HYPOTHESIS I's ROW SUM, AT THE POINTS OF THE FIBRE THE OBJECTS CAN REACH, EXCEEDS ONE AT THE SECOND SCALE FROM β = 2.2 — the window theorem's hypothesis is not met at a_c = 4a from 2.2 at those points, and the only way the fibre average lies below one there is a fibre dominated by outsides like the random one, which the typical outside at 2.2 is not. The measured per-scale contraction (C_ω ×0.12–0.22, the ladder ×0.36–0.75) puts the adjacent sum's crossing of one at the third or fourth scale IF the contraction continued — arithmetic, no claim, and the U(1) column of §14 is the standing reason it is not a claim (U(1)'s C_ω contracts ×0.27–0.45 per scale too, in a phase that never meets the hypothesis).

**Where the exact reach ends, and what the theorem therefore owes.** The three-step object is out of reach (§8's count), so no object reads the third scale's integrand; the fibre average at any scale is an integral over the whole lattice that no finite object takes (§9); the supremum over coarse configurations is demanded by the hypothesis as stated. The window theorem is therefore, in its exact shape: (W4-1) THE DECAY LEMMA — a bound on C_ω(A, B) + Σ_{N(B)} β_{Aℓ}(ω) at scale 2^k a, UNIFORM IN THE FINE OUTSIDE ω, decaying in k — the sup over ω of the two quantities the objects read; (W4-2) THE FAR TERM — Hypothesis II's clustering below a_c, the UV half's business (W5); (W4-3) THE LOOP–LINK LEMMA — (8.5)'s link-holonomy coefficient controlled by the loop one after a maximal-tree gauge fixing (iii-73's reading, §14), the direction the objects do not give for free. W4-1 is the research object: a sup over SU(2)^{532} at the second scale and over the whole lattice at every later one, where the strong-coupling gas bounds it for β below the certified descent and the small-field expansion for β large, and the crossover is exactly the coupling at which neither converges. THE INSTRUMENT THAT READS W4-1's INTEGRAND AT ITS WORST POINT is the one §11 ADDENDUM 3 (4) named — the oscillation at the worst configuration of the others — and §14 gives it its currency: C_ω maximised over ω by an ascent over the outside links (each evaluation one sweep of the object; a found configuration a LOWER bound on the sup, never the sup; a lower bound above one at a scale is a proof that W4-1 fails at that scale in the sup form, which is a theorem about the theorem's shape and would force the measure-weighted form — the chessboard estimate as the tool to bound the fibre's mass on such configurations). That ascent is the next instrument of the crossover leg; its stake is written when the gate's run e and §14 Part 2 have landed.

**What is not claimed.** The window theorem; any value of the fibre average; any statement about the third scale; a verdict on the gap from any number above. This section claims the inequality (textbook), the placement of the readings in it, and the reading that the hypothesis's integrand exceeds one at the objects' reach at β = 2.2.


**§15 CORRECTED — THE INEQUALITY BOUNDS THE ROUTE, NOT THE HYPOTHESIS: ITS DIRECTION, READ WRONG ABOVE AND SET RIGHT (2026-11-01 18:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a reviewer's reading of §15 within the hour, verified and carried; nothing above edited).** The decomposition of §15 is an UPPER bound on C^λ(A, B)(v): the total variation of two mixtures is at most the fibre average of their components' total variations (plus the far term), with strict slack whenever different fine outsides push A's law in different directions under B's change — the mixture can cancel what no component does. So a large integrand at the fibre points the objects reach says that THIS ROUTE — the pointwise bound — cannot certify Hypothesis I at that scale; it does not say the hypothesis fails. The sentence "the window theorem's hypothesis is not met at a_c = 4a from 2.2 at those points" is withdrawn, dated; the reading it should have been is: THE ROUTE'S BOUND ON THE ROW SUM EXCEEDS ONE AT EVERY FIBRE POINT THE OBJECTS REACH AT THE SECOND SCALE FROM 2.2, so a proof of Hypothesis I at 4a from 2.2, if there is one, must go through the mixture's cancellation and not through any pointwise bound — and W4-1 as §15 framed it (a supremum over ω of C_ω + Σβ, decaying in the scale) is a STRICTLY STRONGER demand than Hypothesis I, which the readings already place at or above 0.16 per adjacent pair at the second scale. The worst-configuration ascent keeps its design and changes its purpose: it reads how far the pointwise supremum lies above 1/20 — the size of the cancellation the proof must find — as a lower bound on that supremum, and that is what its stake will say. WHAT WOULD READ THE HYPOTHESIS FROM BELOW: a linear functional averages through the mixture without slack, so for any observable f of A with |f| ≤ 1 the blocked law's moment influence |λ(f | v) − λ(f | v′)| is the fibre average of the pointwise moment influences E_ω[f(A) | V] − E_ω[f(A) | V′] (plus the far term) and C^λ ≥ ½ of it; the pointwise moment influence is readable from the same junction tensor at every datum, and a sign consistent across the fibre would make the fibre average a sum without cancellation — the (½,½) mode of the pair coupling is positive at every datum read (§10, §12), which is the reading that suggests it, and the fibre average itself remains unreadable by any exact object. Three smaller corrections to §15's arithmetic: (i) the 0.16 added C_ω at W3L2 to a ladder at W3L1 — the house rule is matched caps (§11), and §13 read the plaquette cap moving the ladder ×1.7; the matched sum at W2 (C_ω 0.026 at W2L2, the OVER-B ladder 0.053 at W2L1) is 0.08 per pair, 1.6 over twenty; at W3 the C_ω column is 0.056 and the ladder's OVER-B at W3L1 0.105, quoted as two caps, not one number; (ii) the OVER-B position class is a PROXY for N(B), the outside links whose weights move when B's value moves, which are B's own staple links and their neighbours, not every link beside face B — named as a proxy until the ladder is read on N(B) itself; (iii) the W = 0 label covers the first inequality (mixtures and the triangle inequality) alone; the far term's coupling is a sketch until Hypothesis II is stated as the quantity it bounds. §15's claims stand as corrected: the inequality, the placement of the readings in it as the route's integrand, and the reading that the route's bound exceeds one at the objects' reach at β = 2.2.


## §16 — THE WORST-CONFIGURATION ASCENT: HOW FAR THE POINTWISE SUPREMUM OF (8.5) LIES ABOVE THE READINGS, STAKED BEFORE ANY NUMBER (2026-11-01 19:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the purpose as §15 CORRECTED fixed it; every direction UNGUESSED where it is unguessed)

**What it reads, and what it cannot.** §15's route bounds the blocked coefficient by a fibre average of C_ω; the sup form W4-1 demands sup_ω C_ω. This instrument climbs C_ω(A, B) over the fine outside ω of the ONE-STEP object (174 outside links, each an SU(2) element) from the ordered datum, and reads the largest value it finds. A found value is a LOWER bound on the supremum — a climb finds a configuration, never the sup — so the reading is "the pointwise supremum lies at least here", which is the size of the cancellation a proof at this scale through the mixture must find (§15 CORRECTED), and a value near one would say the sup form is dead at this scale outright. It cannot read the fibre average (no exact object does), the supremum itself, or the second scale (the two-step object's outside is 532 links and a sweep there is a minute; it is the next rung if the first says something).

**The instrument.** The engine takes the whole outside from a file (`W2_DATUM`, the campaign's engine w2_strip_n.c; three controls MET before this stake: unset byte-identical below the timings to the landed one-step record; the ordered datum written as a file reproduces the ordered dump's 196 junction-tensor entries to the digit; a file with link 56 flipped to −1 reproduces the `W2_SET` road's entries and the §9 ADDENDUM's 0.635430 for E[χ_½(A)] at that flip). The climb (`w2_ascent.sh`): coordinate ascent over the outside links in a shuffled order, each link tried at the eight unit quaternions ±1, ±i, ±j, ±k (the centre flip and the three quarter-turns, the moves the oscillation instrument found extremal at the ordered datum: §9 ADDENDUM RESULTS, the extremes at the centre-flipped side), a move accepted when the reader's C rises; the reader in its fast mode (the degree-20 rule, the degree-8 grid, six refinement rounds; its fast reading of the ordered base 0.16649 against the full reader's 0.16624, the difference the grid's) for the climb and the full reader (30/60/12/24) on every accepted datum at the end of each pass; the link set restricted to the links the W2L1 ladder read above 10⁻⁴ (the influence's support; a restriction only weakens a lower bound); two passes, or until a pass accepts no move; every accepted move logged with its link, its value and C. At the end the found datum is re-read at plaquette spins to 3/2 (W3L2), the cap check, and its positivity census printed — an adversarial outside may push the truncated law signed, in which case the reader refuses by name and the climb's last POSITIVE datum is the reading.

**THE STAKE.** (A1) THE FOUND SUPREMUM against the ordered reading 0.1662 (W2L2) — the ratio UNGUESSED; written beside it in advance: below 2, the typical points of the fibre are within a factor two of the worst the climb finds and the pointwise route's shortfall (1.6 over twenty at matched caps, §15 CORRECTED) is its shortfall; between 2 and 6, the cancellation a proof must find at this scale is the size of the ratio; above 6, so that 20 C exceeds twenty, the sup form is dead at the first scale by an order of magnitude and only a measure-weighted theorem survives. (A2) THE CLIMB'S PATH: the first accepted moves lie on the links the ladder read strongest (the staple links of the shared edge and face A's near column), UNGUESSED beyond that. (A3) THE CAP: the found datum's C at W3L2 lies within a factor 1.6 of its W2L2 value (the cap's move on the named data in §14 was ×1.4–1.5), else the cap is the first thing said about the number. (A4) THE CANCELLING DATUM'S PLACE: the found supremum exceeds the cancelling datum's 0.2492; a climb from the cancelling start (the second run) reaches within 10 % of the climb from the ordered start, or the two are two local maxima and both are printed. (A5) THE ABELIAN COLUMN IS NOT CLIMBED HERE (standard 2 names the control at every instrument; the U(1) engine has no datum-file door yet; the U(1) climb follows when the SU(2) one has said something worth controlling, and until then no SU(2) number from this instrument is set beside U(1)'s). RESULTS follow the runs, every value copied whole from its record.

## §18 — THE BENT OBJECT WAS NEVER COMPUTED: EVERY "BENT" READING AT A NAMED DATUM IS THE PLANAR GEOMETRY (2026-11-02 05:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; found by the proof agent M2 (§6.0 of its work page), confirmed line by line by the second reader M2R; nothing above edited)

1. **The defect.** Both W2 engines, `w2_strip.c` (the 2×2 faces; records `w2_strip.2026-11-01a.*`) and `w2_strip_n.c` (the n×n faces; records `w2n{2,4}_*`), have the same staple loop. It skips only the object's face plaquettes (`is_obj_plaq` over the 2n² face plaquettes). `okey` registers every link it is handed as an outside link and never consults the object-link table. The bent object has n + 1 corner plaquettes: the xz-plaquettes at base (0, j, 0), j = 0..n, each holding face A's x-link (0, j, 0)→(1, j, 0) and face B's z-link (0, j, 0)→(0, j, 1). Each corner plaquette is therefore entered twice as a staple, once for each of its two object links, and each time the OTHER object link is read from the datum. That makes 6 such staples at n = 2 and 10 at n = 4, and none for the planar object (M2R, by a copy of the build logic). The direct A–B coupling through the corner plaquettes was never integrated.

2. **What it does to the record.** At the ordered and the cancelling data, a corner staple is indistinguishable from an identity-valued outside staple, so there every "bent" number IS the planar object's number. §14 RESULTS Part 2's (D6), "the planar two-step equals the bent to the digit at the ordered (0.026260892) and the cancelling (0.029883394) data", is the defect's signature, not a property of the geometry. At the Haar-random data the bent cells are a third geometry, neither the bent object's nor the planar one's. Hence:
   - W2's first-light table (the 2×2 faces, 11-01a, "THE DATUM IS THE EFFECT", 0.81 to ±0.01–0.04) and the n = 2, 4 runs are readings of the planar geometry at the ordered and cancelling data and of the third geometry at the random data. None is a reading of the bent pair.
   - (D1), S₂₀ = 1.11 at the ordered datum and 1.329 at the cancelling, sums twenty copies of a planar-geometry C as if every adjacent pair had it. Sixteen of the twenty adjacent pairs are bent, and their C is unread. **(D1)'s verdict, "Hypothesis I's adjacent sum is not met at the object's reach at that integrand", is WITHDRAWN.** So is the same use in the abelian column (D4): S₂₀ = 3.33 and 3.92 at U(1), β = 1.2, are planar geometry at the named data by the same mechanism.
   - **§17 item 3, "the sup form of (8.5)'s integrand at the second scale from β = 2.2 has adjacent sum at least 1.33 at the sharper cap", is WITHDRAWN as stated.** The same lower-bound argument applied to the four coplanar pairs alone (the planar object is built correctly) gives at least 4 × 0.066463830 = 0.2659 at the cancelling datum and 4 × 1.113/20 = 0.2226 at the ordered datum. Both are below one and decide nothing about the adjacent sum. The sixteen bent pairs add a nonnegative amount that has not been read.
   - The numbers derived from these sums elsewhere carry the same withdrawal: the "factor 1.6–3 from Hypothesis I at the second scale from β = 2.2" (the operator's correction note of 11-01 19:5x, corrected there), and the "1.33" quoted to the proof agents (corrected to them 05:3x).

3. **What stands.** The planar object's readings stand as planar readings: §12 (b), the planar two-step, and the proof agent M2's L² readings (20ρ: 0.304/0.349 at W2L2, 0.630/0.772 at W3L2, U(1) at 1.2 2.40/2.96, gapped U(1) at 0.9 1.31/1.49, every one labelled planar geometry on M2's page). So do the engines' known answers K1–K4, and the datum dependence as a property of the planar geometry.

4. **The next instrument, with its two-sided control.** The bent row needs the corner plaquettes as internal cycles of the network. `okey` must consult `lkey(b, d, 0)` first, so that an object link enters a staple as itself. The n + 1 corner plaquettes then join the contraction as plaquettes with two object wires and two outside wires, and their cost is a state space counted before the run. The control is two-sided. On the planar object (corner count 0) every reading must be unmoved. At the ordered datum the bent reading must PART from the planar one, the sighted half the defect hid.


## §19 — THE ONE-STEP MAP RUNS DOWN AT EVERY DATUM; LINE 111'S "β ≈ 3.5" IS A β ↔ β/2 SLIP, AND TWO EXACT STEPS REACH THE STRONG-COUPLING WINDOW AT THE READ DATA (2026-11-02 06:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; found by the reading of scratchpad share 027_P against the engine's own convention; nothing above edited)

1. **The slip.** §7's results (line 111 of this page) read "at the ordered datum the blocked one-plaquette coefficient a′_½ = 0.41 is Wilson's at β ≈ 3.5, above the fine coupling … the 'map' runs up or down by the datum's choice". The engine's convention is the paper's (`w2_strip.c` line 8: w(V) = Σ_s (s+1) a_s χ_{s/2}(V), a_s = I_{s+1}(β)/I₁(β); line 190; `w2_strip_n.c` line 215), so the fine coefficient at β = 2.2 is a_½ = I₂(2.2)/I₁(2.2) = 0.4645 (THE GROUND's 0.464). The blocked a′_½ = E[χ_½(A)]/2 is 0.41 (ordered), 0.28 (cancelling) and 0.019 (Haar-random, seed 1), smaller at every datum. Inverting a_½(β) = I₂(β)/I₁(β) gives β_eff ≈ 1.85, 1.19 and 0.076 (the reader's double-precision Bessel series, which reproduces the record's own 40 at β = 0.05 and 4.2 at 0.4764). Inverting I₂(β/2)/I₁(β/2) by mistake gives 3.69 and 0.152, and that is the page's 3.5 and the 0.15 of §7's "a gas near β ≈ 0.15". One exact blocking step from 2.2 LOWERS the coupling at every datum read; the withdrawal reason "runs up or down by the datum's choice" is false.

2. **Two steps.** §10 (b)'s two-step coarse loop at W2L2 inverts under the same convention to β_eff = 0.2930 at the ordered datum (E[χ_½(A)] = 0.146) and 0.2225 at the cancelling (0.111). Both lie inside Theorem 8.9's certified window (0.4764), and the cancelling one inside Theorem 8.7's unconditional 0.2452. The one-step table is 1.8469 / 1.1859 / 0.0760 at W4L4 and 1.3304 / 1.0127 / 0.1903 at W2L2. At the data the record calls nearest the measure's typical one, two exact ℤ⁴ steps reach the window that the hierarchical model reaches in eight.

3. **Strength.** These are READINGS: fixed fine data, the planar geometry (§18: every "bent" dump is planar at the named data), and truncated (spins to 1 at W2L2). The one-plaquette coefficient is the marginal of one coarse plaquette at a pinned datum. That is neither the blocked action's coefficient with the fine field integrated, nor its supremum over coarse configurations, which a single-site criterion must take (the proof agent M2b's Theorem A1). A landing needs (a) Theorem 8.9's certificate passing in its coefficient-supplied mode at the two-step blocked action, multi-plaquette couplings included through its tilt, and (b) that action certified over every coarse configuration with the fine field integrated. The Dobrushin disk (β < π/24) is not reached at two steps. The certificate's 0.4764 is, which is why the exit runs through the certificate and not through the disk.

4. **The next stage.** The certificate in its coefficient-supplied mode is run first on the read two-step coefficients, with every one of a′_s at both data and the read pair couplings as its tilt; that test is the falsifier of the two-step landing at the reading level. Then the two-step object with the fine outside integrated. Then the supremum.


## §20 — THE COARSE HYPOTHESIS FROM THE FIBRE: A REDUCTION, ITS CONSTANT, AND WHY IT DOES NOT CLOSE THE WINDOW FOR GAUGE-COVARIANT COARSE LINKS (2026-11-02 07:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat, at the desk; nothing above edited)

1. **The theorem (proved here).** Work in §8.3's setting, with a block-local coarse map and versions of the conditional laws defined for every coarse configuration.
   - Let ∂y be the fine links outside B_y that share a plaquette with a link of B_y. By the paper's partition they lie in the 80 blocks at distance one.
   - Let p_y(dv_y | ω_∂) be the law of v_y given the links of ∂y. It is a finite pinned object, because the Wilson measure's conditional law of B_y given everything outside depends only on ∂y.
   - Let δ_y = sup_{ω,ω′} ‖p_y(·|ω_∂) − p_y(·|ω′_∂)‖_TV be its ergodicity coefficient.
   - Let T_∂(y, y′) be the supremum, over coarse configurations differing only at y′, of the total variation between the fibre laws ν_j(ω_∂ ∈ · | v).
   Then for y ≠ y′:

   **C^λ_j(y, y′) ≤ δ_y T_∂(y, y′)/(1 − δ_y).**

   *Proof.* Integrate block y's fine field. By the tower and Markov properties, λ(dv_y | v_{−y}) = ∫ p_y(dv_y | ω_∂) μ̃(dω_∂ | v_{−y}), where μ̃ is the law of ω_∂ given the coarse configuration off y, and μ̃(·|v_{−y}) = ∫ ν_j(dω_∂ | w, v_{−y}) λ(dw | v_{−y}). Let v, v′ differ only at y′, and write c for the total variation between λ(·|v_{−y}) and λ(·|v′_{−y}).
   - Dobrushin's contraction gives c ≤ δ_y ‖μ̃ − μ̃′‖.
   - Inserting ∫ ν(·|w, v′_{−y}) λ(dw | v_{−y}) and applying the triangle inequality gives ‖μ̃ − μ̃′‖ ≤ T_∂ + c.
   - Hence c(1 − δ_y) ≤ δ_y T_∂. ∎

   Under Hypothesis II(b), applied to indicator functions of ω_∂ (N(f) ≤ 80, distance at least |y − y′| − 1), T_∂(y, y′) ≤ min(1, 80K′ρ′^{|y−y′|−1}). So **Hypothesis I holds with (κ, α) as soon as sup_y δ_y = δ satisfies δS/(1 − δ) ≤ α < 1**, where S(K′, ρ′, κ) = Σ_{r≥1} n(r) min(1, 80K′ρ′^{r−1}) e^{κr} with n(r) = (2r+1)⁴ − (2r−1)⁴ and ρ′e^κ < 1. Theorem 8.5 then holds from Hypothesis II alone plus the one pinned number δ.

2. **The constant (computed, double precision, a table and not a proof input; scratchpad `reduction/S_table.2026-11-02a.txt`).** The threshold δ* = 1/(1 + S):

   | K′ | −ln ρ′ | κ | S | δ* |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | 1 | 1 | 0.5 | 1.08 × 10⁶ | 9.2 × 10⁻⁷ |
   | 1 | 4 | 1 | 5318 | 1.9 × 10⁻⁴ |
   | 1 | 8 | 1 | 325.7 | 3.1 × 10⁻³ |
   | 1 | 16 | 1 | 217.5 | 4.6 × 10⁻³ |
   | 10 | 16 | 1 | 217.8 | 4.6 × 10⁻³ |

   The stake was met at (1, 1, 0.5). It was REFUTED at (1, 8, 1): the stake read about 4.2 × 10³, having dropped the min-factor 80e⁻⁸ at r = 2, and the run reads 325.7. When the fibre decays over one coarse unit by e⁻⁸ or faster, which a physical correlation length well below the block gives, δ* ≈ 0.003–0.005. The near shells (80 at r = 1) set the threshold.

3. **Two-sided reading.**
   - With the holonomy map, δ → 0 along the sequence because the coarse variable equidistributes. Hypothesis I then holds trivially and all content sits in II(a), exactly the diagnosis of revision 32.
   - For U(1) in the Coulomb phase, the fibre has no long modes: the coarse variables carry them, and the zero-block-average modes have the block-scale Laplacian gap. So II plausibly holds for both groups. The group dependence then sits in δ, since the coarse variable's law becomes insensitive to its boundary only where the coupling has run to strong coupling.

4. **Why it does not close the window, the pre-mortem done before landing.** δ is a supremum over PINNED fine data, and for gauge-covariant coarse links it is of order one at every scale. The paper's coarse link, the holonomy along a block edge in the block's fixed gauge, and every block-local link variable run through links adjacent to ∂y. Pinned boundary data can therefore align those links: flat data pull them toward the identity, with an effective field of order β times the number of straddling plaquettes. The data can also rotate them, since a gauge transformation of the data at the boundary sites rotates the law of v_y. So p_y(·|ω_∂) is far from Haar and depends strongly on ω_∂, even where λ(dv_y | v_{−y}) is near Haar. The blocked measure's small influence at a strongly coupled coarse scale comes from AVERAGING over the fibre's strongly fluctuating boundary layer, which the pinned supremum discards. This is the proof agent M2b's distinction carried to the coarse kernel: averaging is legitimate for what is integrated. The pinned sensitivity of the gauge-INVARIANT coarse loop is small at two steps from 2.2: E[χ_½(A)] lies between 0.005 and 0.146 at the read data, so δ_loop ≥ 0.035. But loops are not a product space, and Dobrushin's comparison needs one.

5. **What this places.** Every route through a pinned supremum of a gauge-covariant coarse variable is closed by item 4: the tower lemma, this reduction, and fixed-datum influence readings. The window's difficulty is the AVERAGING over the fibre of gauge-covariant coarse links at O(1) coupling. The next objects are:
   - a fibre-averaged δ, the boundary-layer law integrated against the fibre given the coarse data, bounded through Hypothesis II itself (a self-consistent form δ ≤ f(K′, ρ′));
   - or a gauge-invariant reformulation of Theorem 8.5's comparison step on coarse loops, where pinned sensitivities are small.
   The second is the L² currency's natural home: the reduction in L² (Theorem 8.5′) works with conditional maximal correlations of coarse observables. The proof agents M5 ((W-V)) and M2b ((W-S)) are on the two channels of that form.


## §21 — THE ONE FLOW: THE CLAY STATEMENT FOR EVERY COMPACT SIMPLE G AS ONE THEOREM ABOUT ONE OBJECT, ITS INPUTS LISTED; AND §20'S LEMMA ADVERSARIALLY VERIFIED (2026-11-02 08:00 EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat, at the desk; the operator's orders of 07:5x–08:1x: "the 4d infinite mass gap … the one that Clay wants, proven perfectly" and "prove it across every group"; nothing above edited)

1. **§20's lemma, verified adversarially.** The claim C^λ(y, y′) ≤ δ_y T_∂(y, y′)/(1 − δ_y) HOLDS, with two precisions.
   - **The tower step needs block-locality.** Since σ(v_{−y}) ⊆ σ(links outside B_y), λ(dv_y | v_{−y}) = E[p_y(dv_y | ω_∂) | v_{−y}]. For a straddling coarse map, Bałaban's averaged variables included, a neighbour's coarse variable sees B_y's own links. The proof then does not apply as written, and widening ∂y does not repair it.
   - **The bound is stated for δ_y < 1.** At δ_y = 1 it is vacuous, and C ≤ 1 trivially.
   The other steps check:
   - the Markov property: given ∂y, B_y's links are independent of the rest;
   - the mixture μ̃ = ∫ν dλ, total probability for conditional laws, with versions defined for every coarse configuration as revision 32 requires;
   - Dobrushin's contraction;
   - the triangle step, whose compared configurations (w, v_{−y}) and (w, v′_{−y}) differ only at y′.

2. **The object.** The renormalisation-group flow of the G-gauge theory: the sequence A₀ (the Wilson action at the bare β₀) → A₁ → A₂ → … of effective measures on the coarse fields at scales a, La, L²a, …, each obtained from the last by one exact blocking step, with its fibre law (the finer field given the coarser). Its aspects at each scale are its effective coupling β_k, the cone C(β) it lies in, and the clustering constants (K′, ρ′) of its fibre. Its one capability is reaching the strong-coupling cone in finitely many certified steps. Theorem 8.5's two hypotheses are aspects of the flow at its exit, not separate demands:
   - Hypothesis I is "the flow is in the strong-coupling cone at the exit scale", through the exit lemma (III-Yang_Mills 8bcfffd);
   - Hypothesis II is "the fibres cluster", accumulated along the flow.

3. **THE ONE FLOW THEOREM (the architecture; each input listed with its status).** Let G be a compact simple group. Suppose there are a family of cones {C(β) : β ≥ β_s} of effective actions, a drift c(β), and fibre constants (K′, ρ′) such that:
   - **(F1) entry:** the Wilson action at β lies in C(β), for β ≥ β_s;
   - **(F2) step:** one exact blocking step maps C(β) into C(β − c(β)) for every β ≥ β_s, with c(β) ≥ c_min > 0 and c(β) → b₀-rate as β → ∞, and its fibre satisfies Hypothesis II with the constants (K′, ρ′) uniformly;
   - **(F3) exit:** C(β_s) lies inside a strong-coupling region where a certified expansion gives Hypothesis I (Theorem 8.9's certificate in its coefficient-supplied mode for SU(2); Osterwalder–Seiler's region for every G).
   Then for every bare β₀ the flow enters C(β_s) after k* ≤ (β₀ − β_s)/c_min steps. Because c(β) has the asymptotic-freedom rate, along the asymptotically free sequence the exit scale L^{k*}a converges to a fixed physical length. Hypotheses I and II then hold there uniformly in the cutoff, and Theorems 7.4 and 8.5 (or 8.5′) give every limit point a unique vacuum and a Hamiltonian with no spectrum in (0, m), m > 0. The group dependence sits in ONE place, the drift c(β): it is positive for every non-abelian G (b₀ = 11C_A/(48π²) in the program's normalisation) and zero for U(1). So the U(1) flow never reaches the exit, which is the control every valid chain must pass.

5. **(E2) reduced to a UV comparison: a lemma proved at the desk; the reduction's UV input open.**
   - **LEMMA.** A closed subgroup H of O(4) containing the hyperoctahedral group B4 is finite or equal to O(4). *Proof.* If H is infinite, H⁰ is a nontrivial connected Lie group normalised by B4, so Lie(H⁰) is a nonzero Ad(B4)-invariant subspace of so(4) ≅ Λ²ℝ⁴. Λ²ℝ⁴ is irreducible under B4: the sign changes act on e_i∧e_j by the six distinct characters ε_iε_j, which S4 permutes transitively. Hence Lie(H⁰) = so(4), H ⊇ SO(4), and B4's reflections give H = O(4). ∎
   - **Consequence.** Suppose a limit point is invariant under B4 and translations, and its stabilizer in O(4) is closed. The stabilizer is closed when the Schwinger functions are continuous in their points on the class of observables used; ME2 is verifying that. Then the limit point is O(4)-invariant iff it is invariant under ONE rotation of infinite order.
   - **The rotation.** Take R = left multiplication by q = (1+2i+2j+4k)/5. Re q = 1/5, and 2cos θ = 2/5 is not an algebraic integer, so R has infinite order. 5R and 5R⁻¹ are integral, so 5ℤ⁴ ⊂ ℤ⁴ ∩ Rℤ⁴: the lattices aℤ⁴ and R(aℤ⁴) share the coarse lattice 5aℤ⁴.
   - **What (E2) then needs.** (a) **UV universality** on the common coarse lattice: the effective actions from the Wilson action on aℤ⁴ and on R(aℤ⁴), at a small physical scale, converge to one limit. This is the same kind of statement as (E1) in full, M3d's two-cutoff comparison of the complete model: a change of the bare lattice, by refinement for (E1) and by rotation for (E2). (b) **Uniqueness of the infinite-volume state** for that limiting action, which Hypotheses I and II along the flow supply.
   - So (E2) is not a separate infrared identity: it is a UV comparison plus the hypotheses the gap already needs. The proof agent ME2 is verifying every step and the literature; M3f has the comparison.

6. **The core at the desk: one route refuted in its naive form, its surviving form named.** M2b's Theorem A1 proves the coarse supremum is necessary for criteria built from single-pair coefficients, and Lemma A2 proves the fine-data supremum removable. A1's class holds no JOINT-influence criterion. The two-step coarse measure is disordered at its typical datum (0.006) and coupled only at aligned data (0.28), so a disagreement-percolation criterion with chessboard-priced bad (aligned) environments was staked.
   - **HOLE FOUND twice in its reduction lemma.** (i) Adapted, environment-dependent bounds do not compose: P(∩E) ≤ E[Π r] is false (r₂ = 1_{E₁}, E₂ = E₁ gives r₁ against r₁²; the true form with predictable bounds is √E[Π r]). (ii) Chessboard bounds joint probabilities, not products of conditional probabilities (two disjoint events of probability ½).
   - **Repaired** by revealing each site's environment first: P(γ ⊂ D) ≤ (√q + √2·z^{1/4})^{|γ|}. The repair is the trunk seat's alone, with no second reader.
   - **Too weak where it was wanted.** It needs q ≲ 10⁻³ against about 0.1 at two steps, gains nothing over the coarse-supremum Dobrushin at three, and the true coarse measure is quasilocal, not finite-range Markov.
   - **The surviving form:** a large-field polymer expansion at the IR end, with aligned regions as the large field priced by local activity bounds.

7. **The window for every G** (M2b; MG). (W-S)_G, the 0⁺⁺ glueball exchanged across one block, is the core. (W-V)_z adds one supplement per nontrivial central element.
   - For SU(2), Hom(½⊗½, ½) = 0 puts the leading coupling (≥ 99.98 %) in the centre-odd sector.
   - For G₂, Hom(7⊗7, 7) ≠ 0 puts the leading (7,7) channel inside (W-S), so SU(2)'s small SO(3) channel is no evidence for G₂. For G₂, F₄ and E₈ the window is Hypothesis I in full.
   - A twist moves only a sheet iff the twisting element is central (W-S2).
   - Turán slaving does not generalise: Wilson's coefficients break the Cartan-product form for SU(3) (9/8) and for F₄, E₆, E₇, E₈ (E₈ 3844/3375).
   - "The centre is a confinement mechanism, not a gap mechanism" (MG).

8. **THE FLOW RE-SEEN, AND THE INPUTS REGROUPED** (no status changed by regrouping). The flow on effective actions is the motion of the measure's typical set across scales, from the vacuum to the disorder. At each controlled scale its control is one act, a typical/rare decomposition priced at its reference point:
   - **UV:** the typical field is near the vacuum; the rare field is the large field, priced by the action (Bałaban).
   - **IR:** the typical coarse field is near Haar; the rare field is the aligned region, which A1 forbids ignoring. Hypotheses I and II are this decomposition's two halves.
   - **The crossover** is the exchange: the bounded band of scales where neither reference makes the rare set rare.

   U(1)'s drift is zero, so its typical set never leaves the vacuum (the Coulomb phase). Every non-abelian G has drift c_G ∝ h^∨ > 0, and the centre decides only the IR channels. (E1) and (E2) together are one UV aspect: the typical set at each physical scale does not depend on the bare lattice.

## §23 — THE ULTRAVIOLET CHASM PRICED: BAŁABAN'S FORMAT CERTIFIED AT ≥ 10^641 AFTER LEMMA RD, §21'S ESTIMATE REFUTED; THE TUBE ROUTE WITH ONE MONOTONE INEQUALITY; ITS CENTRE CORRECTED AND ITS LARGE-FIELD STEP NAMED AN ASSUMPTION; THE CONVEXITY WALL IS WILSON'S 90°, AND THE HEAT-KERNEL STAKE; THE JUNCTION ATTACKED FROM ABOVE (2026-11-02 09:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat, with the proof agents M6b and M8; the operator's orders of 09:1x–09:2x: "the goal has been and still is the irreducible final conclusion wherein you prove the mass gap infinitely across 4d" and, on a side agent's note, "either verify or correct, then proceed"; nothing above edited)

1. **Lemma RD is proved, given CMP 116's own inputs** (the same status as M3c's Lemma M). It does not lower Bałaban's threshold to 10²–10⁴. §21's estimate is **REFUTED** by the proof agent M6b.
   - **The method.** Every occurrence of κ₁ on CMP 116 pp. 2–21 bounds an object and none defines one. The Cauchy step is replaced by exact inclusion–exclusion evaluation of the decoupling integrals, a multilinear expansion in the walk operators, and decay from walk lengths. Every κ₁ condition disappears.
   - **What binds instead.** Bałaban's output format relocalises history terms on □̃⁵, a block of 11⁴ M-cubes with d_k ≥ 914.0625 (Lemma MS). The format's decay therefore charges e^{(1−2δ)κ·914} to the constant, whichever decoupling is used.
   - **CERTIFIED** (interval arithmetic, every stake held):

     | Chain | log₁₀ β_B ≥ |
     |---|---|
     | Lemma RD as proved, Bałaban's format, κ = 1, L = 3 | 747.261 |
     | same, infimum over L | 641.402 |
     | Bałaban's printed chain with Lemma 1's base carried, κ = 1, L = 3 | 2021.980 (not M6's 982.39; C₂ ≥ 115.64, not 56) |

   - **The page's own floor.** One printed step sums at rate δκ over at least 91^t domains (c₃ = 91, counted by brute force). It therefore needs κ ≥ 10 ln 91/(1 − 2/L) (135.33 at L = 3). At that floor Lemma RD leaves ≥ 10^{28,850} for every L, and Bałaban's own chain ≥ 10^{65,525}. This is a necessary condition of the printed step, not his constant. Lemma KP (one entropy payment per scale) would cut the κ floor tenfold.
   - **Projection, uncertified:** Lemmas M_w and KP would give 10^{226.37} at L = 3, and 10^{151.57} at L = 11, where T18 binds.
   - **What these numbers are.** They price **Bałaban's output format**, not the ultraviolet problem: a forced cost of the printed and derived chain, not a lower bound on the true size of any term (M6b). The proof agent M6c carries the format's constants.

2. **A side agent's summary of item 1, relayed by the operator:** VERIFIED in its numbers and CORRECTED in three places.
   - "Lemma RD is proved" → proved **given CMP 116's inputs**.
   - "Hundreds of orders of magnitude from the middle" → true of Bałaban's format and not established for the problem; item 3 is a route with physical constants.
   - "The computer-checked middle near β ≈ 5" → **no certified middle exists.** M8 found no lowest certified β ("none"); the exact engines give READINGS up to β ≈ 3–5; Hypothesis I is certified only at β ≤ 0.4764 (conditional) / 0.2452 (unconditional).

3. **THE TUBE** (the proof agent M8). The cone of effective actions is a tube around the renormalised trajectory, not a ball around Wilson's action. Tree-level blocking keeps the F² coefficient exactly and generates dimension-six loops of the same order in β, which contract by exactly L⁻² (READ: −0.250026 at L = 2, −0.111126 at L = 3; the free fixed point's dimension-six content (kL)²/6 on the axis, (kL)²/8 on the diagonal, the same for L = 2 and 3).
   - **PROVED:** six inequalities (H1)–(H6) imply the cone property, and the scheme offset telescopes. Certification of the whole half-line [β*, ∞) is then ONE monotone inequality,
     A1/β + K₃/β² ≤ c₁ − δ_min, with c₁ = 8b₀ ln L = 0.257511806833 (L = 2) and 0.408146557324 (L = 3) (CERTIFIED),
     given a remainder K₃ uniform in β. That is not 38,814 / 24,489 step certificates.
   - **LOWEST CERTIFIED β: NONE.** Conditional thresholds: K₃ = 10² gives β* = 27 (L = 2) / 20 (L = 3); 10⁴ gives 224 / 170; 10⁶ gives 2196 / 1675. Loop-structured remainders (K₃ ~ 10–100) would give 7–27. At β = 50 the sufficient budget is K₃ ≤ 417 / 792, with a certified convexity radius ≥ 0.3.
   - **Every G:** C_A → h^∨, and dim G enters through the χ² tail and the vertex counts.

4. **The trunk seat's adversarial check of the tube (math-olympiad): a correction and an assumption.**
   - **(i) The centre.** M8 centres the tube on the four-class truncation of the renormalised trajectory. Tree-level blocking generates the full classical fixed-point action, whose quadratic tail outside the four classes is O(1) in β units. That tail falls into the polymer remainder, so (2.3)'s "(ε_K-feedback)" term is not 1/β-suppressed, and that is the term M8 left unanalysed. The repair is to centre on the full Hasenfratz–Niedermayer action, with K the deviation from it.
   - **(ii) (H5) is an assumption.** It bounds large fields by a χ²₃ tail outside the convexity domain, which is circular. Large-field regions form a polymer gas with activity ≤ e^{−β(1−cos a)κ_LF} per plaquette, which is a Kotecký–Preiss problem. The program's certified KP instrument, fixed today, would price the UV's large fields as it prices the IR's strong end.
   - Both are with the proof agent M8b.

5. **THE CONVEXITY WALL IS WILSON'S 90°, AND THE HEAT-KERNEL STAKE.**
   - **The wall.** M8's search (rec/h6_convexity) lost convexity on every ray where single plaquettes pass ≈ 90°: the plaquette function's own radial Hessian cos|Θ| changing sign, not the BCH cross terms. The tube's large-field floor β ≥ 1.4771/b² (16.41 at b = 0.3) is therefore set by the Wilson cosine.
   - **The trajectory's shape.** The renormalised trajectory is not a cosine: the classical fixed-point action spreads a coarse plaquette's flux over L² fine plaquettes and is nearly quadratic in the angle. The heat-kernel weight models it, and M8's §2.2 already admits it.
   - **The formula (checked).** With t = 1/(2β), the SU(2) heat kernel's principal branch K ∝ (θ/sin θ)e^{−θ²/4t} has radial Hessian d²(−log K)/dθ² = 1/(2t) + 1/θ² − 1/sin²θ. It is positive below θ_c, where 1/(2t) = 1/sin²θ_c − 1/θ_c². So θ_c = 2.03 (116°) at β = 1, 2.40 at β = 2, 2.47 at β = 2.5, 2.69 (154°) at β = 5, against Wilson's π/2 at every β.
   - **STAKE.** If the link-slice radius follows θ_c, b_cvx moves from ≈ 0.66 to ≈ 0.85–1.1 and the large-field floor falls toward ≈ 1.5–2.
   - **COUNTERWEIGHT.** In link coordinates the Hessian also carries (∇s)·(curvature of the composition map). The heat kernel's gradient grows like θ/2t where Wilson's is bounded by β, so the BCH term may bind earlier. M8b is running the adversarial search with both weights.
   - **What binds next:** A1/c₁, the one-loop fixed-point motion against the one-loop drift. It is ≈ 7.8 at L = 2 on M8's estimate S′K_r ≈ 2, and it falls with ln L (γ₀ falls with L, 1.85 → 1.00). M8b computes S′K_r.

6. **THE JUNCTION, ATTACKED FROM ABOVE.** The shortest complete path is:
   - the tube theorem, with its centre repaired, large fields proved and K₃ certified;
   - the tube pushed down toward β ≈ 3, through the heat-kernel convexity and the A1/c₁ floor at larger L;
   - certified exact steps with Hypothesis II′ on [0.48, ~3];
   - per-group strong ends;
   - the proved reduction.

   Once proved, the tube would also supply the UV comparison (E1) and (E2) need (§22 item 5), for bare actions whose blocked images enter the tube, because it contracts the irrelevant directions.

   **The least-supported object** is certifying the exact steps' tails at O(1) coupling: the readings rise with the cap, and the instrument is the character expansion whose convergence fails there. Pushing the tube down shrinks that interval from above. If the tube reaches β ≈ 3, the engines are needed only for a_½ ≤ 0.568 (and only for a_½ ≤ 0.32 if it reaches 1.4) instead of up to the UV.

## §24 — FOUR CORRECTIONS AND TWO READINGS OF THE UV HALF: THEOREM PI-4 AS STATED HAS A HOLE (REPAIRED AT SMALL OR REAL d), E1 AND LEMMAS B AND D PROVED GIVEN NAMED INPUTS, §20'S RANGE STALE, §22'S U(1) CONTROL OVERSTATED, §23'S TIME LABEL; THE BAND'S DIAGNOSIS (2026-11-02 09:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat, with the proof agents M3dR and M3f and the reader 046_R; nothing above edited)

1. **THEOREM PI-4 AS STATED HAS A HOLE** (found by M3d's second reader, M3dR; it corrects §22 item 4, where PI-4 was listed as proved pending that reader).
   - **The hole.** The boundary-layer field A_k (CMP 119 (3.23), (3.25), (3.29); LFR I (1.60)) stays in the large-field factor T with scaled size up to C₁p₁(g_k). A complex Wilson deviation d therefore carries the cross term −2d𝒲(A, A_k): linear in A, with coefficient up to |d|C_WC₁p₁. Bałaban removes his own cross term by completing the square with a REAL shift; for Im d ≠ 0 nothing on the page controls it.
   - **Counterexample to M3d's 9.2:** d = iθ_*/2 and |A_k| = ½C₁p₁ give a constant ≍ θ_*C_WC₁²p₁², which tends to ∞. A complex-mean Gaussian route would need new estimates, and analytic continuation from real d fails (the shifted cutoffs are not analytic in d).
   - **The repair, signed:** |d_j| ≤ cε_*/(C_WC₁p₁(g_j)), or d real, with the pure-layer term −d𝒲(A_k, A_k) kept inside T's Gaussian. Theorem S₁(i)–(iii) survives on M3d's own τ-disc when ∇h̃ ≢ 0, because the repair condition holds there automatically (r_τ ∝ δ_* ≤ P(γ)^{−3}). For ∇h̃ ≡ 0 the disc shrinks by p₁(γ)^{−2}. Every radius stays independent of the cutoff and the volume.

## §25 — THE TRUNK SEAT'S OWN SECTIONS CORRECTED: THE W3 WEIGHT IS SIGNED; §22'S "REFUTED" STANDS ONLY AT ONE FRAME (§19'S LANDING NOT ESTABLISHED); §24 ITEM 3 WITHDRAWN; "U(1) AT 1.2 IS GAPLESS" IS NUMERICS, NOT A THEOREM; THE U(1) GATE; (E2)'S REDUCTION PROVED BY ME2, ITS UV INPUT NEEDING ONE MORE ROW (2026-11-02 10:0x EST, read from `date` before this header; the trunk seat, with the reader 045_R and the proof agent ME2, at the desk — math-olympiad and clear-thought on its own §§22–24; nothing above edited)

The comparison in this page's §18 ("the plaquette cap (×1.5 from spins 1 to 3/2) is the mover") set a positive truncation against a signed one.

W4 was read at frame L1, and enlarging the frame lowers the coupling multiplicatively (M2b: 0.98–0.99 per integrated edge-adjacent plaquette). So W2L2 → W4L1 mixes the spin cap with the frame.
   - **The accurate status:** §19's two-step landing at the ordered datum is NOT ESTABLISHED. It held at the lowest spin cap only, and the one positive higher cap read, W4 at frame L1, lands outside every window (β_eff 0.5652).
   - **What decides it:** whether the ordered datum still lands outside at W4L2 and at the largest positive cap at frame L2. That is the proof agent M7b's pending reading, now staked to it. M2b's "the 0.4764 landing is a cap artefact" rests on the same two objects.

   - **(i) Mixed objects.** |ΔE|/4 bounds the total-variation distance only between two data on ONE object. 0.005 (the Haar datum, W2L2) and 0.279 (the ordered datum, W4L1) belong to different objects.
   - **(ii) A signed weight.** It cited W3L2 values, which are signed-weight ratios.

4. **"U(1) at 1.2 is gapless" (§22 item 1, §24 item 4) is NUMERICS, not a theorem.** The Wilson action's U(1) transition near β ≈ 1.011 and its Coulomb phase above it are established numerically. The rigorous Coulomb-phase theorems (Guth 1980 for the Villain action; Fröhlich–Spencer 1982) hold at sufficiently weak coupling, above a β₀ they do not compute to be 1.2. So a pass of the readings pipeline at 1.2 contradicts the numerically established phase diagram; it is strong evidence that the pipeline's objects are IR-truncated, not a refutation by theorem.

5. **THE U(1) GATE** (creative-solve). The fixed-datum readings drive U(1) at 1.2 into its confining range at two steps (§24 item 4). If the same pipeline lands U(1) inside the window at three or four steps, it would certify a gap for a theory with a massless photon, and its objects cannot decide the crossing for SU(2) either.
   - **Where the gate runs:** through three and four steps at β = 1.2, as evidence, AND at a coupling inside a proved Coulomb regime for the action the theorem covers, where a pass refutes the pipeline outright.
   - **Who runs it:** the proof agents M7b (the exit form) and M2c (the Hypothesis I row) beside every SU(2) reading. A decider that cannot refuse U(1) must carry the photon's long-range modes: an integrated rather than pinned exterior, or a Gaussian-integrated exterior.

## §27 — THE U(1) GATE REFUTES THE READINGS PIPELINE AS A DECIDER; §25'S "FRAME" PREMISE WITHDRAWN AND §22'S REFUTATION OF §19 REINSTATED ON CONVERGED READINGS; THE TWO-SECTOR THEOREM; THEOREM 8.7 FOR EVERY COMPACT G; THE STRONG END'S OPEN CLASS PRICED; THE LITERATURE MAP (2026-11-02 11:0x EST, read from `date` before this header; the trunk seat, with the proof agents M7b, MF, MG2, M1c and M3fR, the reader 050_R, and the operator's order of 10:4x: "work in conjunction with your agents AND relevant research, but only conduct research manually"; nothing above edited)

1. **THE U(1) GATE REFUTES THE READINGS PIPELINE AS A DECIDER** (the proof agent M7b; the gate staked in §25 item 5).
   - **P1, pinned exact steps composed:** it lands Wilson U(1) at β = 1.2 within four steps, and Villain U(1) at β_V = 2 and 4. The drift is SCALE-FREE: the two-step pinned map reads β_eff = 0.391·β_V at β_V = 2, 4, 8. From any β_V it therefore reaches the window in O(log β_V) steps, including above Guth's and Fröhlich–Spencer's (uncomputed) β₀, where the photon is massless by theorem.
   - **P2, typical datum with one plaquette plus adjacent pairs, sharply priced:** it lands the free photon at n* ≈ 16β_V for every β_V (exact Gaussian flux variances; a U(1) Monte Carlo reading at 1.2 matches them to 2%).
   - **Verdict.** Both are refuted outright as deciders. Every two-, three- and four-step PASS on this page therefore says nothing about the gap. That covers the two-step typical-datum PASS (a Monte Carlo ⟨W(4,4)⟩ = 0.00136) and the three- and four-step pinned PASSes.
   - **The mechanism:** fixed-datum objects are IR-truncated. The coarse angles forget the integer fluxes, which is Polyakov's and Guth's monopole Coulomb gas seen through the holonomy map.
   - **The requirement on any decider:** carry the long-range modes, with an integrated, not pinned, exterior.

2. **§25 item 2's premise is WITHDRAWN; §22 item 1's refutation of §19 is REINSTATED** (M7b).
   - **The premise was false.** The "L" in W2L2 / W4L1 is the loop-representation cap, not a frame: E[χ_½] agrees to 12 digits at L1 and L2. So §25's "W2L2 → W4L1 mixes the spin cap with the frame" is withdrawn.
   - **The cap problem was the staples.** The engines truncated the staple weights pointwise (328 of 360 plaquettes; reader 050_R: at the identity a link's five staples carry 0.3727 (W2), 0.7556 (W3), 0.9354 (W4) of the exact weight).
   - **Converged readings.** With exact staples the series converges by face cap 4. The two-step a′_½ is 0.1441 at the ordered datum (β_eff = 0.5845, outside every window) and 0.0954 at the cancelling one (β_eff = 0.3839, inside only the conditional 0.4764). §19's two-step landing at the ordered datum is REFUTED on converged readings.      - Shen–Zhu–Zhu, CMP 400 (2023), arXiv:2204.12737: SU(N) |β| < 1/(16(d−1)) in 't Hooft scaling.
     - Cao–Nissim–Sheffield, arXiv:2505.16585: an area law for U(N) at β ≤ β₀(d), unspecified, better than Osterwalder–Seiler only for large N.
     - Osterwalder–Seiler 1978: β ≲ c(d)/N.
   - **The multiscale log-Sobolev machinery** (Bauerschmidt–Bodineau–Dagallier: arXiv:2202.02295, 2307.07619; Probab. Surveys 21 (2024)) turns decay into a log-Sobolev inequality through a Gaussian decomposition the Wilson action lacks. It does not create decay, and no gauge-theory version was found.
   - **Computer-assisted Dobrushin–Shlosman criteria** exist for discrete spins only (Dobrushin–Kolafa–Shlosman 1985; Salas–Sokal). None for continuous spins or gauge theories, which would be new.
   - **Adhikari–Cao** (Ann. Probab. 53 (2025), arXiv:2202.10375): finite non-abelian groups at weak coupling have a gap from discreteness. This BOUNDS finite-subgroup approximations of SU(2) at weak coupling.
   - **DeGrand–Hasenfratz–Zhu**'s eight-parameter SU(2) fixed-point action (hep-lat/9604018, Eq. 4, Table 1): its plaquette part is radially convex to ≈ 136° against Wilson's 90° (the trunk seat's computation; with M8b). [CORRECTED 2026-11-02 13:0x EST: M8b read every weight — Wilson, heat kernel, Manton and this fixed-point action — losing convexity on the L = 2 slice at link radius 0.5–0.7, the term going negative being the composition (BCH) term and never the radial plaquette Hessian, so the 136° arithmetic stands and its use as a wall does not; DHZ moves the radius only by spreading weight over loops.]
   - **No accepted 4D proof** exists as of 2026. Self-published claims are unrefereed. No public "Alpöge–Sussman" work on Bałaban or Yang–Mills was found.

Stroock–Zegarlinski (CMP 1992, finite range, compact continuous spins) give uniform log-Sobolev ⟺ Dobrushin–Shlosman mixing, and the two-scale step is Otto–Reznikoff (2007) and Menz (arXiv:1309.0862).
   - **Pairing 2, with Chatterjee's confinement theorem** (CMP 2021, arXiv:2006.16229): it needs decay under ARBITRARY boundary conditions and a nontrivial centre. That excludes G₂, F₄ and E₈, which have no centre and whose fundamental Wilson loops break.
   - **Both are with the proof agent MPAIR.** Neither reaches the band or the continuum gap.

P1: T1 and T2 through the same engine (28.2), T0 not passable by construction ⇒ its 2.2 reading is UNVALIDATED. P2 (the exact local engine inside, the Gaussian exterior integrated, block-averaged coordinates — M2c's named object) is the candidate speaker; its tooth 2 can only be run at the largest β its spin cap reaches (the exact engines cap at spin 2; SU(2) at β = 10 needs spins near 8: c_j/c_0 ≈ e^{−((2j+1)²−1)/2β} = 0.30, 2.5·10⁻³, 6·10⁻⁷ at j = 2, 5, 8), with the cap's own truncation error as the tolerance.

**29.1 THE CROSSING THEOREM, STATED AS THE CHAIN'S SPINE WITH EVERY INPUT NAMED AND HELD.** Fix L = 2, the averaging block map b (each coarse link the average of the L³ parallel fine paths of its block; THE GROUND §8, MF), and k straight steps from the Wilson measure μ_β at β in the band; write λ_k for the blocked measure (MIR Lemma 2.1: positive continuous density, gauge and translation invariant, reflection positive) and a_c = 2^k for its scale.

THEOREM X (conditional). Suppose
 (i) [the coarse row — the proof agent AD's object, from M2c §5.1] the Dobrushin matrix of λ_k in block-averaged coordinates, with the exterior integrated, admits a positive vector v and a margin η > 0 with Σ_{y′} R(y, y′) e^{κ|y−y′|} v(y′) ≤ (1 − η) v(y), checked entrywise in outward-rounded arithmetic;
 (ii) [the fibre — (F) of the Two-Sector Theorem] the conditional law of the fine field given every block average clusters exponentially at a rate κ_F uniformly in the coarse data on a set of coarse data whose complement is priced: PROVED for its Gaussian part at every L (MF Theorem G; the exact rate of Theorem 3.1, cosh κ₂ = 3, now on two roads — 29.3); for its non-Gaussian part a STAKED PLAN with a named theorem (29.2), its convergence UNPROVED;
 (iii) [the bridge] given (ii), λ_k's specification is quasi-local with exponential tails, so Dobrushin–Shlosman's theory of completely analytical interactions carries (i) from the box where the row is read to ℤ⁴ (classical; its constants depend on κ_F).
Then λ_k lies in the uniqueness regime with exponential decay of its coarse correlations at rate ≥ κ (Dobrushin's comparison, as in Theorem 8.7 applied to λ_k), the Two-Sector Theorem (MF) gives the coarse sector's gap κ/a_c, and (ii) completes it to the full transfer-matrix gap of μ_β through Theorem 7.1's reflection positivity — the lattice gap at β. Then, with the ultraviolet half (Bałaban's renormalisation group from the continuum to the crossover scale, PROVED GIVEN its named black boxes, §§23–27, at a threshold whose floor is 29.6's), the gap is uniform along the flow and survives the continuum limit, for every compact simple G whose constants the strong end certifies (MG2).
What the theorem does NOT assume: a small parameter at any single step of the band on the coarse field (the readings pipeline's assumption, refuted as a decider in §27); a coupling compared with a window (the decider's output is (i), the theorem's own input); the holonomy map (M2c: its row passes gapless theories; MF: its fibre is not uniformly massive).
The refuters inside it: on Villain U(1) at β_V ≥ 2, (i) must FAIL at every k (M2c's Lemma P: a gapless Gaussian's conditional row is ≥ 1 by a sum rule; the averaged row refuses the massless field at every block size, 1.41, 1.83, 2.08 at n = 2, 4, 8) while (ii) HOLDS (the photon's fibre is exactly Gaussian and massive) — so the construction cannot pass U(1) by the fibre alone, and a certified (i) on SU(2) at a_c is a statement about the gauge group, not about disorder. And the band's own non-perturbative refuter (29.4): any decider reading k straight-path steps must refuse wherever β > β_c(N_t = 2^k), the deconfinement coupling at time extent 2^k.
The deciding numbers, in order: AD's deficit at a_c (positive ⟺ (i)); PM's pair moments at the wall (≤ 0.0161 decides MIR's Theorem IR-1 for the straight-path coarse sector, the alternative route to (i)); for (ii), the fibre expansion's activities and the convex domain's probability (29.2); CC's composition is the strong end's own lever and does not enter the band (29.5).

**29.2 THE FIBRE'S INTERACTION IS SMALL PER PLAQUETTE AND O(1) PER BLOCK — (ii)'S NON-GAUSSIAN PART IS A STAKED PLAN WITH A NAMED THEOREM, NOT A RESULT (the desk, 13:5x–14:0x; the fibre reader `photon_carried/fibre_gauss.py`, `fibre_gauss2.py`, record `fibre_gauss.2026-11-02a.txt`; the theorem's page `reduction/fibre_theorem.md` in the records).** The fibre of the averaging map is the law of the fine field given every block average. Its Gaussian part is massive by the block constraint alone — the precision on the block-zero-mean subspace is bounded below by the block's first nonzero Laplacian eigenvalue, g_L = 2 − 2cos(π/L) = 2 at L = 2, uniformly in the volume, the mass and the coarse data (MF, Theorem G; PROVED) — and the reader measures what that mass buys: the fibre's in-block variance is 0.128/β (against the unconstrained field's 0.155/β, Watson's constant, read to 5·10⁻⁵ at NK = 48), and its block covariance decays exponentially while the unconstrained field's decays as a power law. At β = 2.2 the fibre's fluctuation is θ_rms = (0.128/2.2)^{1/2} ≈ 0.24 rad per site, and the plaquette angle's fibre variance ≈ 0.18: the Wilson cosine's quartic term is β·3Var(θ_p)²/24 ≈ 0.009 PER PLAQUETTE — small — and a 2⁴ block owns 96 plaquettes, so the interaction is ≈ 0.9 PER BLOCK — order one. The small parameter is therefore per plaquette, not per block: the polymers of any convergent expansion must be plaquettes, not blocks. The band's coarse field has no small parameter at all (why every single-scale criterion failed on it), the fibre has one per plaquette, and whether that is enough is exactly the theorem not yet proved:
- (F3, the small-field expansion; STAKED, UNPROVED) the fibre expanded about its Gaussian with plaquette polymers (activity ≈ 0.01 quartic, ≈ 0.02 for the non-abelian cubic vertex at second order), the Kotecký–Preiss sum over a plaquette's 24 neighbours, 24·0.01·e^{1/2} ≈ 0.4 against the limit 0.5 — a THIN margin at 2.2 — the Gaussian's off-diagonal covariance carried by the Brydges–Kennedy tree-graph form; STAKED for `fibre_kp.py`: the per-plaquette quartic activity in [0.006, 0.015], the cubic's second-order activity in [0.01, 0.04], the KP margin positive at a = 1/2, the expansion's own threshold in (1.2, 2.2); falsifier: a per-plaquette activity above 0.04 (then the plaquette-polymer expansion fails even where the mass is largest and (ii) needs a resummation the chain does not have);
- (F4, the fibre's own large fields; classical given F2) priced by the Gaussian tail — Lemma CF's mechanism (M8b: Caffarelli on the convex domain);
- (F2 and F5, the domain and its probability; UNPROVED, the weak link) the fibre's mass is uniform only where the fibre Hessian at the coarse background is convex — M8b READ that domain as link radius 0.5–0.7 for every weight (by the composition (BCH) term, Nielsen–Olesen on smooth modes; the certified radius b* is CVX's object) — and the probability of backgrounds outside it is not yet bounded with a usable constant: the typical background at 2.2 has θ_rms ≈ 0.35 per fine link (the sup over a whole block's sphere being what M8b's radius measures), and the chessboard price of an excursion beyond b* (M8b's CB-0 as corrected by UV1 to single orientations, 29.6) is small per block only if the free-energy constant C is near its true value 1.25 (UV1's reading; the proved C ≤ 5.27).
The refuter inside (ii): for Villain U(1) the fibre is exactly Gaussian — (ii) is where the two theories AGREE, (i) is where they part, as Theorem X requires. READ AS CONDITIONAL until `fibre_kp.py` reports the activities and the convex-domain probability is bounded.

**29.3 THE FIBRE'S GAUSSIAN RATE ON TWO ROADS — MF's THEOREM 3.1 REPRODUCED TO NINE DIGITS (the reader `photon_carried/fibre_pole4.py`, records `fibre_pole.2026-11-02a–d.txt`).** The fibre's asymptotic clustering rate is a pole, not a tail: the smallest x at which the block precision of the free field given every block average, A(K) + Π, is singular at K = (Re k₀ + ix, k⊥). On the line Re k₀ = 0 the nearest singularity is x = 2.634 (cosh x = 7, at k⊥ = (π, 0, 0); 3.525 at k⊥ = 0, cosh = 17) and does NOT govern the decay; MF's Theorem 3.1 sits on Re k₀ = π — the fibre's decay ALTERNATES, (−1)^d e^{−κd} — where the pole at k⊥ = 0 is x = 1.762747174, cosh x = 3.000000000: MF's cosh κ₂ = 2 + 3/(L² − 1) = 3, by an instrument sharing no construction with MF's (the block Fourier transform and the smallest singular value, against MF's cardinal cubic spline bracket). The reader's own known answers: the unconstrained massive field's pole equals the lattice dispersion 2 arccosh(1 + m²/2) to nine digits at m = 0.5 and 1, and the massless one sits at 0 (no gap). The exact rate rises with the mass (1.847 at m = 0.5, 2.063 at m = 1) while Theorem G's Combes–Thomas bound is the uniform one. A first draft's tail stake (the operator norm of the alternating blocks, ratios 0.130 → 0.146 at d = 6..10, rising toward e^{−κ} = 0.1716) was ill-posed as an asymptote and is withdrawn; the pole is the observable. The reader is ready for the SU(2) fibre once the exact engines carry block averages (EI).

**29.4 THE STRONG END AND THE BAND'S NON-PERTURBATIVE REFUTER (the proof agent MPAIR, two reports; `records/agents/2026-11-02/math/MPAIR/WORK.md` §§0–16.7).**
- **Pairing 2, confinement: the SU(2) area law for β < 0.4924** (0.4544 on Lemma 8.6's constants alone; MG2's L86 mode reproduces 0.4544), by two independent roads — PROVED GIVEN THE GROUND's Lemma 8.6, its §8.1 MAD lemma and Chatterjee's perimeter lemma (read in full); on ℤ⁴ also the standard Gibbs-conditioning fact, on even tori nothing more. Wilson's area law holds for every Gibbs measure and every half-integer spin. Classical: the Durhuus–Fröhlich reduction to a slab σ-model and the checkerboard decimation. New: the slab's staple has length one, so the decimated σ-model has no direct terms, α_σ = 30·C_med. It is 2.95× the Cao–Nissim–Sheffield line (0.1667). **Every group with a nontrivial centre** on [0, β^DF_G): SU(3) 0.4633, Sp(2) 0.4557, SO(6) 0.6224, SO(8) 0.7517, E₆ 1.2731, E₇ 1.5551; Chatterjee's centre symmetry on the same range. Chatterjee's own hypothesis (his Definition 2.3) is NOT met verbatim (plaquettes whose cover link is a fixed boundary edge stay unintegrated: 40 local types, at most 3 per link) and is delivered for β ≤ β^C_G, SU(2) 0.2432, open on (0.2432, 0.2452]; the trivial-centre groups (SO(odd), G₂, F₄, E₈) get nothing from it. **This is confinement, not the gap.**
- **Pairing 1, log-Sobolev:** an EXPLICIT LSI on [0, β^BE_G) PROVED (Bakry–Émery on the decimated marginal, whose Hessian is second order in β, lifted exactly) — SU(2) β^BE = 0.195, 2.34× Shen–Zhu–Zhu's 0.0833, better for 13 of 15 groups (E₇, E₈ lose); an explicit Poincaré inequality on the whole Dobrushin range [0, β*_G) PROVED; the LSI on [0, β*_G) PROVED GIVEN Stroock–Zegarlinski (its constant finite, not evaluated); no gauge fixing anywhere. MPAIR's unification: exact integration of linearly-entering variables in disjoint factors turns first-order couplings into an exponential-family covariance, second order in β — THE GROUND's C_dir ∝ m(5β), the Bakry–Émery gain and the σ-model's missing direct terms are one mechanism.
- **Object 1, the Durhuus–Fröhlich road one straight-path step from 2.2: REFUSED at 2.2, validated by both teeth.** The theorem's input α_σ (the Dobrushin row of the checkerboard-decimated slab σ-model) reads R⁻ = 9.811 as a LOWER bound on the true row (exact total-variation distances between two conditional laws, interval Riemann sums) against a certified upper bound 398.36: the row itself is above one. Teeth through the same code: T0 passed (SU(2) at 0.2452: R⁺ = 0.20960, R⁻ = 0.18992, CERTIFIED; at 0.4924 R⁺ = 0.99978); T1 passed (compact U(1) at 1.2: R⁻ = 9.146, refused; Villain at β_V = 2 / 4 / 8: R⁻ = 12.45 / 13.63 / 13.93, refused each — the path can refuse Villain and never certify it). PROVED: Theorem DF_h (Wilson's area law from uniform decay of straight-path holonomy correlations in slabs of height h; height 2^k is k straight-path steps); Lemma COMP (height 1 → height 2 with squared constants); **Lemma FT (decay at height h implies confinement at time extent N_t = h, so deconfinement at N_t = h REFUTES the height-h hypothesis)**; and, GIVEN Fröhlich–Simon–Spencer 1976, the height-1 slab with identity boundary data is the 3D O(4) model (SU(2)) or O(2) (U(1)), whose infrared bound makes the height-1 hypothesis FALSE for SU(2) above 2W₃ = 1.010924 and for U(1) above W₃ = 0.505462 (W₃ the cubic Watson integral, two ways to 10⁻²³). READING (Fingberg–Heller–Karsch, source read): β_c(N_t) = 1.8800, 2.1768, 2.2986 at N_t = 2, 3, 4 — with Lemma FT the one-step hypothesis is false at 2.2 and two steps (height 4) is not excluded. **THE NEW TOOTH INSIDE THE BAND: every slab-type decider must refuse wherever β > β_c(N_t = h) — a non-perturbative known answer at the crossover itself**, Monte Carlo in grade, a theorem in form (Lemma FT). MPAIR's next object: the height-4 hypothesis at 2.2, refutation test first, then a finite-volume Dobrushin–Shlosman criterion on height-4 slab boxes with the boundary data as variables.

- **UV3 (LF in full; `math/UV3/WORK.md` §§0–23, floors certified in `mpmath.iv`):** pricing large fields per plaquette gains nothing in the worst case (an isolated large plaquette); the decades come from the gauge, the geometry and the normalization. - **UV2 (the rate of (QGQ*)⁻¹, the slice Poincaré; `math/UV2/WORK.md` §§0–13):** THEOREM A (PROVED): for self-adjoint T ≥ λ the Hermitian part of e^{μw}Te^{−μw} has the exact kernel cosh(μΔw)·T(y, y′), the first-order term drops, and the rate scales as (λ/m₂)^{1/2} — the κ^{−1/2} law of Demko–Moss–Smith and Jaffard with no polynomial and no banded kernel, sharp within √2; THEOREM B (given Theorem L′ as repaired by M3gR, G's kernel bound, CMP 96 Lemma 2.1): the decay of (QGQ*)⁻¹ with an explicit rate δ₁, every compact G, a gain of ×10¹⁰–10¹⁵ over M3g's rate; the floor of record on the proved route: B_ar 748.03 → 641.28, C 460.89 → 394.59, D(q₁ = 1) 70.08 → 60.83, D(q₁ = 0) 14.16 → 14.09 — and the compression-walk expansion now binds (M_W = 10^{20.0} at L = 4). ## §30 — THE COARSE SECTOR IS OPEN, NOT PASSED: AD'S ROW FAILS AT THE ALIGNED ENVIRONMENT WHILE PM'S WALL PASSES ON AVERAGE, AND THE ONE OBJECT BOTH POINT AT IS AN ENVIRONMENT-AVERAGED CRITERION — THEOREM U STATED WITH ITS PROOF PLAN; THE FIBRE'S CONVEX DOMAIN CERTIFIED TO 1/16 AND BRACKETED BY 0.40, SO THE SMALL-FIELD ROUTE TO (ii) IS CLOSED AT 2.2; THE HOUSE INSTRUMENT IN GAWK REPRODUCES THE ANCHOR TO THIRTEEN DIGITS (2026-11-02 15:2x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat iii-43; every report verbatim in `records/agents/2026-11-02/read/REPORTS.md`)

**30.1 THE TWO BAND READINGS PULL OPPOSITE WAYS, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.**
- **AD (the averaged deficit at a_c = 4a; `math/AD/WORK.md`): THE KILL-SWITCH FIRES.** On the blocked SU(2) measure at β = 2.2 with n = 4 (24⁴, the exterior integrated by heat bath, a score-matching fit of the blocked action — a READING), the single-link averaged L² row at the ORDERED environment is 1.1639 (R_ord v = 1.1639 v with v = 1, the quadrature to 4·10⁻¹³), so the deficit 1 − Σ R e^{κ|·|} ≤ −0.164 ± 0.025 at every κ ≥ 0 (6.6σ; 32⁴ gives −0.154); the six-link loops carry it (a link sits in 72 chairs and 24 parallelograms at θ ≈ 0.0056 each, adding ≈ 0.55 to the plaquette's 0.578). IT IS A SUPREMUM FAILURE: at three Monte Carlo environments the row reads 0.816, 0.776, 0.776, and at a Haar environment 0.801 — typical environments sit below one; M2's Theorem 3.1 needs the ess sup. Only the single-link form was computed (THE GROUND's coarse-point form, SU(2)⁴ per site, estimated at 0.64 from plaquettes), so the finding is "the single-link averaged row fails at 4a on the fitted action", not "Hypothesis I fails"; the single-link form refuses even fine Wilson at 0.2452, where Theorem 8.7 certifies, and refuses every β > 0.2226809 by a PROVED bound (the row ≥ 18·I₂/I₁). Teeth through the same chain: Villain U(1) at β_V = 2, 4, 8 refused at every block size (rows 13–18, the coarse U(1) coupling RISING with n); SU(2) at β = 0.2452, n = 4, CERTIFIED 0.030 by a one-term Birkhoff bound; the massive-Gaussian known answers met on two roads to 10⁻¹⁴. PROVED on its page: Lemma A (the averaging map built on one axial tree per block, the same for every direction, has no perimeter self-energy — the trunk's `avg_link` in `photon_gauss.py` used a different tree per direction, which is why its "averaged" column grew 0.489 → 1.449: THAT COLUMN OF 28.1 IS RELABELLED a different map; tooth 1's pass stands, its ratio being read from long-distance correlations and map-independent); Lemma B (ρ ≤ tanh(Δ/4), Birkhoff–Hopf); Lemma C (any explicit environment gives a lower bound on the ess-sup row — the form of every refusal certificate); Lemma D (a von Mises–Fisher tilt cannot raise a one-loop pair's correlation at first order; the free environment is the maximum, 196-point scan); LEMMA E, the multiplicity law (the row is (1/4)·Σ_{ℓ∋c}(|ℓ|−1)|t_ℓ|; polygons through one link number 6, 114, 2868 at lengths 4, 6, 8 — a coarse action passes only if its couplings fall faster than this polygon entropy). At a_c = 8a the couplings are unresolved at 150 configurations and the crossover of this criterion from 2.2 lies in (4a, 8a] as a resolution-limited reading.
- **PM (MIR's pair moments at the wall; `math/PM/WORK.md`): PASS, AS A READING.** The two-step wall floor, with 22 of the 28 loop links integrated exactly given the rest and the exterior averaged over its law (Lemma D1, the tower property, PROVED: this is the number the ring-integrated object converges to), reads [0, 0.0030] for the planar pair and [0, 0.0044] bent, against the certificate's ceiling 0.016175 — E[χ₁(A)χ_½(B)]_c = −3.3·10⁻⁹ ± 3.5·10⁻⁸ on 16⁴, 384 configurations, 15–35× tighter than the 10⁻⁶ asked. Two limits belong with it: it covers one bond type of the KP condition in the certificate's character-priced form only (MIR's crude L¹ form fails at two steps by ≈ ×300 on the one-plaquette term, a desk estimate), and IT IS NOT A GAP TEST — the free photon at SU(2)'s own coarse disorder passes the same wall (0.0019). The U(1) arm: at two steps the test refuses compact U(1) at 1.2 (×39) and Villain at β_V = 2 (×190; monopoles below 10⁻⁶ there), separating SU(2) from U(1) THROUGH DISORDER, and Villain passes at five steps (10.6 → 3.15 → 0.80 → 0.077 → 0.0006) — MIR's Lemma 7.2 seen on the floor. ONE STEP FAILS (planar floor [0.196, 0.222]): the wall is crossed between the first and second exact step. The nearest axis is the spin-½ one (c_½ ≤ 0.060 against the ceiling 0.12809, margin ×2.1). ⟨½ tr W(4×4)⟩ = 0.0013006 ± 0.0000062 replaces the page's 0.00136. What would make it PROVED: a certified bound on the sampled exterior's influence (no convergent expansion exists at 2.2), or certified bounds on E[χ₁χ₁] and E[χ_½χ_½] with reflection positivity in the shared-edge plane bounding the cross moment by their geometric mean — nothing in the program provides either.
- **Read together:** a supremum criterion fails at the aligned environment, which is Haar-rare and is not a phase; an averaged criterion passes, and passes the photon too. The coarse sector's hypothesis (i) is OPEN in every form the record can read, and the object both readings point at is a criterion that averages over environments instead of taking the worst — stated next.

**30.2 THEOREM U — UNIQUENESS AND CLUSTERING FROM A GOOD-ENVIRONMENT ROW AND A PRICED BAD SET (the desk; the full statement, proof plan, inputs and falsifier on `reduction/theorem_u.md` in the records).** For a translation-invariant reflection-positive specification (the blocked measure's, MIR Lemma 2.1), call the environment ω GOOD at y if the Dobrushin row AT ω, ρ_y(ω), is ≤ 1 − η, BAD otherwise (a local event). THEOREM U (conditional): if (U1) the row is ≤ 1 − η on good environments, (U2) the bad event is priced uniformly over the RP Gibbs class — μ(B_{y₁} ∩ … ∩ B_{y_n}) ≤ εⁿ for well-separated sites, by the chessboard estimate on the periodic blocked measure — and (U3) ε·(N+1)·e^a + (1 − η)·e^a < 1 (the Kotecký–Preiss form of "disagreement does not percolate"), then the Gibbs measure is unique in the RP class and clusters exponentially at rate a. Proof plan from classical pieces: van den Berg–Maes' disagreement coupling with the environment-dependence of the per-site bound kept rather than sup'd (U-A); the bad set sparse under the coupling by (U2), the argument run as one-site perturbations on the torus so that every measure in play is periodic and RP (U-B); the mixed-percolation path sum with the bad sites as marked polymers (U-C). Every compact G. THE INPUTS TODAY: (U1) q ≈ 0.78–0.82 at 4a in the single-link form (AD's typical rows, READINGS), q ≈ 0.7 expected in the coarse-point form; (U2) the bad set is the near-aligned environment, Haar-rare (MIR: the aligned probability of a coarse plaquette is the Haar value, 0.058 at 30°), priced by the chessboard estimate on a near-Haar measure (MIR's potential 10⁻³, PM's sup|R − 1| ≤ 1.9·10⁻⁵) — STAKED ε ≤ 10⁻³ at 45° with k ≥ 4 links; (U3) with q = 0.82 and N ≈ 100 (the chair and parallelogram couplings reach range 2) needs ε < 8·10⁻⁴ — BORDERLINE in the single-link form, which is why the coarse-point form is where the theorem should be read. THE REFUTERS INSIDE IT: Villain U(1) has NO good set (rows 13–18 at every environment, AD's T1), so Theorem U refuses it by (U1), not by averaging; the free photon at SU(2)'s disorder has the massless Gaussian's row ≥ 1 at every environment (M2c's Lemma P): refused. The theorem cannot pass a gapless theory through its averaged form, because a gapless Gaussian's row is above one everywhere, not only at rare environments — and it is silent, correctly, where an aligned phase would exist ((U2) fails for that state). What it does and does not do: it gives (i) a hypothesis the exact engines CAN read (rows at sampled environments plus a chessboard-priced tail) in place of a supremum they cannot; it does not remove (ii) — (U2)'s uniform price needs the blocked potential's size, which is the fibre's clustering again, and the rows are read on a fitted action whose certification is (ii).

**30.3 THE FIBRE'S CONVEX DOMAIN — CVX's CERTIFICATE CLOSES THE SMALL-FIELD ROUTE TO (ii) AT 2.2 (the proof agent CVX; `math/CVX/WORK.md` §§0–26).** THEOREM CVX (COMPUTED in verified IEEE arithmetic on PROVED lemmas; M8's additive exponential coordinates, the linear slice V): on the (4,2,2,2) torus with half-open L = 2 blocks every background with all link angles ≤ 1/16 has λ_min(Hess S|_V) ≥ 0.0192; at L = 3 (centred blocks, link angles ≤ 2294/65536) λ_min ≥ 0.0186. The proof: H = H₀ + H₁(B) + R(B) with H₁ the cubic vertex (exactly the Hessian of the four-determinant V₃, by an exact rational Taylor expansion), Lemma M (colour covariance turns the cubic vertex into one 4×4 Hermitian majorant inequality per plaquette position), Lemma R (r ⪰ −16b²h₀ − (9.989b² + 40.27b³ + 42.67b⁴)I from exact integer Taylor tensors and a Dyson bound), one verified Cholesky per b — no cover of the 270-dimensional background family, which cannot be done. AGAINST M8b: a certified non-convex direction at b′ = 0.40133 (a three-point second difference −2.23·10⁻⁵ in 60-digit interval arithmetic), so THE CERTIFIED BRACKET IS 1/16 ≤ b_cvx ≤ 0.40133 at L = 2 and M8b's "Wilson convex to 0.50" IS REFUTED (its heat-kernel, Manton and DHZ brackets are untested and suspect); and the quadratic-majorant method itself caps near b ≈ 0.08–0.1 (Lemma B: every quadratic majorant is colour-blind). THE NIELSEN–OLESEN THRESHOLD with the covariant block constraint is CERTIFIED in (0.5890, 0.6872) at L = 2 ((0.2327, 0.3103) at L = 3); without the constraint every quantised field is unstable; a weak field first RAISES the constrained minimum (1.850 → 2.125). FLOORS: M8's accounting at the certified radii gives β ≥ 378 (L = 2), and no convexity-based floor on this slice can go below 9.17; M8b's 5.9 rested on the refuted reading. WHAT THIS DOES TO §29.2: the fibre's small parameter lives on the convex domain; that domain is certified to link radius 1/16 and bracketed by 0.40, while the typical fine link at 2.2 has θ_rms ≈ 0.35. So the domain on which the fibre's Gaussian reference is massive by certificate is far smaller than the typical fluctuation — the large-field region of the fibre at 2.2 is not a tail to be priced but the bulk of the measure, and its physics is the Nielsen–Olesen instability: charged modes condense in the background's own field strength. THEOREM F OF §29.2 CANNOT BE PROVED BY THE SMALL-FIELD ROUTE AT β = 2.2; the sentence "the fibre is a massive field expanded about a Gaussian with a small parameter" is WITHDRAWN, dated, as a statement about the measure (it is true on the convex domain, which the measure does not fill). What survives: (F1) the Gaussian reference's mass at the ordered background (PROVED, two roads); the per-plaquette smallness of the quartic term AT a convex background; the three lemmas' structure. The band's open core, with today's coordinates: (a) an environment-averaged criterion for the coarse sector (Theorem U, its two numbers to read); (b) the fibre's clustering at 2.2 OUTSIDE the convex domain — the charged sector at O(1) field strength — for which no route in the record gives a proof: the readings pipeline, the sup criteria, the KP route and the convexity route each refuse at 2.2 by their own kill-switches, and the record now says so for each.
