THE SECOND ROAD TO THE GAP NUMBERS (`STDLIB/build/aperanton/cei/eigcheck.c`, sharing no line with `cei_spectrum.c`; the record `flux/eig_all.txt`): the whole spectrum of each dumped T_W2 (196 × 196, row-major, the operator on column vectors) by the complex Hessenberg QR algorithm — Householder, Francis single-shift steps with Wilkinson's shift, deflation on a small subdiagonal — sorted by modulus, the trace identity checked, the two leading eigenvalues re-verified by inverse iteration at themselves (complex LU): at all twenty Haar data λ₁ equal to the trunk's to every printed digit, λ₂ within 1.1e-13 relative, the gap |λ₁|/|λ₂| equal to all six printed decimals at every datum, trace defects 7e-17 … 7e-15, inverse-iteration residuals 7e-16 … 4e-13 — the gap column now has two instruments.

## §32 — STEP 1(a) OF THE SIX STEPS: THE STRUCTURAL THEOREM — THE CELL'S EXTERIOR ENTERS THROUGH ITS GAUGE ORBIT, THE COMPLETE INVARIANTS NAMED, THE CERTIFICATE'S DOMAIN A COMPACT ORBIT SPACE; WHAT IT DOES AND DOES NOT DO (2026-11-03 02:5x–03:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat, at the desk; the operator's six-step plan of 01:3x adjusted on iii-08's and iii-9e's advice, both refusing a fitted polynomial as a lemma; the engine's gate arm 5 the known-answer instance)

**1. The object.** The strip cell C of §31.15: its link set L_C (the ten links of steps 20..29 of the K = 3 strip at W, seven surviving the substitution and three paired with an eliminated partner), its vertex set V_C, its plaquettes P_C (those with all four links in C ∪ the fixed coarse edges), and its cut, the state space 𝓗_W spanned by the open cycles' spins s ≤ W and the open wires' matrix indices. The exterior datum ω — the 248 outside links — enters the cell's Wilson weight in exactly three ways: (a) through the STAPLE SUM of each cell link ℓ = xy, S_ℓ(ω) = Σ_{p ∋ ℓ, p ∉ P_C} U_{p∖ℓ} ∈ ℍ, the sum over ℓ's staples of the ordered product of the other three links of p read as a quaternion, so that the link's weight is exp(β⟨U_ℓ, S_ℓ⟩) with ⟨·,·⟩ the inner product of ℝ⁴ and |S_ℓ| ≤ n_ℓ, the number of staples (for a paired survivor the one field S_ℓ + conj S_{ℓ′} of iii-08's correction, §31.17); (b) through the corner multipliers M_c, the products of outside links along the outside steps of the corner cycles; (c) through the substitution, which fixes the coarse edges at c = 1. Write ω ↦ (S(ω), M(ω)) for the staple-and-corner data; the cell's weight is W_ω(U) = Π_{p ∈ P_C} exp(β·½ tr U_p) · Π_{ℓ ∈ L_C} exp(β⟨U_ℓ, S_ℓ(ω)⟩) · (the corner factors), and the transfer operator T_ω on 𝓗_W is the integral of W_ω over the cell's links in the character basis with the cut's wires left open.

**2. THEOREM S (the exterior enters through its gauge orbit).** Let G_res be the residual gauge group of the cell: the maps g : V_C → SU(2) with g_x = g_y across every fixed coarse edge xy. Let G_res act on the data by S_ℓ ↦ g_x S_ℓ g_y⁻¹ for ℓ = xy and on each corner multiplier by the same rule at its endpoints.
- (i) For every gauge transformation of the lattice supported on the exterior and on V_C whose restriction to V_C lies in G_res, T_{ω^g} = U_g T_ω U_g⁻¹, where U_g is the unitary on 𝓗_W that multiplies each open wire at a cut vertex x by D^s(g_x) in its spin block; hence spec T_{ω^g} = spec T_ω.
- (ii) A gauge transformation supported on exterior vertices not in V_C leaves S(ω) and M(ω), hence T_ω, unchanged.
- (iii) Consequently the spectrum of T_ω, its gap γ(ω) = log(|λ₁|/|λ₂|) and every spectral quantity are functions of the G_res-orbit of (S(ω), M(ω)). The orbit space Ω_C = {(S, M) : |S_ℓ| ≤ n_ℓ, |M_c| = 1}/G_res is compact, and the gap is a function on it. A complete set of invariants is: the norms |S_ℓ|² and the real parts of the quaternion products along the closed walks of the cell's graph with the fixed coarse edges contracted, a link traversed backwards contributing its conjugate and a corner multiplier entering as a link — the first fundamental theorem of invariant theory for SU(2) along a graph (Procesi, *Adv. Math.* 19 (1976), for the matrix form; the lattice form, in which the gauge-invariant functions of link variables are generated by the Wilson loops, Giles, *Phys. Rev. D* 24 (1981)).
- (iv) The character truncation by spin commutes with the action of (i) — a wire transforms within its spin block — so (i)–(iii) hold for the exact operator and for every W-truncated engine alike.

*Proof.* (i) The gauge transformation is the change of variables U_ℓ ↦ g_x U_ℓ g_y⁻¹ on the cell's links, under which the Haar measure of each link is invariant (bi-invariance), the plaquette traces of P_C are invariant, and the fixed coarse edges are invariant by the hypothesis g_x = g_y across them. Its effect on a staple sum is S_ℓ ↦ g_x S_ℓ g_y⁻¹: the staple of ℓ = xy is a path from y to x through two exterior vertices, at which the factors g and g⁻¹ of consecutive links cancel, leaving the endpoints' factors; the same cancellation gives the rule for the corner multipliers. Hence W_{ω^g}(gUg⁻¹) = W_ω(U) pointwise, and the integral over the cell's links of W_{ω^g} with the wires open equals the integral of W_ω in the new variables, in which every open wire's D^s(U_ℓ) has become D^s(g_x)D^s(U_ℓ)D^s(g_y)⁻¹; the factors D^s(g) at the cut vertices are the unitary U_g (D^s is unitary on each spin block, and the cycles' spins are unchanged), so T_{ω^g} = U_g T_ω U_g⁻¹. (ii) At an exterior vertex not in V_C every staple and corner path passes through the vertex with one link in and one link out, and g⁻¹g cancels. (iii) is (i) and (ii) together with the compactness of a product of closed balls modulo a compact group acting continuously; the completeness of the invariants is the cited theorem, the fixed edges contracted because they carry the identity under the residual group. (iv) is read off the proof of (i): U_g is block-diagonal in the spins. ∎

**3. The known-answer instance, already in the gate.** `cei_gate.sh` arm 5 (run i GREEN, e5ca5b5be): the datum rotated by a gauge transformation at the MID-PATH vertex (1,0,0,0) leaves the substituted reading unmoved, |diff| < 1e-12, at c = 1 and at c = (0.6, 0, 0.8, 0) — that vertex is exterior, (ii) — while the same rotation at the COARSE vertex (2,0,0,0) moves it by more than 1e-6: that vertex is an endpoint of a fixed edge whose other endpoint is not rotated, so the transformation is not in G_res and the datum leaves the orbit. Theorem S is the statement the arm measured, with the group that makes it true named.

**4. What the theorem does.** It reduces the supremum over the exterior that Step 1's certificate must take — iii-08's k-step Dobrushin–Shlosman coefficient as a supremum over SU(2)^248 — to a supremum over the compact orbit space Ω_C, of dimension at most 4|L_C| − 3·|V_C/∼| (ten links, the vertices identified across the fixed edges), parametrised by the invariants of (iii); and it tells the instrument which readings of the datum can matter at all: only the norms and the closed-walk traces, which are the magnitudes and the preferred-holonomy traces iii-08's flux table measured, together with the walks that table did not form. It also settles one question qualitatively: for the EXACT operator of the strip cell at fixed β, T_ω has a strictly positive continuous kernel on the group variables, so by Jentzsch's theorem λ₁ is simple and strictly dominant and γ(ω) > 0 at every datum; γ is lower semicontinuous on the compact Ω_C, so min_{Ω_C} γ > 0 exists — the uniform-in-datum positivity of the strip cell's gap is a theorem, with no value attached.

**5. What it does not do, said at its strength.** (a) It attaches no number: the minimum of §4 is existence, and Hypothesis II′ needs ρ′ with its K′. (b) The strip cell is NOT the fibre: its exterior fixes the fine links outside the strip as well as the coarse data, so its gap is the rate of a further-conditioned object, an anchor for the fibre's clustering constant and not its value; the fibre of the reduction (THE GROUND, Hypothesis II′, lines 124–130) is the fine field given the coarse variables alone, a specification on the fine lattice whose clustering at the block scale is a Dobrushin–Shlosman condition over blocks with the sup over the exterior — iii-08's instrument, whose domain this theorem reduces — in the averaged currency MF's file names (the Gaussian part Theorem G, the large-field correction priced by its λ-probability). (c) The truncated engines' gaps are the truncation's as much as the physics' (iii-08's cap table: a pinned link at x = β|S| = 22 keeps 24 % of its weight at W2, 50 % at W4), so the certificate's object is the exact operator with the cap's tail bounded or the cap raised at the pinned links, which the trunk is building into the engine (per-link caps) as Step 1(c). The readings of §31.15 stay readings. Nothing above is a claim about Hypothesis II; it is the first lemma of its certificate's construction, and the sup it reduces is the one the instrument now takes.

**31.17 RESULTS 3 — THE GAP COLUMN CERTIFIED, AND ONE CORRECTION TO RESULTS 2 (2026-11-03 02:1x EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat db5968f8; the trunk's delegation (ii); the instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/cei/certspec.c`, the record `cei/flux/cert_all.txt`, the long form `cei/flux/READING.md` §10).** THE INSTRUMENT: a float Schur form by the complex Hessenberg QR with Q accumulated; the leading eigenvalue swapped to the top; the one well-conditioned similarity X = [[1, x₀],[0, I]] with x₀(λ₁I − U_r) = −u, V = QX, Y = X⁻¹Q^H explicit (the first draft diagonalised the whole operator and every real dump refused it at |I − YV| ~ 1e5 … 1e7 — the tail of tiny eigenvalues conditions the full eigenvector matrix beyond the double word; said and dated); midpoint–radius arithmetic throughout (Higham's γ bound on every product, every radius inflated), C = YV = I − R with ‖R‖∞ < 1 verified, D = YTV with the entries' radius n·2⁻⁵²|entry|, B = C⁻¹D enclosed through ‖C⁻¹ − I‖∞ ≤ r/(1 − r); Feingold–Varga on Bᵀ with the blocks {0} and the rest, the rest's modulus by Gelfand on its ‖u‖-pseudospectrum in enclosures (ten squarings, normalised); the leading disc disjoint from it holds exactly one eigenvalue. THE KAT: a 12 × 12 of known spectrum enclosed (|λ₁| = 10 within 2e-12, |λ₂| = √5 bounded 0.04 % above), a one-percent entry radius honestly widened, the planted perturbation REFUSED NO-SEPARATION by name, a Jordan pair's bound honest. THE COLUMN: all 22 W2 dumps and the 3 W3 dumps CERTIFIED — W2 ordered/cancel GAP ≥ 51.722484 (float 51.751769); W2 random 1–20: 9.040449, 10.485290, 6.893895, 8.870146, 17.353820, 28.715800, 40.181880, 29.795997, 31.642495, 9.792740, 16.249625, 13.860926, 24.056659, 17.682291, 20.045490, 10.342750, 13.827347, 29.454017, 20.749620, 27.525544 (the float gaps 0.06–0.2 % above each); W3 random 1–3: 10.341061, 23.833007, 13.044192 (float 10.356446, 23.859518, 13.069854). **§32 CORRECTION — THEOREM S (i) AND (iii) WERE TOO STRONG, AND THE CONTROL THAT DECIDES THE CORRECTED FORM IS BUILT AND READ: THE EIGENVALUES ARE INVARIANT UNDER THE PERIODIC RESIDUAL GROUP ONLY, THE SINGULAR VALUES AND THE TV-TYPE QUANTITIES UNDER THE WHOLE RESIDUAL GROUP (2026-11-03 03:4x–04:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the engine's multi-vertex gauge door `CEI_GAUGES` in the cei_funcs fragment, called from the inserter's tensors anchor; `gauge_control.2026-11-03a.txt`; `cei_gram.awk`, `cei_specdiff.awk`)**

**1. What was wrong, found by reading the cut.** The dump is the operator from the cut after step 19 to the cut after step 29 READ AS ITS TRANSLATE τ (cycles +4, wires +16: CEI-TRANSFER's own line). A gauge transformation g acts on the entry cut's open wires by U_A(g) and on the exit cut's by U_B(g) = U_A(g∘τ), so T_{ω^g} = U_B(g) T_ω U_A(g)⁻¹ — a UNITARY EQUIVALENCE for every admissible g and a SIMILARITY only when U_A(g) = U_B(g). §32's (i) wrote the similarity for every g in the residual group and (iii) rested on it; both are replaced by Theorem S′ below, and §3's sentence that arm 5 "measured" the spectral statement is withdrawn: arm 5 rotated the datum and read a scalar observable, which instances the substitution's constraint and the exterior-only invariance, not the spectrum. §1 (the object), (ii) exterior-only, (iv) the truncation commuting with the wires' D^s(g), §4's Jentzsch statement and §5 stand; §4's dimension count is withdrawn (it omitted the corner multipliers and used the wrong group).

**2. The groups, read off the substituted strip.** CEI-SUBST fixes ten coarse edges at c = 1, each a straight path of two fine links with one of the two eliminated in favour of its partner (U_e = U_p⁻¹): the bottom and top rows' x-pairs and the four full rungs at x = 2, 0, −2, −4. A pair is preserved by a gauge transformation iff g agrees at the pair's two ENDPOINTS (the middle vertex cancels), and the ten coarse edges connect all eight coarse vertices (2,0), (2,2), (0,0), (0,2), (−2,0), (−2,2), (−4,0), (−4,2): so **G_res = {g : one common value on the coarse class, arbitrary at every other vertex}**. The entry cut's four open wires sit at the free vertex (−2,1) (one wire of each open cycle) and at the coarse class (the other wire of each); the exit cut's, by τ, at (−4,1) and the coarse class. Hence **G_per = {g ∈ G_res : g_{(−2,1)} = g_{(−4,1)}}** and **G_0 = {g ∈ G_res : g = 1 at (−2,1), at (−4,1) and on the coarse class}**.

**3. THEOREM S′.** (i) For g ∈ G_0: T_{ω^g} = T_ω. (i′) For g ∈ G_per: T_{ω^g} = U_g T_ω U_g⁻¹ with U_g = D^{s}(g_{(−2,1)}) and D^{s}(g_coarse) on the wires in their spin blocks, so the spectrum is invariant. (ii) For g ∈ G_res ∖ G_per: a unitary equivalence U_B T_ω U_A⁻¹ — every singular value, every unitarily invariant norm and, for the exact kernel, the total-variation distances between conditional laws (a gauge transformation is a measure-preserving bijection of the cell's variables) are invariant; the eigenvalues are not. (iii) exterior-only transformations leave T_ω unchanged (§32's proof). (iv) A transformation outside G_res — one coarse vertex alone — is not a symmetry of the substituted object; nothing need hold. (v) Consequently the spectrum of T_ω is a function of the G_per-orbit of the staple-and-corner data (S, M), and the singular values and TV-type quantities of its G_res-orbit; the orbit spaces are compact, and the orbit of a compact group acting linearly on a real vector space is determined by its polynomial invariants (the orbits are compact and disjoint; a continuous separating function is approximated by polynomials and averaged over the group), which for the gauge action on the strip's quotient graph are generated by the traces of closed walks — in the quotient with the coarse class one vertex and, for G_per, (−2,1) identified with (−4,1) — a link traversed backwards as its conjugate (the quiver invariant theory: Le Bruyn and Procesi, Trans. AMS 317 (1990), the compact form by the unitarian trick; the lattice statement for group-valued links Giles, Phys. Rev. D 24 (1981)). *Proof* as §32's, with the wires' open indices picking up D^s(g) at their vertices and the entry and exit wires identified by τ: U_B T U_A⁻¹ is a similarity iff g∘τ = g at the wire vertices, which on this strip is the one condition g_{(−2,1)} = g_{(−4,1)}, the coarse class carrying one g already. ∎

**4. THE CONTROL, two-sided on two observables (W2, β = 2.2, the strip K = 3, datum random:1, the cell steps 20..29; the ungauged engine reproduces the dumped T byte for byte; g = (0.6, 0, 0.8, 0), h = (0.28, 0.96, 0, 0); the eigenvalues all 196 by `cei_spectrum`, the singular values as the eigenvalues of the Gram T^H T by `cei_gram.awk` + `cei_spectrum`; HELD below 1e-9 of |λ₁|, MOVED above 1e-6):**

**5. What follows for the two certificates, said once.** The k-step Dobrushin–Shlosman coefficient of the fibre kernel is a supremum of TV distances, so its domain reduces by the WHOLE residual group, and its first reduction — the exterior entering each link only through its staple sum, |S_ℓ| ≤ n_ℓ — is untouched by this correction: the instrument iii-08 is building takes that sup as designed. The per-datum eigenvalue gap of §31.15 is a reading of a G_per-orbit, so two data in one G_res-orbit may read different gaps (case f: 9.05 against 11.54 at the same physics); its infimum over the datum space is an anchor for the fibre's rate and never the lemma, which is what §32 §5 already said and this correction makes exact. §32 §4's uniform positivity needs no group: for the exact operator on L²(SU(2)²) (the two open cycles' partial holonomies, Peter–Weyl) the kernel K_ω(h, h′) is the integral of the cell's strictly positive continuous weight over the cell's links with the two exit cycles' partial holonomies held fixed — each exit cycle holds a private surviving link ((−3,0)x and (−3,0)y for the row-0 cycle, (−3,1)y and (−3,2)x for the row-1 cycle at the cut after step 29), so the fixing is a Haar-invariant fibration — hence strictly positive and continuous on the compact SU(2)² × SU(2)², Hilbert–Schmidt, compact with discrete spectrum; Jentzsch's theorem gives λ₁ simple, positive and strictly dominant; the nonzero spectrum of a compact operator varies continuously with the operator and T_ω with ω, so γ(ω) = log(λ₁/|λ₂|) is positive and continuous on the compact SU(2)^248 and min γ > 0 exists, as §32 §4 stated and with no value attached. The truncated engines are compressions whose weights are not pointwise positive, so Jentzsch is not theirs; their gaps stay readings.

## §33 — THE CROSSOVER'S BOUNDARY, MEASURED FROM BOTH SHORES IN ONE NIGHT: THE LOCAL ROUTE TO HYPOTHESIS II REFUSED AT β = 2.2 BY EXACT NUMBERS, STEP 1 OF THE SIX STEPS WITHDRAWN AS WRITTEN, THE GAUSSIAN PART OF THE FLOW A THEOREM WITH ITS CONSTANT, DIAGONAL REFLECTION POSITIVITY FALSE ON THE EVEN TORUS AND REPAIRED IN THE LIMIT (2026-11-03 02:2x–04:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat recording what the three seats found; every number with its record)

**1. The refusal (iii-08, `dobrush1.c`, landing with its KAT; the certified column 88c5608e3).** Hypothesis II consumes the clustering of the FIBRE — the fine links given the coarse configuration — and a k-step Dobrushin coefficient of the fibre kernel is a Dobrushin coefficient of that measure at the FINE coupling of the rung: the fine links inside a block interact at the rung's β, and no blocking lowers the fibre's own coupling (the blocked β_eff 0.293 / 0.223 of §18 is the coarse marginal's). The heat-bath law of one link given its staple sum V is p_V(U) = e^{β⟨U,V⟩}/Z, Z = 2I₁(β|V|)/(β|V|); the influence of a neighbour sharing m staples is the total-variation distance between p_V and p_{V′} with |V − V′| ≤ 2m, its supremum over the move read exactly (Simpson at 200², the KAT TV(V, V) = 0 and two opposite peaks at x = 22 reading 1.000000). AT β = 2.2: sup TV = 0.7356 at m = 1 (attained at |V| = 1, the staple reversed), 0.957 at m = 2; the row sums 4.41 for the strip cell's fibre (six neighbours inside the fibre) and 13.2 for the bulk fibre (eighteen) — the single-site condition fails by a factor 4 to 13 at EVERY exterior, and no supremum over the exterior lowers a row sum below its value at one exterior. The tilt route (Proposition 8.4) reads 1 − e^{−2β} = 0.988 per neighbour. The supremum over the exterior of the cell's k-step coefficient by enclosure is a supremum over an 88-dimensional compact set (22 effective fields × 4), not a computation. **REFUSED, with its reason, and the trunk accepts it at full strength.**

**2. What the same instrument certifies: the cone's exact boundary by the local route (readings; the enclosure is iii-08's next instrument).** The single-site route closes at β < 0.396 for the six-neighbour fibre and β < 0.131 for the eighteen-neighbour bulk; the tilt route, rigorous now, at β* = −ln(1 − 1/n)/2 = 0.0912 and 0.0286. ## §34 — THE BLUEPRINT WEIGHED AGAINST THE RECORD BEFORE ANY CODE: WHAT IT STATES RIGHTLY, WHAT IT OVERSTATES, WHAT IT REFUTES ITSELF ON, AND THE ASSIGNMENTS (2026-11-03 04:35 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; the operator's blueprint of 04:0x — Part I, YM-1a–c, YM-2A–B, YM-3 — as it reached iii-08's seat and was forwarded verbatim at 04:3x; the operator's standing order of 04:4x repeated: close every open item, no placeholders, no scope minimization; nothing above edited)

**1. The frame.** "99 % built" is struck as a measure: the open core is the band — Hypothesis I and Hypothesis II′ at O(1) coupling — and the ultraviolet threshold, and a percentage says nothing about where the difficulty sits. What the blueprint names as the three gaps is the record's own state table (31.6, 30.5): YM-1 the fibre's non-Gaussian clustering uniformly over the exterior and in 4D; YM-2 the coarse sector at 2.2; YM-3 the ultraviolet-to-crossover bridge. VERIFIED. Its account of the Gaussian fibre sector (cosh κ_n = (2n² + 1)/(n² − 1), e^{κ_n} ≥ 2 + √3 at every level, the staggered pole leading on every cell read) is T-816 and §33.4 (a): VERIFIED. Its account of diagonal reflection positivity is CORRECTED in one word: "resolved" means IN THE INFINITE-VOLUME LIMIT UNDER (H) (ΑΝΑΚΛΑΣΙΣ Addendum I Theorem C; the construction's own clustering, the same input the gap rests on, counted once), while on finite even tori it FAILS with exact certificates (PK; ΑΝΑΚΛΑΣΙΣ Lemma 2) and is not needed there, Corollary 8.3 consuming the limit's. The strong end for every compact simple G at MG2's thresholds stands with one reader; iii-9e's Ο5 per-group inputs are the second road, in progress.

**2. YM-1a — Stone 5 and the k-block split: VERIFIED as the record's own next instruments** (31.15 RESULTS 5; ΟΛΟΚΛΗΡΩΣΙΣ Addendum V; iii-08's certspec.c, §31.17 RESULTS 3). "GAP-UNCONDITIONAL at the full precision" is the target of the split, not a result in hand. ASSIGNED: iii-08 — the certspec × eig_cert k-block split first (owed to iii-9e), then `cei_exact_cell.c` (Stone 5: the seven surviving links contracted in the monomial basis with Stones 1–4, the entries certified rationals with their radii), its known answer the floating operator's spectrum (λ₁ = 3.6097197687738e18, the singlet at λ₁/1552.958844 at W2); this lane re-derives on a road it did not write.

**3. YM-1a's spin-cap tail — REFUTED AS STATED, its right tool kept.** The sentence "the Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the tail block is bounded by the two-plaquette cut tail 2 Σ_{s>W} d_s² a_s⁴, ≤ 2.4 × 10⁻⁵ at W4; by Weyl/Bauer–Fike this certifies the infinite-spin gap" carries no derivation in the record and is wrong in three places. (a) The character cap acts on EVERY weight factor of the cell — about forty, the plaquettes and the staple weights of seven links — not on the cut's two plaquettes alone; a norm bound on T's tail compounds over them, which is exactly RESULTS 5's remark ("crude when compounded over a cell's forty weight factors"). (b) "Every discarded spin sector drops a positive-definite summand" bounds the LEADING eigenvalue from below for a positive operator and says nothing about |λ₂|: the gap is a ratio and is not monotone under dropping positive summands. (c) The exponent four on a_s and the number 2.4 × 10⁻⁵ are not derived from anything on the record. What is right and is kept: Weyl's and Bauer–Fike's perturbation bounds on the similarity-split block, once the tail block's operator norm is CERTIFIED — from the certified Bessel tails of RESULTS 5 (Σ_{k>K} (k + 1)² I_{k+1}/I₁ ≤ 0.929, 0.283, 0.0688, 0.0139 at K = 2..5 per weight) compounded honestly over the cell's factors, or sharper on EI's positive squared weight where the per-weight tail is a tail of a product. ASSIGNED: the trunk, at the desk — the honest tail bound of the cell operator at W, written with its compounding and its number, before any certificate names γ_∞.

**4. YM-1b — the observable switched from the eigenvalues to the singular values and to the Birkhoff coefficient: VERIFIED in part, CORRECTED in one sentence.** Theorem S′ (ii) already states the singular values' invariance under the whole residual group, and the control of §32 CORRECTION read it (case f: the eigenvalues moved by 95 % of λ₁, every singular value held to 3e-17). The Birkhoff–Hilbert projective contraction coefficient of the EXACT positive kernel is invariant under the whole residual group too, since a gauge transformation is a measure-preserving bijection of the cell's variables and preserves the cone of positive functions — and for an INHOMOGENEOUS chain it is the multiplicative quantity (the Hilbert-metric contraction of a product is at most the product of the cells' contractions, Birkhoff): the right observable for Theorem X (ii)'s uniform clause, which §31.17's road (3) already names together with its size, e^{−2βN_p} per cell, true and useless in 4D. CORRECTED: "the singular values govern the product ⋯T_{ω₂}T_{ω₁}" — they do not; ‖T₂T₁‖ ≤ ‖T₂‖‖T₁‖ bounds the NORM of the product, and σ₁/σ₂ of each cell bounds no decay rate of the chain, because the top singular vectors rotate between cells. The chain's rate is the Birkhoff coefficient's, on the singlet-projected cone at symmetric data (Lemma RP-F) and on the full positive cone at a general datum.

**5. YM-1b's interval-box exhaustion of Ω_C — REFUTED AS PRICED.** The planar strip cell's orbit space has dimension about twenty-two (ten links' staple sums, forty real coordinates, less the residual group's eighteen at the five free vertices and the coarse class; the §32 CORRECTION withdrew the earlier count and this one is an estimate said as such), and the gap varies by a factor of fifty across twenty data; a certified branch-and-bound cover needs boxes whose Lipschitz width resolves the gap — of order 10⁻² to 10⁻³ in the staple coordinates against sides of order ten — so the count of boxes exceeds 10⁶⁰ on any accounting. §31.17 (2) priced it in one line ("a bound over the datum space SU(2)^{~40} of one cell's staples, which no cover reaches"). The method is sound in principle and useless at this dimension; the structural lemma — the gap, or better the Birkhoff coefficient of 4 above, as a function of FEW invariants of the datum — remains the object, and the flux table's silence (§31.17 RESULTS 2: |ρ| ≲ 0.3 for every local gauge-invariant reading at W2) is the measured difficulty of naming them. The blueprint's own explanation of that silence (the W2 cap; the periodic-subgroup invariance of the eigenvalue gap) is VERIFIED as the §32 CORRECTION's reading.

**6. YM-1c — the 4D block fibre by the tesseract in the intertwiner basis: VERIFIED as lead 3 (§31.13 (3)), the record's named instrument.** ASSIGNED: iii-9e after Ο5 — `cei_tesseract.c`, the 2⁴ block's 24 internal plaquettes with the 48 straddling datum plaquettes and the four single-path constraints, the vertex factors by ΑΓΚΥΛΗ II's chord and junction doors (T-774), anchored on the Python tesseract value before any new number, Lemma R's node-by-node bound through Ο6's inertia door on the proof side; this lane re-derives.

**7. YM-2A — PM's wall pass certified through shared-plane reflection positivity: CORRECTED, and REFUTED as a route in its stated form.** (a) The bent pair — loop A in the xy-plane, B in the yz-plane — is carried one to the other by the reflection exchanging x and z, a DIAGONAL reflection; diagonal reflection positivity fails on every finite even torus with certificates (PK Theorem T.4; ΑΝΑΚΛΑΣΙΣ) and holds in the limit only under (H), the construction's own clustering: as a route TO the gap it is circular. The COPLANAR pair is carried by a coordinate reflection in the plane through the shared edge, and there MIR Lemma 2.1's reflection positivity of the straight-path blocked measure gives the blueprint's Cauchy–Schwarz bound in the Osterwalder–Schrader inner product, |⟨χ₁(A)χ_½(B)⟩_c| ≤ ⟨χ₁(A)χ₁(θA)⟩_c^{1/2} ⟨χ_½(B)χ_½(θB)⟩_c^{1/2}, VALID (W = 0) for the planar bond type. (b) "The sensitivity to the exterior is bounded by §20's reduction lemma with T_∂ from YM-1": §20's lemma is refused for this use twice on the record — its tower step needs block-locality, which the straddling and averaging maps lack (§21.1), and its δ_y is of order one for gauge-covariant coarse links at every scale (§20.4). What survives: the planar cross moment's bound by two same-spin moments; those moments WITH THE EXTERIOR INTEGRATED are readings (PM: a sampled exterior, no convergent expansion at 2.2) until an expansion carries the exterior, which is the open object unchanged. MIR's Theorems IR-1 and IR-2 stand as implications with their hypothesis unmet.

**8. YM-2B — the coarse-point row at a_c = 4a through the DOMINANCE door: CORRECTED to what it is, a reading worth taking.** The "≈ 0.64" is AD's ESTIMATE from plaquettes ("only the single-link form was computed … estimated at 0.64 from plaquettes"), not a computed row; the coarse-point matrix at 4a would be read on a fitted action (a score-matching fit of Monte Carlo configurations, a reading) at sampled environments, where Hypothesis I's row is an essential supremum over environments; Ο6's DOMINANCE door certifies the inequality Cv ≤ (1 − η)v for the matrix handed to it and never the matrix's provenance. So YM-2B is THE COARSE-POINT READING — the ordered environment's row in THE GROUND's SU(2)⁴-per-site form, the next reading after AD's single-link one, and the premise of any environment criterion (§31.3's Gibbs-typical row beside it) — not a certificate of Theorem X (i). ASSIGNED: the trunk specifies it; AD's instrument (its code in the agents' record) is the engine; no agent is launched.

**9. YM-3 — the tube lowered by CVX's joint linear-box certificate and lead 4's decimated potential: VERIFIED as the record's own next objects (§31.13; CVX's named next, the trunk's).** Their numbers ("past 0.18", "β ≈ 9.17–14.5") are targets. "Compute A₂ by fourth-order jets" is UV1's own next (§29.6, Theorem K): PLAN, VERIFIED.

**10. YM-3's mixed-orientation chessboard floor by diagonal reflection positivity on the cut torus: REFUTED AS A DESK DISCHARGE.** (a) A chessboard estimate needs reflection positivity for a family of reflections whose group tiles the torus; Lemma A's measure μ_N° has diagonal reflection positivity for ONE diagonal reflection's moved set cut, and the group generated by the coordinate and diagonal reflections contains reflections whose moved sets are not cut. (b) The estimate on μ_N° bounds μ_N°'s activities, which differ from the Wilson measure's at the cut; Theorem B bounds the defect for observables at distance r FROM the moved set, and a chessboard image sits on it. (c) At β ≥ 14.5, where the tube lives, Theorem C's (H) is the tube's own conclusion: circular. (d) The "single-orientation homogeneous product" of a mixed-orientation set is the ALL-orientation homogeneous state's free energy, not UV1's single-orientation constant C — the thing CB-0 lacks is exactly that constant. What survives: the question is well posed; the honest instrument is UV1's route — CB-0 for mixed orientations by a direct free-energy bound on the mixed homogeneous state, machine-checked as Lemma AF was — the floor 45.5 standing until it lands.

**11. YM-3's T_loc′ (5)(i) by the implicit function theorem: CORRECTED.** The implicit function theorem gives the nonabelian shift's FORM at first order, −H_ab⁻¹∇H₁(U*_ab), with the tree-level factor; (5)(i) asks a BOUND on the one-block term's nonabelian part uniform in m WITH ITS CONSTANT, which is the finite computation iii-32 priced (the 8⁴ → 4⁴ exact box-average minimizer, days) or a bound from CVX's Lemma R at the block scale with constants of its own; the sketch is a derivation's first line, not its discharge. ASSIGNED: iii-32, after Lemma M_w's line is read here (NOTE 4) and the Wilson d = 3 instance ends — the minimizer as priced.

**12. The formal integration: VERIFIED as the validation protocol (§31.18)** — every finite piece a `decide` theorem with no axiom and no sorry, every analytic link with its hypotheses named and its finite pieces decided; nothing enters on a seat's say-so.

**13. **§34 NOTE — THE SPIN-CAP TAIL AT THE DESK: THE HONEST BOUND AND ITS NUMBER (2026-11-03 05:11 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; §34 item 3's assignment; the instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/cei/captail.awk` (gawk, the house language), its record `captail.2026-11-03a.txt`).** THE BOUND. The cell's exact transfer operator has the kernel K(h, h′) = ∫ Π_i f_i over the cell's links with the cut's holonomies fixed, each f_i a positive factor on SU(2) — a plaquette weight e^{β·½ tr U_p} or a link's staple weight e^{β⟨U_ℓ, S_ℓ⟩} = e^{x·½ tr(U_ℓ T)} at coupling x = β|S_ℓ| — and the cap-W engine replaces every f_i by its character truncation f_i^W. Normalising each factor by its supremum (the gap λ₁/|λ₂| is scale-free), the telescoping Π f_i − Π f_i^W = Σ_i (Π_{j&lt;i} f_j^W)(f_i − f_i^W)(Π_{j&gt;i} f_j) and the Schur test against probability measures give ‖T − T_W‖_op/‖T‖ ≤ Σ_i ε_i(W) with ε_i(W) := ‖f_i − f_i^W‖_∞/‖f_i‖_∞ ≤ Σ_{k&gt;W}(k+1)² I_{k+1}(x_i) / Σ_{k≥0}(k+1)² I_{k+1}(x_i) (|χ_k| ≤ k + 1, every coefficient positive, the supremum at the identity) — RESULTS 5's per-weight tail, compounded over the cell's factors by a SUM, which is the honest form of "the tail block's norm"; Bauer–Fike then certifies the infinite-spin gap from the cap-W matrix only where that sum is small against |λ₂|/λ₁. THE NUMBERS (the record; the KAT: I₁(2.2) = 1.914095 against the table's 1.914089, I₂/I₁ = 0.464479025 the engine's digits, the plaquette tail at K = 4 reproducing RESULTS 5's 0.0688 to 2 %): the per-factor relative tail ε at character index K (spins ≤ K/2) reads, for x = 2.2 (a plaquette): 0.18, 0.054, 0.013, 0.0027, 4.6e-4 at K = 2..6 and 9.1e-6 at K = 8; for x = 11 (a link under five identity staples): 0.78, 0.61, 0.44, 0.29, 0.17 at K = 2..6, 0.049 at K = 8, 0.010 at K = 10, 2.1e-4 at K = 14; for x = 22 (a paired survivor under ten): 0.91, 0.82, 0.71, 0.59, 0.47, 0.25, 0.12, 0.044, 0.014 at K = 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14; for x = 4.4: 0.44, 0.23, 0.097, 0.036, 0.011, 8.1e-4. THE ALIGNED CELL (four plaquettes at 2.2, seven links at 11, three paired survivors at 22): the compounded tail reads 8.87, 6.95, 5.25, 3.79, 2.62 at K = 2..6, 1.11 at K = 8, 0.42 at K = 10, 0.144 at K = 12, 0.044 at K = 14 — against the non-singlet gap's |λ₂|/λ₁ = 1/51.75 = 0.0193 — so THE SUP-NORM ROUTE CERTIFIES NOTHING AT THE ALIGNED EXTERIOR BELOW K ≈ 16, where the cut state (Σ_{n ≤ K+1} n²)⁴ exceeds 10¹², and the blueprint's "2.4 × 10⁻⁵ at W4" is five orders of magnitude from the honest 5.25 (its 2Σ d_s² a_s⁴ counted two plaquettes where the cell has fourteen factors, and the squared coefficient where the sup-norm tail is linear). A HAAR EXTERIOR (ten links at staple sums of norm about 1.5, x = 3.3, four plaquettes): 3.93, 1.57, 0.52, 0.145, 0.035 at K = 2..6, 0.001 at K = 8 — against |λ₂|/λ₁ = 1/6.90 at the slowest datum: equal at K = 5 (spins ≤ 5/2, the strip's 91⁴ peak, 4 h 43 min a run) and below it by a factor four at K = 6 (140⁴ = 3.8 × 10⁸ entries, 6 GB), so at mild exteriors a POSITIVE infinite-spin gap, not its value, is certifiable by Bauer–Fike at K = 6 — exterior by exterior, since the staple sums range 0.7–3.2 (x up to 7, where the K = 6 tail is 0.05 a link). This inequality can be obtained by simple, but awkward, geometric and combinatoric considerations. It follows by considering locally many possible cases." — STATED, NOT PROVED, IN PRINT. **§34 NOTE 2 — THE SPIN-CAP TAIL, CORRECTED AT THE DESK: THE HAAR-EXTERIOR SENTENCE WITHDRAWN, THE NORM ROUTE CLOSED AT EVERY CAP (2026-11-03 05:43 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat, law 18 on its own §34 NOTE of 05:1x; nothing above edited).** THE DEFECT. The §34 NOTE compared the compounded relative tail Σ_i ε_i(W), a bound on ‖T − T_W‖_op in units of the SUP scale Π_j ‖f_j‖_∞, against |λ₂|/λ₁ — which presumes ‖T‖ = λ₁. For a product kernel of positive weights that is false by the product of the weights' sup-to-mean ratios: a weight e^{x·½ tr U} has supremum e^x and Haar mean 2I₁(x)/x, the ratio 2I₁(x)/(x e^x) ≈ 0.80 x^{−3/2} — 0.193 at x = 2.2, 0.133 at 3.3, 0.022 at 11, 0.0077 at 22 — and λ₁ is of the order of the kernel's AVERAGE while ‖T‖_op is bounded only by its supremum, so λ₁/Π‖f_j‖_∞ can be as small as the product of those ratios, 10⁻⁹ at a mild Haar exterior (ten links at x ≈ 3.3, four plaquettes) and 10⁻¹⁹ at the aligned one. Bauer–Fike needs ‖T − T_W‖ small against λ₁ − |λ₂| ≈ λ₁, and the sup-scale bound Σ_i ε_i · Π‖f_j‖_∞ is never that small at any cap in reach: THE SENTENCE "at mild exteriors a positive infinite-spin gap is certifiable by Bauer–Fike at K = 6" IS WITHDRAWN; the aligned-exterior conclusion (nothing certifiable) stands a fortiori. THE POINTWISE ROUTE FAILS TOO: a relative bound f_i^W = f_i(1 − r_i) with sup|r_i| ≤ ρ_i would give the Perron eigenvalue's enclosure directly (positive kernels, entrywise comparison), but the truncation's relative error is largest exactly where the exact weight is smallest (near U = −1, e^{−x} against a sum of large terms; the spins-to-3/2 weight is NEGATIVE there, EI's fix 2), so ρ_i is not small at any cap. WHAT STANDS: the cell's infinite-spin gap cannot be read off any finite character-basis matrix by a norm or a pointwise perturbation bound, at any exterior, at any reachable cap; the certified tier's object for γ_∞ is the EXACT operator in the group variables — its kernel strictly positive and continuous (Jentzsch's positivity, §32 §5) — with a value by a route that never passes through a cap's tail: the Birkhoff–Hopf contraction of the exact positive kernel (true and useless in 4D, §31.17 road (3)), or an exact finite-rank reduction of the kernel whose error is bounded in the KERNEL's own L² (Hilbert–Schmidt) norm against λ₁ with λ₁ bounded below by the vacuum's matrix element — the owed derivation, said here as owed and not done; the captail numbers stand as what they are, the sup-scale tails. The L² sharpening named in the NOTE is moot for the same reason and is not pursued.

CMP 116 p. 20 (read): the resummed terms' exponentials "(1 − 5δ)κd_k(Z_i) … replaced by (1 − 6δ)½Lκd_{k+1}(Z′_i)", Z′_i the smallest D_{k+1}-domain containing Z̃_i (the one-layer enlargement) — that replacement IS (2.36), applied with no additive constant; "Of course, we assume that ½(κ₁ − 1) ≥ 2Lκ"; C₃ = 2(L + 2)⁴O(1)·2E₀C₁α₄⁻¹α₆⁻¹M^q exp C₂κ₁ (α₆⁻¹ in C₃, NOTE 7's reading confirmed on the page); Lemma 3 (2.38). THE REFUTATION. Let Z be the L⁴ small cubes of one big cube C. Z̃ is the (L + 2)-cube about C, properly meeting C and its 3⁴ − 1 neighbours and nothing else, so Z′ = the 3⁴ block of big cubes about C, and d_{k+1}(Z′) = 15 EXACTLY: the sixteen corner cubes of the block (every index ±1) need sixteen distinct tree vertices (a vertex meets the cube of index −1 in a coordinate only with that coordinate on the near wall, +1 only on the far wall), two of them at distance ≥ 1 for every coordinate in which their patterns differ, so a tree holding them has ≥ 15 edges of the big grid, and the central cube's skeleton's spanning tree attains 15. d_k(Z) = δ_L, the same number for L = 3 (Z IS a 3⁴ block of small cubes) — so at L = 3 (2.36) demands 2(1 − 5δ)·15 ≥ (1 − 6δ)·3·15, i.e. 25 ≥ 36 at δ = 1/30: FALSE. iii-32 reached the same domain and the same verdict at the desk in the same hour (bab6d568c, Z = one big cube's small cubes, "2δ₃ ≥ 3δ₃: FALSE"); this lane's road was the 3⁴ block of small cubes straddling the big lattice — the same Z — with the corner argument giving the 15 exactly; the convergence between two readings blind to each other is the finding (law 40). On the chain family (m cubes in a row, d_k = m − 2, the enlargement's span m + 2 meeting j big cubes, d_{k+1} = j − 2) the house instrument finds (2.36) FALSE at L = 3 as well (m = 3: a deficit of 11/15 under the containment convention), and PASSING at every L ≥ 5; the block family passes at every L ≥ 5 by Lemma MS's bound δ_L ≥ (L⁴ − 16)/16 (53.3 against 48 at L = 5, 1080 against 84 at L = 11). THE CONVENTION FOR Z′ DECIDED: "the smallest domain containing Z̃" admits a big cube only if Z̃ meets it with positive measure — a cube Z̃ touches on a face alone is not needed to contain Z̃, that face lying in the properly-met neighbour — so the "proper" column is the page's; a tree meets a closed cube at a point. THE VACUITY THEOREM (desk): a wall-connected domain of n cubes spans at most n per axis, its enlargement at most n + 2, so for n + 2 ≤ L every big cube met shares one corner point and d_{k+1}(Z′) = 0: (2.36) holds trivially for n ≤ L − 2, i.e. for n ≤ 9 at L = 11 — the n ≤ 8 instrument named for L = 11 would decide nothing; what is undecided at L = 11 is the class of domains of span ≥ L − 1 in some axis, where the block, chain, sheet and cross families all pass by their formulas and Bałaban's statement is otherwise unproved. THE SHAPE OF THE TRUE STATEMENT: an additive constant, d_{k+1}(Z′) ≤ (2/L)d_k(Z) + c₀(L), entering the activity bound as e^{(1−6δ)κ·Lc₀/2} per polymer — NOTE 7's junction constant c = Lc₀/2 — and the ladder as + 2(1 − 6δ)κ·Lc₀/2 in log β. ## §35 THE COARSE-POINT READING, SPECIFIED — HYPOTHESIS I's ROW IN THE GROUND's SU(2)⁴-PER-SITE FORM AT THE ORDERED ENVIRONMENT (2026-11-03 06:50 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat, §34 item 8; AD's page and its Lemmas A–E read; THE GROUND §8.3 and Theorem 8.5′ the form)

**35.1 THE OBJECT.** The coarse variable at a coarse site Y is the bundle V(Y) = (V₀(Y), V₁(Y), V₂(Y), V₃(Y)) ∈ SU(2)⁴ of the tree-averaged coarse links of AD's map (the axial-tree transport of every one of the n⁴ fine lines of the block to the block's centre, their sum normalised to the group; gauge covariant, codimension zero, the identity at n = 1; Lemma A: its legs are a coarse gauge transformation, so a coarse loop of V is the chain-map smoothing C²F of the flux with no perimeter self-energy), at the record's two-step scale n = 4 from β = 2.2 (a_c = 4a). The site's conditional law given the environment ξ (every other site's bundle) is the law of V(Y) under the coarse measure; the PAIR's maximal correlation is ρ(Y, Y′ | ξ) = sup E[f(V(Y)) g(V(Y′)) | ξ] over f, g of zero conditional mean and unit conditional variance; the ROW ENTRY is R(Y, Y′) = ess sup_ξ ρ(Y, Y′ | ξ) (M2's L² form); HYPOTHESIS I AT THE BLOCK SCALE in THE GROUND's form is the weighted row sum Σ_{Y′ ≠ Y} R(Y, Y′) e^{κ|Y − Y′|} < 1 for some κ > 0, every site alike by translation. It is one predicate; the certified strong-coupling row (Theorem 8.7, the decimated measure of the perfect cover) is a different one, and AD's single-link row (one coarse link against its 122 neighbours, 1.163904 ± 0.024706 at the ordered environment on the fitted twelve-term action — the kill-switch) is a third: the single link is not THE GROUND's site variable, and a bundle's correlation with a neighbouring bundle is neither the sum nor the maximum of its links' (the four links of a site are read TOGETHER, a bundle's zero-mean functions including the products across its links).

**35.2 WHAT IS READ, AND WHAT EACH READING CAN SAY.** (a) THE ORDERED ENVIRONMENT ξ₀ (every other bundle the identity). By Lemma C (the fitted density continuous and positive) R(Y, Y′) ≥ ρ(Y, Y′ | ξ₀) entry by entry, so a row sum ≥ 1 at ξ₀ REFUTES the hypothesis on the fitted action and a row sum below one PROVES NOTHING by itself; the reading at ξ₀ is the premise of any environment criterion, never its certificate — §34's correction of YM-2B stands. (b) THE GIBBS-TYPICAL ROW beside it: ρ at environments drawn from the fitted coarse measure, the typical size (0.78–0.82 against 1.16 for the single link, AD §12 (v)); it bounds nothing. (c) THE FIRST-ORDER ROW, EXACT ON THE FITTED COUPLINGS: Lemma E lifted from a link to a bundle — at the free environment, to first order in the couplings, the spin-j components of the two bundles are coupled by the 4 × 4 matrix T^{(j)}_{ab}(Y, Y′) = Σ_{loops ℓ ∋ (Y, a), (Y′, b)} t_{ℓ,j}/d_j² (a loop through link a of Y and link b of Y′ couples their spin-j blocks at t/d_j²; the blocks of different j orthogonal at first order), so ρ₁(Y, Y′) = max_j ‖T^{(j)}(Y, Y′)‖₂ (the largest singular value, the bundle's zero-mean functions including the cross-link products) and the first-order row is Σ_{Y′} ρ₁(Y, Y′) — a finite exact arithmetic on a reading (the couplings) with no quadrature; AD's "≈ 0.64 from plaquettes alone" is its plaquette-only value. (d) THE EXACT ROW AT ξ₀ on the fitted action: the pair law p(u, v | ξ₀) ∝ exp Σ_ℓ t_ℓ χ_ℓ(u, v, 1) over SU(2)⁴ × SU(2)⁴, 24 real dimensions — the single link's six were the quadrature's limit (4 × 10⁻¹³) — so the bundle's exact maximal correlation is read by Monte Carlo on the eight links (a READING) with (c) as its exact companion and the single-link row as its known answer.

**35.3 THE ENGINE (house, no Python instrument runs of record).** ymc.c (AD's sampler, C; the exact Wilson weight) supplies the configurations (24⁴ × 400 and 32⁴ × 150 exist in the record); the score-matching fit of the coarse couplings is one linear solve on the blocked loop averages (a C or awk port of fit.py, the known answer the fit's own table: plaq_½ = 2.19976(24) at n = 1, 0.13082(94) at n = 4; AD's fit.py the foreign anchor); the first-order bundle row (c) is a C program on the loop census through a site (sap.py's polygon counts 6 / 114 / 2868 at lengths 4 / 6 / 8 as the known answer of the census); the exact bundle row at ξ₀ (d) is a C Monte Carlo of the eight-link conditional law under the fitted action, the maximal correlation estimated as the top singular value of the empirical conditional-expectation operator on the character basis to spin 1 per link (a basis truncation, said so), the single-link row's 1.163904 reproduced by the same code with the bundle width one as its KAT. Order of build: the fit port and its KAT; the census and the first-order row; the Monte Carlo row at ξ₀ with the single-link KAT; the Gibbs-typical row; then the n = 8 bundle row (AD read 0.28 ± 0.08 for the single link at a_c = 8a).

**35.4 THE KNOWN ANSWERS.** The fit at n = 1 returns β = 2.2 (KAT of the fitter); the single-link row at ξ₀ on the twelve-term fit 1.163904 ± 0.024706 (the bundle code at width one); the photon's bundle row EXACT by the Schur complement of the blocked Gaussian covariance at n = 2, 4 (the abelian side's known answer of the bundle machinery; the single-link photon row is exactly 3 at n = 1); the U(1) refusal (the averaged row fails wherever the photon is massless, M2's Theorem 4.1) reproduced by the bundle code on the Villain configurations.

**35.5 THE STAKES (written before the engine exists).** S-CP1: the first-order bundle row at a_c = 4a on the twelve-term fit lies in [0.5, 1.3], its side of one UNGUESSED (the plaquette-only first order is 0.64; the six-link loops carried 0.55 of the single link's 1.16, and in the bundle form a loop through two links of one site counts once). S-CP2: the Monte Carlo bundle row at ξ₀ ≥ its first-order value (the first order discards the positive higher-spin couplings' squares) — a direction, not a number. S-CP3: the Gibbs-typical bundle row below the ordered one (Lemma D's direction, read not proved). S-CP4: at a_c = 8a the bundle row at ξ₀ below one (the single link read 0.28 ± 0.08). KILL-SWITCH: a bundle row ≥ 1 at ξ₀ at a_c = 4a is a refutation of Hypothesis I on the fitted action in THE GROUND's own form, and then the crossing scale lies in (4a, 8a] as the single-link reading already says.

**35.6 THE PRICE AND THE STATUS.** The fit port minutes; the census and the first-order row seconds; the Monte Carlo row at ξ₀ an eight-link sampler at ~10⁷ sweeps, minutes; the n = 8 row needs the 32⁴ record (exists). Status: SPECIFIED (this section); the engine is this lane's next instrument after the gate landings in flight, and the number is UNGUESSED until it runs.

**§35 RESULTS 2 — RESULTS 1's FIRST-ORDER BUNDLE ROWS WITHDRAWN: THE ENGINE COUNTED A LOOP WITH TWO LINKS IN ONE BUNDLE AS TWO COHERENT SINGLE-LINK COUPLINGS; THE CORRECTED LAW, THE SAMPLER'S KNOWN ANSWERS, AND THE READING THAT EXPOSED THE ERROR (2026-11-03 07:44 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat, law 18 on its own RESULTS 1 of 07:2x; the sampler `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_mc.c` landed with this note; nothing above edited).** THE ERROR. cp_row.c built the first-order coupling matrix T_{ab} by crediting every loop through the link a of the origin's bundle and the link b of the neighbour's bundle with t/d_j², with EVERY OTHER LINK OF THE LOOP TAKEN AS THE IDENTITY — including the other links of the two bundles themselves. For the single-link form that is right (its conditional law fixes every other link). For the bundle form it is wrong wherever a loop holds two links of one bundle: the plaquette at the origin in the plane (a, ν) holds the links a and ν of the origin's bundle and the link ν of the bundle at e_a, and cp_row entered it TWICE, as (a → ν) and as (ν → ν), each t/4, coherently in one column — a nearest site's plaquette matrix then has operator norm 2 × t/4 = t/2 (the 0.1088 of RESULTS 1's table). THE CORRECT FIRST-ORDER LAW: a loop with links a₁..a_p in one bundle and b₁..b_q in the other couples the normalised matrix elements of the PRODUCT P = V_{a₁}^{±}⋯V_{a_p}^{±} (a degree-p function of the first bundle) to those of the product Q of the b's, at strength t/d_j² (Schur orthogonality; the product of Haar elements is Haar), under the pairing fixed by the orientation; loops with the same (P, Q) add coherently, loops with orthogonal parts add as separate blocks (D(V_a), D(V_{a′}) and D(V_aV_{a′}) are mutually orthogonal under Haar). So the plaquettes at a site (p = 2, q = 1) are ISOLATED entries t/4 between a product of the origin's bundle and a single link of the neighbour's, one per plane, maximal correlation t/4 and not a coherent sum; the longer loops through one link of each bundle (p = q = 1) populate the single-link block as in the single-link form; loops with two links in each bundle the product-to-product block. ON THE PLAQUETTE COUPLINGS ALONE the first-order bundle row at a_c = 4a is twenty sites — the eight nearest (p = 2, q = 1, three entries each) and the twelve at e_μ − e_ν (p = q = 1, the plaquette at base −e_ν in the plane (μ, ν)) — at t/4 = 0.0321 each: 0.642, AD's own estimate ("≈ 0.64 from plaquettes alone"), NOT RESULTS 1's 0.916; the full first order with the six-link loops is owed to a corrected engine that keeps the loops' parts (cp_row2.c, next). WITHDRAWN, DATED: RESULTS 1's bundle rows 15.41 / 17.99 / 1.885 ± 0.041 / 1.869 ± 0.054 / 0.378 ± 0.175, the "one and a half times the single-link row" law, the "S-CP1 refuted upward" verdict (S-CP1's [0.5, 1.3] is live again, UNGUESSED), the by-distance table and the per-site table. WHAT STANDS: the single-link rows (9.908 / 12.300 / 1.256 ± 0.028 / 1.249 ± 0.038 / 0.279 ± 0.114 — every loop a two-point coupling there), the fit's digit-for-digit reproduction, the loop census, the n = 8 couplings, the jackknife correction of run d. THE SAMPLER (cp_mc.c: Metropolis on the pair's links under the fitted action with every other link the identity; the maximal correlation read on a basis of matrix-element functions by a SPLIT-SAMPLE estimate — the function pair chosen on one half of the blocks, its correlation read on the other, so the bias is DOWNWARD; the in-sample top singular value is printed beside it and is biased upward by the noise: the dead instrument reads 0.049 naive against 0.006 ± 0.004 split). KNOWN ANSWERS MET: two links coupled by ONE loop e^{t w(V_aV_b⁻¹)}, whose pair law is a class function of V_aV_b⁻¹ so the maximal correlation is EXACTLY I₂(t)/I₁(t) (a class-function kernel acts on the spin-j matrix elements by a_j/d_j; a₀ = 2I₁/t, a_½ = 4I₂/t): 0.464391 ± 0.002673 at t = 2.2 against 0.464479; 0.033481 ± 0.002185 at t = 0.13082 against 0.032682; the dead instrument 0.0058 ± 0.0036. THE READING THAT EXPOSED THE ERROR: the nearest bundle pair (0, e₀) at a_c = 4a on the single-link basis (52 functions a bundle: 2w, 2x, 2y, 2z and √3·R_ij of each link) reads 0.0230 ± 0.0026 (and 0.0193 ± 0.0027 at −e₀), against RESULTS 1's first order 0.1088 — impossible under RESULTS 1's law, since a bundle's correlation cannot fall below the direct single-link couplings its basis contains; the plaquette coupling of that pair lives on the PRODUCT of two links of the origin's bundle, which the single-link basis sees only through the links' means (⟨w⟩ ≈ 0.13–0.18 per link at the ordered environment, the record's line). The single link (0, 0) against (e₀, 1) reads 0.0520 ± 0.0030 (657 and 221 loops through the bundle and the link pair). NEXT, this lane: cp_row2.c (the first order with the loops' parts) and cp_mc with the spin-½ products of the bundle's link pairs in its basis (96 more functions a bundle); the bundle rows then read anew, S-CP1 to S-CP4 judged on them.

**§35 RESULTS 3 — THE FIRST-ORDER BUNDLE ROW WITH THE LOOPS' PARTS: AT a_c = 4a THE RIGOROUS FIRST-ORDER UPPER BOUND IS 2.635, THE PARTS ESTIMATE 0.905, THE PLAQUETTES ALONE 0.654; THE SAMPLER WITH THE PRODUCT FUNCTIONS READS THE NEAREST PAIR AT 0.034 ± 0.003 AGAINST THE PARTS ESTIMATE's 0.038 (2026-11-03 07:56 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_row2.c` and its record `cp_row2.2026-11-03a.txt` landed with this note; the sampler's class-wise row running).** THE CORRECTED ENGINE (cp_row2.c): for every loop through the origin's bundle and a neighbour's, the loop's P-part (its links in the origin's bundle, in cyclic order with orientations) and Q-part (the neighbour's) are kept; three readings per site pair with the spins as orthogonal blocks — TRIANGLE U(y) = max_j Σ_ℓ |t_{ℓ,j}|/d_j², a rigorous upper bound of the first-order maximal correlation (the norm of a sum is at most the sum of the norms, a loop's coupling has norm |t|/d²); PARTS, the entries of equal (P, Q) summed and distinct keys taken as orthogonal, the operator norm of the sparse matrix — an ESTIMATE (products in different orders are not orthogonal in general); PLAQUETTES ALONE. THE FULL FIRST ORDER AT a_c = 4a (the n = 4 FULL couplings): TRIANGLE 2.635183 (rigorous upper bound), PARTS 0.904885 (estimate), PLAQUETTES-ALONE triangle 1.156050; the nearest sites 0.178 / 0.038 / 0.096 with 38–40 distinct keys each; at a_c = 2a 28.175940 / 8.077531 / 8.620020 and at 8a 0.590357 / 0.141532 / 0.051390 (triangle / parts / plaquettes, the n = 8 couplings at their large errors). THE SAMPLER WITH THE PRODUCT FUNCTIONS (cp_mc.c, 148 functions a bundle: the 13 of each link and the 16 products of the spin-½ coordinates of each link pair; the one-loop known answer 0.4609 ± 0.0024 against 0.4645 — the split estimate's downward bias at 10⁵ measurements — and the dead instrument 0.0001 ± 0.0038): the nearest bundle pair at 4a reads 0.0339 ± 0.0031 (RESULTS 2's single-link basis 0.0230: the product functions carry the plaquette coupling, as the corrected law says), the pair at e₀ − e₁ 0.0147 ± 0.0031, at −e₁ 0.0261 ± 0.0025 — each a lower bound in expectation of its pair's maximal correlation and each within the parts estimate (0.038, 0.033, 0.038), the split estimate diluted where the signal is one entry against a 148-dimensional noise; the class-wise row (one representative per S₄-class of sites, 2 × 10⁶ sweeps each, the class size by the coordinate multiset) is running and lands as RESULTS 4. WHAT THE FIRST ORDER NOW SAYS AT 4a: the bundle row lies in [the sampled lower bound, 2.635] with the parts estimate at 0.905 — BELOW ONE, where the single-link form's first order is 1.264 and its exact quadrature 1.164: the bundle form is NOT harder than the single-link form at first order; whether it passes at 4a is what the sampled lower bound and a sharper upper bound decide. S-CP1 ([0.5, 1.3]) holds for the parts estimate and is undecided for the exact row; S-CP4 (below one at 8a) holds on every first-order reading.

The file goes into the kernel tree (Aperanton/Hull3.lean) and the proof's kernel/ once the development gate's run a ends (one build directory). **§33 ADDENDUM, NOTE 17 — (2.36) READ VERBATIM ON PAGE 19: THE BARE INEQUALITY, THE HULL OF THE ENLARGEMENT, NO HYPOTHESIS ON Z_i — NOTE 11's WRITTEN FORM CORRECTED, THE REFUTATION SHARPENED AND MADE METRIC-INDEPENDENT (2026-11-03 08:16 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat, law 18 on its own NOTE 11 after a reviewer's question this lane could not answer from the pages it had read — p. 19 is M3c's `cmp116_19.png`; nothing above edited).** THE PAGE (CMP 116 p. 19, read): "The set Z₀ determining it is a union of connected components, Z₀ = ∪ Z_i, and we denote by Z′_i the smallest localization domain from D_{k+1} containing Z̃_i"; "We extract exp(−δκd_k(Z_i)) from each exponential in (2.35), corresponding to one of these components. The remaining exponential is bounded using the following inequality: 2d_k(Z_i) ≥ Ld_{k+1}(Z′_i). (2.36) This inequality can be obtained by simple, but awkward, geometric and combinatoric considerations. It follows by considering locally many possible cases." So: (a) (2.36) is the BARE inequality 2d_k ≥ Ld_{k+1}, no additive constant; (b) Z′_i is the smallest coarse domain containing the ENLARGEMENT Z̃_i (the one-layer enlargement, as p. 20's (L + 2)⁴ cubes touching a fixed LM-cube say), as both seats had taken it from each other's words and now from the page; (c) no hypothesis on Z_i beyond being a connected component of the resummed set, a localization domain from D_k. The factors (1 − 5δ) and (1 − 6δ) that NOTE 11 wrote INTO the inequality belong to the extraction before it: the page extracts δκd_k(Z_i) and applies the bare (2.36) to the rest. CORRECTED: NOTE 11's "2(1 − 5δ)·15 ≥ (1 − 6δ)·3·15, i.e. 25 ≥ 36" is the extraction's weaker form; THE PRINTED INEQUALITY ON THE ONE-BIG-CUBE DOMAIN AT L = 3 READS 30 ≥ 45 — FALSE; and since at L = 3 the domain Z (one big cube's 81 small cubes) and its hull Z′ (the 3⁴ block of big cubes) are ONE SHAPE AT TWO SCALES, d_k(Z) = d_{k+1}(Z′) =: δ > 0 in ANY metric (two opposite corner cubes share no point, so every tree meeting both has positive length), and 2δ ≥ 3δ fails for every positive δ: THE REFUTATION NEEDS NO METRIC AND NO NUMBER. The 15 (the sixteen-corner argument on the cube-edge graph) enters only the family passes at L ≥ 5 and the kernel piece, both in the edge metric — the page's "trees formed by edges of cubes give an equivalent definition" is read as the definition the families are tested in (a Euclidean Steiner tree can be shorter: the unit square's corners, 1 + √3 against 3). THE LADDER RE-RUN (`ladder_house.awk`, record `ladder_house.2026-11-03b.txt`, LADDER-MET; the block bound max((L⁴ − 16)/16, 15(L − 2))): on the printed form the block family FAILS at L = 3 (30 against 45) and PASSES at every L ≥ 5 (90 against 75 at L = 5; 298 against 105; 818 against 135; 1828 against 165 at L = 11); the chain family under the containment reading of Z′ FAILS at L = 3 (deficit 1 at m = 3) and PASSES at every L ≥ 5, under the face-contact reading fails at L = 3, 5, 7 and passes from L = 9 — the same verdicts as the extraction's form, with the printed margins; the fifteen known answers and every root certificate unchanged. THE KERNEL PIECE RE-CHECKED (`Hull3.lean` run b): `printed_inequality_fails_at_L3` now the bare 30 ≥ 45 (no axiom), `two_delta_lt_three_delta` the metric-free form 2δ ≥ 3δ for every δ > 0 (omega; propext, Quot.sound), `extraction_form_fails_at_L3` the earlier 750 ≥ 1080 kept as the weaker form, the hull and corner enumerations as before; the stamp theorem of run a (16 − 1 = 15, which reads the same whatever the geometry — law 51) REMOVED; the sorry/axiom/import census taken on the stripped source (0), not on the header's prose. **§33 ADDENDUM, NOTE 19 — THE VACUITY THEOREM ABOVE THE BARE KERNEL: THE SPAN LEMMA FOR EVERY COORDINATE, THE INTERVAL CLAUSE ENUMERATED AT L = 11, SHARP AT n = L; THE DESK BOUND n ≤ L − 2 SHARPENED TO n ≤ L − 1 (2026-11-03 08:27 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/ladder/Vacuity.lean` and its check `Vacuity.lean.2026-11-03a.txt` landed with this note, with the ladder's run c).** THE THEOREM (Lean 4 v4.35.0, no import, no axiom, no placeholder; omega, simp and decide the proof terms; checked standalone beside the running development gate): a wall-connected ordering of n cubes (`Conn`: every cube after the first shares a wall with an earlier one — any wall-connected family has one, breadth-first from any cube) has, for every coordinate projection moving by at most one across a wall (`adj_a` .. `adj_d` from the wall relation's four cases, by omega), any two values differing by at most n − 1: `span_gen` by induction on the ordering, the four coordinates its instances (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound — the kernel's standard axioms, from the Decidable and List instances). The interval clause: the enlargement of a family spanning the cells lo .. hi is [lo − 1, hi + 2], and a big cube of index i (the cell [Li, Li + L]) meets it with positive measure iff Li < hi + 2 and Li + L > lo − 1 (`meetsL`); `vacuity_at_L11` enumerates, for every extent e ≤ 9 (n ≤ 10 cubes) and every placement lo ∈ 0 .. 10, that no index has both neighbours meeting — the cubes met are at most two and consecutive, so every one contains the shared wall's corner and the coarse hull's linear size is zero (NO AXIOM, by decide); `vacuity_sharp_at_L11`: at n = 11 = L the placement lo = 0 meets three consecutive cubes (NO AXIOM). THE DESK BOUND SHARPENED: NOTES 11 and 17 said n ≤ L − 2; between integer endpoints three consecutive big cubes need the enlargement's length n + 2 ≥ L + 2, i.e. n ≥ L, so the vacuity holds for n ≤ L − 1 — n ≤ 10 at L = 11 (the ladder's own `jproper` had it right; its printed line said L − 2 and is corrected in run c, `ladder_house.2026-11-03c.txt`, LADDER-MET). What the kernel now holds of NOTE 11's refutation and its context: the 3⁴ hull of the one-big-cube domain, the corner cubes' coordinate disjointness, 30 ≥ 45 and 2δ ≥ 3δ false (Hull3.lean), and the vacuity of every domain of at most L − 1 cubes at L = 11 (Vacuity.lean); both files go into the kernel tree and the proof's kernel/ when the development gate's run a ends.

**§35 RESULTS 5 — THE RIGOROUS CEILING OF THE BUNDLE ROW, UNIFORM IN THE ENVIRONMENT: 10.77 BY BIRKHOFF–HOPF; RESULTS 4's "DOES NOT FIRE" CORRECTED TO "IS NOT SHOWN TO FIRE" — THE ORDERED ROW LIES IN [0.85, 10.77] AND WHETHER IT EXCEEDS ONE IS UNDECIDED (2026-11-03 11:01 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_row3.c` and its record `cp_row3.2026-11-03a.txt` landed with this note).** THE CORRECTION FIRST (law 18; the side note the operator forwarded at 10:4x read it right): RESULTS 4's sampled row is a LOWER BOUND in expectation (the split estimate's bias is downward, its basis 148 functions a bundle), so a figure below one cannot say the kill-switch does not fire — the exact row at ξ₀ may lie above one; "does not fire" reads "is not shown to fire", and the ordered-environment question is UNDECIDED until an upper bound below one or a lower bound above one exists. RESULTS 3's TRIANGLE 2.635 bounds the FIRST-ORDER maximal correlation, not the exact row; the parts estimate 0.905 is an estimate. THE CEILING (cp_row3.c; AD's Lemma B, W = 0 — Birkhoff 1957, Hopf 1963; the formula AD's row instrument carries as its R_birk column): for p(u, v) = a(u)b(v)e^{h(u,v)}/Z on compact spaces the maximal correlation is at most tanh(Δ/4), Δ the supremum of the cross-ratio h(u,v) + h(u′,v′) − h(u,v′) − h(u′,v) — the two conditional kernels have projective diameter at most Δ, a and b cancel from every cross-ratio, each contracts Hilbert's metric by tanh(Δ/4), and ρ² = λ₂(PP*) is at most the contraction squared; for two bundles at ANY environment h is the sum over the loops SHARED by the two bundles (every other loop a function of one bundle or of the environment alone), so Δ(0, y) ≤ Σ_shared (4|t_½| + (8/3)|t_1| + 4|t_{3/2}|) — the ranges 2, 4/3, 2 of the normalised characters doubled — and R(0, y) = ess sup_ξ ρ ≤ tanh(Δ(0, y)/4) entry by entry, environment-free. THE RUN (n = 4 FULL): the eight nearest sites share 75 loops each with the origin, Δ = 3.198520, tanh(Δ/4) = 0.663830; 88 sites share a loop; CEILING 10.767723; the single-link form of the same bound 5.589012 over 122 neighbour links; the plaquette-only action (t = 0.13082): the nearest bundle site Δ = 1.569840 (three shared plaquettes), ceiling 4.548778, single-link 2.341418 = 18 × tanh(0.13082), and the one-loop pair's 0.13008 against the exact I₂/I₁ = 0.032682 — loose by 3.98, the factor AD measured at the free environment; the dead instrument 0 on both forms. WHAT THE TWO SIDES SAY: 0.851 ± 0.035 ≤ R₀(ξ₀) ≤ 10.77 — the ceiling is four times loose on every loop by its construction (it bounds a one-loop pair by tanh(t) where the exact is ≈ t/4), so it cannot reach one on this action and cannot decide; the sampled figure cannot either. THE DECIDING INSTRUMENT, NAMED WITH ITS DESIGN: the second singular value of the exact pair kernel P: L²(V(y)) → L²(V(0)), g ↦ E[g | V(0)], at ξ₀ — the cross-covariance in the product basis of the normalised matrix elements of the eight links to spin 3/2 (30 functions a link), the first-order law's selection rule making the matrix sparse by the shared-loop structure, each entry an exact Haar integral of a polynomial in the matrix elements (the fourth stone's integrator, cei_exact_link.awk, does such integrals exactly), the truncation error bounded by the couplings' size through the same cross-ratio supremum; a certified σ₂ below the sampled figure convicts the sampler, one above one fires the kill-switch in the bundle form, one between settles the ordered question. Until it runs the row's status is OPEN, not PASSES, and THE PROOF's Step 35 says so (its status line amended). THE TYPICAL-ENVIRONMENT ROW (S-CP3) is running: cp_env.c's third snapshot `env6_2.txt` of the 6⁴ coarse torus under the fitted action (4000 sweeps, acceptance 0.950, loop averages at the fit's data — plaq_½ 0.032699 against the fit's 0.032868; record `cp_env.2026-11-03a.txt`), `cp_mc_env_row.sh` over the 88 loop-sharing sites at origin 0 (5 × 10⁵ sweeps at the twenty dominant sites, 10⁵ at the rest; record `cp_mc_env_row.2026-11-03a.txt`), landing as RESULTS 6 with the same reading: a lower bound at one environment.

III; CMP 109 p. 257's definitions, Z′ the big cubes meeting Z̃ in positive measure): d_{k+1}(Z′) ≤ ⌊d_k(Z)/(L − 2)⌋, so L·d_{k+1}(Z′) ≤ (L/(L − 2))·d_k(Z) ≤ 2d_k(Z) at every L ≥ 4 — 11/9 at L = 11; the L = 3 block ATTAINS the bound (15 = 15 in d = 4: NOTE 11's 30 ≥ 45 is the equality's failure by exactly 3/2). **§35 RESULTS 6 — THE EXACT FIRST-ORDER BUNDLE ROW AT a_c = 4a IS 1.097, ABOVE ONE: THE PARTS ESTIMATE's 0.905 WAS WRONG IN ITS ORTHOGONALITY, THE LAW's OWN BASIS IS HAAR-ORTHOGONAL AND NEEDS NO GRAM MATRIX; AT 2a 10.01, AT 8a 0.229 (2026-11-03 11:41 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_gram.c` and its records `cp_gram.2026-11-03a.txt` (the first draft's known answer failing) and `cp_gram.2026-11-03b.txt` (the run of record) landed with this note).** THE INSTRUMENT: the first-order law's own terms are the basis — for spin ½ the products of one quaternion coordinate of each of a loop's links in a bundle, which are Haar-ORTHOGONAL across distinct link sets and coordinate choices (E₀[(q)_c(q)_{c′}] = δ/4, the links independent) with norm 2^{−p}; for spin 1 the products of adjoint matrix elements R_{ij}(q), orthogonal by Schur (E₀[R_{ij}R_{kl}] = δδ/3) with norm 3^{−p/2}; the spins orthogonal blocks (Schur on each link). So, per site pair and per spin, the first-order correlation operator in that orthonormal basis is the matrix M_j[α, β] = Σ_{ℓ, terms} t_{ℓ,j}·s(term)·(E[elem²])^{(p+q)/2}/d_j — spin ½: t·s/2^{p+q} with s ∈ {0, ±1} the real part of the quaternion basis product e_{c₁}⋯e_{c_m} times the conjugation signs; spin 1: (t/3)·3^{−(p+q)/2} on the index chains — and the exact first-order maximal correlation ρ₁(0, y) = max_j σ_max(M_j): exact rationals in the couplings, one singular value, no estimate, no Gram matrix. The spin-3/2 block by its triangle Σ|t|/16 (the smallest couplings; below the ½ block everywhere). WHAT THE PARTS ESTIMATE GOT WRONG (RESULTS 3's 0.905): it took V_aV_b and V_bV_a as distinct orthogonal keys — the real part w(q_aq_b) = w(q_bq_a) is ONE function — and V_aV_b against V_aV_b^{−1} as orthogonal — they share the monomials w_aw_b, x_ax_b, … with signs; the monomials say exactly what is the same and what is orthogonal. THE KNOWN ANSWERS (record b): one plaquette between a bundle pair (p = 2, q = 1): the 16 × 4 block of ±t/8 has four equal singular values t/4 = 0.032705000 exactly (cp_row2.c's isolated t/4; the first draft read t/4·√2 = 0.046252 from an entry written t·s/(2·2^{(p+q)/2}) — the known answer convicted it, law 18, corrected and dated in the source, record a kept); the plaquette-only bundle row 0.654100 = 20·t/4 (RESULTS 2's desk number) and the single-link plaquette-only row 0.588690 = 18·t/4; the dead instrument 0 on both forms; the single-link form at the n = 4 FULL couplings 1.256025 (spin ½ alone 1.254795) — cp_row.c's record b (`cp_row.2026-11-03b.txt`, its per-pair 13 × 13 Jacobi, a road sharing no line with the key matrix here) reads the single-link row ORDERED 1.256025 TO EVERY DIGIT, the triangle 1.264305 above both as it must be. THE RUN at a_c = 4a (n = 4 FULL): THE EXACT FIRST-ORDER BUNDLE ROW IS 1.097064707 [16:24: 1.095166929 with the spin-3/2 block exact — §35 RESULTS 9 ADDENDUM] — the spin-½ block alone 1.094438040, the spin-1 block 0.031588 (never the max at any site), the 3/2 triangle 0.052950 — the eight nearest sites 0.048338011 each (keys 112 × 64; the triangle there 0.178245, the parts estimate had 0.038, the sampler 0.052/0.053), the twelve sites e_μ − e_ν 0.043324725 (29 shared loops), the twelve e_μ + e_ν 0.005623755, the eight 2e_μ 0.000375, the remaining 48 sites together 0.12; at a_c = 2a 10.010598 (spin ½ 10.0106), at 8a 0.228693 (the n = 8 couplings at their large errors). THE LADDER AT 4a NOW READS: triangle 2.635 (rigorous first-order ceiling) ≥ EXACT FIRST ORDER 1.097 ≥ the estimate's 0.905 (wrong, retired); and beside it the sampled EXACT-law row 0.851 ± 0.035 (a lower bound; its 148-function basis is exactly the spin-½ keys of this instrument plus the spin-1 singles, so the first-order optimal functions are in its reach). WHAT IT SAYS: at FIRST ORDER the bundle form does NOT pass at the two-step scale — the row is above one by ten per cent where the single-link first order is 1.256 (the bundle form thirteen per cent below the single-link form, still above one); for the EXACT law, the first order overshoots the exact single-link row by eight per cent (1.256 against the quadrature's 1.164), so the exact bundle row at ξ₀ is expected NEAR ONE and is decided by neither the sampled lower bound nor the first order — the status of RESULTS 5 stands (OPEN, in [0.85, 10.77] rigorously, near one by the readings), with the deciding instrument unchanged: the exact pair kernel's second singular value. THE PROOF's Step 35 carries the exact first-order row and its basis in place of the estimate (f-commit named in NOTE 25). S-CP1 ([0.5, 1.3] for the first-order bundle row at 4a) HELD on the exact first order (1.097); S-CP4 (below one at 8a) HELD (0.229).

**§35 RESULTS 7 — THE BUNDLE ROW AT A GIBBS-TYPICAL ENVIRONMENT: 0.300 ± 0.041, A LOWER BOUND AT ONE ENVIRONMENT, ONE THIRD OF THE ORDERED ROW; RESULTS 5's DECIDING INSTRUMENT WITHDRAWN DATED (ITS ARITHMETIC WAS A PROMISE) AND REPLACED BY THE FIXED-FUNCTION ROW (2026-11-03 11:41 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; the record `cp_mc_env_row.2026-11-03a.txt` landed with this note).** THE RUN (S-CP3): the snapshot `env6_2.txt` (the 6⁴ coarse torus under the fitted n = 4 action, 4000 sweeps), origin 0, every one of the 88 sites sharing a loop with the origin's bundle sampled with the pair's links the variables and every other link the snapshot's (the loops' environment links folded into one constant between consecutive pair links): the twenty dominant sites (5 × 10⁵ sweeps each) sum to 0.239917 ± 0.010319 — the eight nearest reading 0.0104–0.0186 against the ordered environment's class 0.0530/0.0522, the twelve e_μ − e_ν 0.0055–0.0192 against 0.0365; the 68 others (10⁵ sweeps) 0.059879 ± 0.039752, three of them above two standard errors, what noise gives; THE ROW 0.299796 ± 0.041070 — A LOWER BOUND IN EXPECTATION AT ONE ENVIRONMENT (the split estimate's downward bias as in RESULTS 4), one third of the ordered row's 0.851. WHAT IT SAYS AND WHAT IT DOES NOT: at a typical environment the constants between the pair's links carry each loop's coupling into different functions of the pair, and the reading shows it as a row three times smaller — a description of this reading, not a lemma; it supports the ordered environment being the hard case for the essential supremum and proves nothing about the supremum, which is the hypothesis; the 58 non-sharing classes are zero at every environment by the pair law's factorisation. RESULTS 5's DECIDING INSTRUMENT, WITHDRAWN DATED: RESULTS 5 wrote the exact pair kernel's second singular value "in the product basis of matrix elements, each entry an exact Haar integral of a polynomial (the fourth stone's integrator does such integrals)" — under the exact law the weight is e^{S}, not a polynomial; making it one needs a certified character-expansion truncation on every loop TOUCHING the pair's eight links (hundreds, not the 75 shared), whose monomial count no integrator here reaches: as written it was a promise dressed as a design, and it is withdrawn (law 44's candidate for attack, attacked: the obstruction is the weight). THE DECIDER THAT IS BUILDABLE, built next: THE FIXED-FUNCTION ROW — for each site pair the optimal pair (f*, g*) of the exact first-order operator (the top singular vectors of RESULTS 6's spin-½ matrices, chosen before any sample is drawn), evaluated on the exact law's samples at ξ₀ and read as E[f*g*]/√(E[f*²]E[g*²]) with its blocks: a LOWER BOUND of the exact maximal correlation with statistical error only and none of the split estimate's bias; a row at or above one by several standard errors fires the kill-switch in the bundle form as a reading, as the single-link reading fired it by quadrature; the in-sample 148-basis estimate (biased upward by its choice on the sample) run beside it brackets the exact row. An upper bound below one would need the exact kernel's second singular value, which no instrument here computes; the ordered question stays OPEN until the fixed-function row reads. TWO QUALIFICATIONS, DATED: (i) "the first order overshoots the exact by eight per cent" (RESULTS 6; THE PROOF Step 35) is ONE case, the single-link row, not a bound — the sampled nearest class 0.0530 sits ABOVE its exact first-order 0.0483 while e_μ − e_ν's 0.0365 sits BELOW its 0.0433, so the second-order term has no fixed sign per site and the exact row may lie above 1.097 as well as below; (ii) NOTE 24's "in Lemma RD's chain the option costs nothing" is two readers' reading of pp. 20–21, the standing column keeping Bałaban's λ = L/2 — THE PROOF's Step 29 now says so, and its Step 35 carries the fixed-function decider in place of the withdrawn one.

THE LADDER AT THE ORDERED DATUM, plaquette ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ / connected cross-face correlation, CONSTRAINED (the seven coarse edges fixed at the identity by substitution, §31.12's) against UNCONSTRAINED (the edges free): W2 direct 0.681245 / 2.813e-4 against 0.639123 / 1.489e-3; W3 direct 0.746505 / 5.934e-4 against 0.688355 / 2.436e-3; W2 squared 0.621608 / 9.571e-4 against 0.556272 / 2.908e-3; W4 squared 0.762384 / 7.063e-4 against 0.675552 / 2.659e-3; the Gaussian face fibre 0.759185 / 7.369e-4. WHAT IT SAYS: the Gaussian face fibre's agreement in §31.12 (0.4 % on the plaquette, 4 % on the connected correlation at W4) is an agreement with the CONSTRAINED object and with it alone — the unconstrained one sits 12 % below the Gaussian on the plaquette and 3.6× above it on the correlation; the constrained fibre is the law of the fine links GIVEN the coarse edges, which is the object Hypothesis II's fibre is (the conditional law), so the comparison §31.12 made is the right one and its sign is now explained: the free object averages over coarse-edge fluctuations the fibre does not see.
