**§35 RESULTS 9, THE STAKES — THE ESSENTIAL SUPREMUM ATTACKED BY A SEARCH: THE WORST ENVIRONMENT OF A BUNDLE PAIR AT FIRST ORDER, EXACT PER ENVIRONMENT, WRITTEN BEFORE THE SEARCHES END (2026-11-03 15:15 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; the instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_worst.c` landed with this note; the six searches launched at 15:14, their records `cp_worst_{4a,8a}_{1000,-1000,1-100}.2026-11-03a.txt` to follow).** THE CLAUSE: every row of RESULTS 1–8 is a reading at ONE environment — the ordered one, or one Gibbs-typical snapshot — while the hypothesis's entry is R(Y, Y′) = ess sup_ξ ρ(Y, Y′ | ξ); the one uniform bound in hand (Birkhoff–Hopf, RESULTS 5 and NOTE 27) is above one at every scale. The cheapest honest instrument for a supremum is a SEARCH, whose finding is a lower bound of it and can only raise the row. THE INSTRUMENT (cp_worst.c): the first-order spin-½ operator of a pair at an ARBITRARY environment — each shared loop traversed in order with its environment links between consecutive pair links folded into quaternion constants C₀, …, C_m (the trailing one folded into C₀ by cyclicity, as the sampler's environment mode does), the coefficient of each coordinate choice the real part of the NUMERIC product C₀ẽ_{c₁}C₁ẽ_{c₂}⋯, the monomials and their norms 2^{−p}, 2^{−q} the same Haar-orthogonal basis as RESULTS 6 (Haar's orthogonality is environment-free) — so ρ₁(0, y | ξ) = σ_max(M_ξ) exact at every ξ; THE KNOWN ANSWERS MET: at the identity environment the operator is cp_gram's (0.048338011 at the nearest pair, to the digit); a random gauge transformation at every environment site of the box with the pair's two sites fixed leaves it unchanged (difference 0.0e+00 — the constants are gauge-invariant products); the dead action reads 0. THE SEARCH: the 85 environment links of the nearest pair's 75 shared loops (the variables), a coordinate ascent — each link proposed a random rotation of angle ≤ 0.5 or, with probability 1/8, a Haar-random element, kept if σ_max rises — from the identity environment and from six Haar-random environments, 30 sweeps each, stopping when a sweep accepts nothing; the best over all starts a lower bound of sup_ξ ρ₁(0, y | ξ). THE PILOT (6 sweeps, 2 restarts, the nearest pair at 4a): the Haar-random environments read 0.0359 and 0.0360 (the mean of two), the ascent from the identity 0.048338 → 0.048589 (+0.5 %), the ascents from the two random starts 0.0354 → 0.0464 and 0.0367 → 0.0450 — BELOW the identity. THE STAKES, written before the six searches end: S-CP5 (THE ALIGNMENT CONJECTURE at first order, 4a): for the nearest pair and the pair e_μ − e_ν the best environment found lies within 3 % of the identity environment's value — the ordered environment is the hard case to that precision — and no Haar start overtakes the identity start; S-CP6: the same at 8a; S-CP7 (the refutation that would matter): a best environment above the identity's by more than 10 % at either scale, which would raise the first-order ess-sup row above the ordered row by that fraction (at 8a from 0.229 toward 0.25; at 4a from 1.097, already failed) and would make the ordered-environment rows UNDERESTIMATES of the hypothesis's object by a measured factor; S-CP8: the Haar-random environments' mean lies at 0.7–0.8 of the identity value at 4a (the pilot's 0.036 / 0.048 = 0.74), the typical row's fraction (RESULTS 7's 0.30 / 0.85 by the split estimate, 0.30 / 1.18 by the fixed one). WHAT A HOLD WOULD MEAN: the ess sup at first order is the ordered environment's value within the search's reach — evidence, never a lemma, for the ALIGNMENT LEMMA (ρ(0, y | ξ) ≤ ρ(0, y | ξ₀) for every ξ) whose proof would reduce Hypothesis I′'s supremum to the ordered row; WHAT A REFUTATION WOULD MEAN: the supremum exceeds every row read today and the exit scale moves outward. The exact-law lower bound at the best environment is cp_mc --env's business on the snapshot each search writes (`worst_{4a,8a}_<y>.env`).

THE READING FOR THE HYPOTHESIS: Hypothesis II′ at the block scale is an essential supremum over the exterior of the fibre's one-step contraction, and the face fibre's connected two-point function is the object it bounds; between the three data read — the ordered (the fibre's own fixed point), the cancelling and the Haar-random — the random exterior gives the largest |connected| by a factor five over the ordered at W4 and of the other sign, so the ordered exterior is NOT the hard case of the face fibre's correlation, and the uniform bound the hypothesis needs is not the ordered value: the Gibbs exterior at β = 2.2 lies between the ordered and the random, and the measure-weighted datum (the exterior integrated against its own measure, named in §31.12 and not built) is what decides where. WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY: nothing about the contraction itself (a two-point function at one source pair is not a Dobrushin entry), nothing uniform (one random datum, seed 1; `random:2` and `random:3` read only free, at W4 squared: 0.414767 / 2.143e-3 and 0.457955 / −3.448e-3), and the constrained W4 direct weight at the random exterior is unread (its ordered twin took ten hours; the squared weight's 2.4 h is the reason the squared rung was read first). **§31.12 ADDENDUM IV — THE FACE FIBRE UNDER THE FULL WILSON WEIGHT, READ BY A HEAT BATH (A MONTE CARLO READING WITH A STATISTICAL ERROR; THE ENGINE'S LADDERS ARE THE EXACT ARITHMETIC OF THE TRUNCATED WEIGHT), AND THE MEASURE-WEIGHTED DATUM BUILT: THE ORDERED EXTERIOR IS THE SMALL CASE OF THE FIBRE'S CONNECTED CORRELATION, GIBBS EXTERIORS READ AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE LARGER (2026-11-03 17:40 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; the instruments `STDLIB/build/aperanton/cei/cei_fibre_hb.c` and `cei_gibbs_datum.c`, the records `fibre_hb.2026-11-03{a,b,c,d,e,f}.txt`, `gibbs_datum.2026-11-03{a,b,c}.txt`, the data `datum_gibbs_{1..8}.lst`, `datum_gibbsN_{1..64}.lst`, `datum_gibbsconj_1.lst`, `datum_subst_template.lst`, `datum_subst_random1.lst`, the engine's runs `subst_W2_gibbs{1..8}.txt`, `subst_W2_gibbsconj1.txt`, `subst_W3_gibbs{1..8}.txt`, `subst_W3_gibbsconj1.txt`, `geom_subst_W2.txt`, landed with this note).** THE WORD FIRST: the instrument's source header and the records of 16:5x–17:3x say "exact" of the heat bath's numbers, meaning the untruncated weight; a sampled number with a statistical error is a reading, and "exact" on this page has meant the engine's arithmetic on the truncated weight — the records are kept as written, the word is withdrawn here, dated, and every number below from the heat bath is a MONTE CARLO READING with its statistical error (the operator's correction, 17:4x). THE DISTRIBUTION READ WIDER (`gibbs_datum.2026-11-03c.txt`, `fibre_hb.2026-11-03d.txt`: sixty-four Gibbs exteriors, seed 11, each read at 4 × 10⁶ sweeps): the connected correlation's mean +3.1e-3, its rms spread 6.2e-3, its extremes −1.18e-2 and +2.24e-2 (thirty-one times the ordered value), positive at 49 of 64, |connected| above the ordered 7.2e-4 at 55 of 64, its median 2.7e-3, its upper quartile 7.1e-3, its ninetieth percentile 1.19e-2; the fibre's plaquettes average 0.515 and 0.501 there, below the bulk 0.569 because the seven coarse edges are fixed at the identity, a coarse datum a Gibbs configuration does not take. THE FREE OBJECT (no coarse edge fixed, all 22 links free; the engine's `CEI_DROPLOOPS=1` object; `fibre_hb.2026-11-03c.txt`): the ordered datum reads 0.702294 ± 0.000096 / 0.702561 ± 0.000111 / 2.672e-3 ± 2.4e-5 (the engine's direct W4 0.699994 / 2.647e-3, 0.33 % and 0.9 % below), so THE CONSTRAINT'S BITE — the constrained over the free connected correlation — reads 0.270 at the ordered exterior against the Gaussian 0.243 and the W3 rung's 0.244; the random:1 exterior 0.423862 / 0.539488 / +1.795e-3 (the engine's W3 direct +1.955e-3 and W4 squared +1.425e-3 around it); the Gibbs datum 1 0.593787 / 0.585035 / +7.043e-3 — where the constrained fibre reads 1.469e-2, TWICE the free one's: at a Gibbs exterior the constraint RAISES the connected correlation it cuts to a quarter at the ordered exterior, the bite 2.09 there against 0.27 here. WHAT IT SAYS: the face fibre's connected two-point function at Gibbs-typical exteriors is an order of magnitude larger than at the ordered exterior, of either sign, and above the ordered value at seven exteriors of every eight — the ordered exterior, the fibre's own fixed point, is where the fibre is MOST decoupled, and the readings §31.12 took there (the ladders, the Gaussian comparison, the "constraint is not a small perturbation") are readings of the easy case; Hypothesis II′'s essential supremum over the exterior lies above the typical set's values, which are already ten to thirty times the ordered one at this object, and the random exterior is not the extreme either (its −3.7e-3 is a sixth of the largest Gibbs value). The measure-weighted datum named in §31.12 is therefore not a mean to take but a distribution to read: its support's upper tail is what the hypothesis bounds, and on sixty-four draws that tail reaches 2.2e-2. WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY: nothing about the contraction itself (a two-point function at one source pair is not a Dobrushin entry; the fibre of a 22-link object is the reading, the block-scale operator the question), nothing beyond this object (n = 2, one coarse pair of faces; the fibre at the exit scale is a different object), nothing exact (every heat-bath number is a Monte Carlo reading with its statistical error, and the sixty-four exteriors are sixty-four — the tail is read by more). THE PROOF's Step 34 carries the readings as readings.

**§35 RESULTS 10 — THE EXIT CRITERION IS THE SURFACE GAS, NOT THE SINGLE-SITE ROW: ON THE FITTED TWO-STEP-BLOCKED ACTION THE PLAQUETTE COUPLING LIES INSIDE THE CERTIFIED RADIUS BY 3.3×, THE LONG LOOPS ARE FACES OF A MIXED GAS, AND ENTERED AS FACES THEY RAISE THE CERTIFICATE'S SUM THROUGH A LINK TEN-THOUSAND-FOLD, TO A THIRD OF ITS ALLOWANCE AT FIVE FACES — THE EXIT ON THE FITTED ACTION UNDECIDED, EVERY NUMBER A READING OF THE FIT; THE EXIT CLOSES ONLY BY THE FLOW BOUND AND A MIXED CENSUS BOUND (2026-11-03 19:55 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; the instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_mixgas.c` and its records `cp_mixgas.2026-11-03a.txt` (the known answers, the stopped run) and `cp_mixgas.2026-11-03b.txt` (the reading), the solver-corrected record of record `cp_gram.2026-11-03i.txt`, landed with this note).** FIRST, WHAT THIS IS NOT (R1, 18:2x): every coupling here is the fit's n = 4 FULL line — a twelve-term reading of a blocked action that has infinitely many terms, whose long loops already carry 70 % of the plaquettes' activity and whose omitted terms nothing here bounds — so every number below is a reading of one point of the fit's error region, never a bound and never a step; the exit is certified only by R2's road, the blocked action bounded along the flow and the certificate run on the bound; "inside the radius by 3.3×" is a promising reading, and the gap is not closed by it. THE REFRAMING, A CORRECTION OF THIS SECTION'S OWN FRAME: RESULTS 1–9 priced Hypothesis I's single-site Dobrushin row at the blocked actions and named its failure at 2a and 4a "the reach–contraction gap"; but the exit's criterion in THE GROUND's own architecture is Theorem 8.9's — the surface-gas Kotecký–Preiss certificate (`aperanton_kp_cert.awk`, S1 §14), whose clustering is the input Theorem 7.1 takes (the plan's D1–D2) — and §31.12 names the exit as "the coefficient-supplied certificate handed the blocked coefficients". The single-site row and the certificate are two sufficient conditions of very different strength: for the Wilson action itself the row passes one near β ≈ 0.12 (the fitted 4a row 1.095 at plaquette coupling 0.128 IS that failure, read on the blocked action) while the certificate passes to β* = 0.4260 (the page dressing; 0.4764 with the local theorem); so the row's failure at 4a is a failure of the criterion, not of the reach, and TASKS Step 3 is restated accordingly (dad19f6b1). THE NUMBERS ON THE FIT'S LINE: the plaquette coupling t_{plaq,½} multiplies ½ tr U_p, the certificate's own β normalization; it is 0.95778 at a_c = 2a — outside the radius — and 0.12845 at 4a — inside by 3.3×; the certificate in one-point mode at β = 0.12845 (page, γ = 6, F0 = 20, α = 0.002) reads κ_U = 0.004311, u = a_½ = 0.03209044, ũ = 0.03222909, r = 0.1637 and an LHS of order 10⁻⁸ against α = 0.002 — the plaquette gas at the two-step coupling passes by four orders of magnitude. THE LONG LOOPS ARE FACES OF A SECOND KIND: the fit's rectangles (−0.00046), chairs (0.00565) and parallelograms (0.00554) at spin ½ are six-link loops whose character expansions add faces to the gas; their per-link activity in the certificate's normalization (a_½(t) = I₂(t)/I₁(t): 18 × 0.000115 + 72 × 0.001412 + 24 × 0.001385 = 0.137) is 70 % of the plaquettes' (6 × 0.03209 = 0.193) — not a perturbation — so the exit at 4a on the fitted action is the MIXED gas's question, and the plaquette-only certificate's four orders of margin are not its answer. THE INSTRUMENT: `cp_mixgas.c` reads the mixed gas's leaf sum through a link in the certificate's own form — the closing tree (the first face through the root link, then at every step a face through the SMALLEST odd link, every closed connected face set reached [CORRECTED 2026-11-05 10:05, ADDENDUM VI-n: every Z₂-minimal closed set is reached; of the closed sets holding a proper closed subset the search, which returns at the first closure, reaches only those on which the rule does not close early, and that population is charged by the tree's load, the 4 of α̃ = 4α], the closing-tree lemma of S1 §3 being face-shape independent), the weight Π_f |a_½(t_f)| e^κ e^{2α̃|L(f)|/4} with α̃ = 4α and κ the certificate's own dressing at this β applied to every face, 4σ(S) per set with 2^χ ≤ 4 ASSUMED for mixed faces (flagged: the pure-pairing lemma under proof on the S1 page tonight is for plaquette faces), the pruned branches' mass bounded by the weighted tree with W = Σ over the 120 faces through a link of w_f, and refusal by name at W ≥ 1; double arithmetic, so a reading beside the certificate's intervals. Known answers: plaquettes only, the closed sets through a link 12 at F = 6 and 180 at F = 10 — three times the certificate's per-site N(6) = 4 and N(10) = 60, its factor 3 being exactly the per-link conversion (the first build wiped the odd-link list after the first face and counted a lone plaquette as closed; this KAT caught it, 18:51); the dead action 0; the one-step action (0.958) REFUSED, W ≥ 1. THE READING AT 4a (record b: the fit's spin-½ couplings, κ = 0.004311 held at every α, threshold 10⁻¹¹, depth 8 — every closed set with one hexagon and at most five plaquettes, two hexagons and at most three, three hexagons and one plaquette, or at most seven plaquettes alone is reached; 43,851,656 tree nodes, 12,663 closed sets through the link, seven minutes): the closed sets by face count and hexagon count with their weighted sums at α = 0.002 — F = 3: 105 sets with one hexagon, 5.343·10⁻⁴ (the 84 chairs and 21 rectangles each with the two plaquettes it bounds, the desk's count met exactly), 192 with two, 5.21·10⁻⁵, 324 with three, 2.76·10⁻⁶; F = 4: 72 with one hexagon, 1.44·10⁻⁵ (the parallelograms with their three-plaquette staircases), 3,186 with two, 2.00·10⁻⁵, 4,656 with three, 1.83·10⁻⁶; F = 5: 132 with one, 8.8·10⁻⁷, 3,984 with two, 1.16·10⁻⁶; F = 6: the 12 cubes, 5.9·10⁻⁸, the plaquette gas's whole leaf sum at this depth. THE LEAF SUM 6.275·10⁻⁴ at α = 0.002 (6.095·10⁻⁴ at 0.001, 6.650·10⁻⁴ at 0.004) — A LOWER BOUND on the certificate's sum through a link, every enumerated set being a polymer of the gas — is 31 % of α = 0.002 and 61 % of α = 0.001, where the plaquette gas alone reads 6·10⁻⁸: THE LONG LOOPS RAISE THE CERTIFICATE'S SUM TEN-THOUSAND-FOLD, 85 % of it the three-face sets, and the sum is linear in the chair coupling, so at 3.2 × the fitted 0.00565 the three-face sets alone would exceed α — the fit's own box on that coupling is 0.00033, so the reading is not the box's. The instrument's upper bound, the weighted closing tree's pruned mass over 1 − W, 3.4·10⁻³, exceeds α at this threshold and at every feasible one — the 96 hexagon faces through a link multiply the tree's count sixteen-fold a level while dividing the weight twenty-three-fold, so the hexagon-rich levels carry a mass 0.139^k that no weight threshold removes (four hexagons weigh 4.4·10⁻¹² each and 3.7·10⁻⁴ together); the run at threshold 10⁻¹⁴ and depth 12 was stopped after 1,460 CPU seconds, record a, some ten hours from its end and unable to PASS. WHAT IT SAYS: on the fitted two-step-blocked action the exit's own criterion is UNDECIDED by this instrument — not passed, as the plaquette-only certificate read by four orders, and not failed: "inside the radius by 3.3×" is a statement about the plaquette gas, and with the fitted long loops as faces the margin at five faces is a factor of three, before any tail; the single-site row's failure there was never the exit's failure, and the exit's own criterion is open there. WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY: nothing about the blocked action beyond the fit's twelve terms (R1); nothing of the fit's higher-spin faces (the spin-3/2 plaquette term 0.00482 ± 0.00187 and the spin-1 loop terms are face classes the enumerator does not carry); nothing certified (double arithmetic; 2^χ ≤ 4 for the mixed faces assumed — derivable, the sheet identity being face-shape independent for disc faces; the dressing's walls and pieces the plaquette gas's); nothing at 2a (outside) or 8a (the fit there undetermined). THE TWO OBJECTS THAT CLOSE THE EXIT, NAMED WITH THEIR SHAPES: (1) THE FLOW BOUND — the two-step-blocked weight's character coefficients bounded above along the flow from β = 2.2, every loop family and the omitted tail together, the UV half's deliverable at the exit scale (Step 2 and Step 4 of the six steps); (2) THE MIXED CERTIFICATE — `aperanton_kp_cert.awk`'s interval arithmetic extended to the long-loop faces, with a census bound sharper than the weighted tree: the closed mixed surfaces counted by (plaquettes, hexagons) through a theorem (each hexagon the boundary of a two- or three-plaquette disc, so a mixed closed surface maps to a plaquette surface with multiplicities — the branched-patch count the Theorem 8.9 plan names as B4), since a weighted tree cannot certify a gas whose faces are many and light; run on the bound of (1). Neither is a reading; both are the work. THE PROOF's Step 35 carries the reframing as a correction of the criterion and the numbers as readings.

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM — THE EXACT SIX-FACE CENSUS: ON THE FITTED TWO-STEP-BLOCKED ACTION THE MIXED SURFACE GAS PASSES THE CERTIFICATE THROUGH A LINK BY A FACTOR 1.9 [CORRECTED 21:29, RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM II: AT α = 0.002 IT FAILS, RATIO 1.56 — the merge read each root range's tail one character late and dropped its leading digit; IT PASSES AT α ≈ 0.015 BY A FACTOR 1.6 on the closing tree's antichain tail, about seven per cent in the face activities] — RESULTS 10'S "UNDECIDED" DECIDED, AS A READING OF THE FIT (2026-11-03 21:02 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_mixgas.c` in its exact mode, `cp_mixgas_lhs.awk` the second road to its weighted sums, the records `cp_mixgas.2026-11-03d.txt` (the census) and `cp_mixgas.2026-11-03e.txt` (the corner), landed with this note).** RESULTS 10 left the leaf sum to five faces at a third of α and the weighted tree's threshold tail above α, and named the next instrument: the exact closed-set census with the closing tree's tail. BUILT AND RUN. THE INSTRUMENT: no weight threshold; THE CLOSABILITY PRUNE — a node whose odd links need more faces than its budget (more than six links a face; a two-link boundary needing two faces; one face left closing only a 4- or 6-link boundary; odd links whose base sites lie pairwise three apart in max norm needing a face each, no face spanning more than two) sends its subtree's mass beyond the cap to the tail at W^(r+1)/(1 − W), every Z2-minimal closed set of more than six faces lying under a cut node whose subtree bound covers it; THE CANONICAL ROOT — a leaf counted only from the smallest of its faces through the root link, so the 120 root ranges are disjoint and the census runs in parallel (24 ranges of five, the five plaquette roots singly: their range refused by name at 2,097,153 sets, its record table half full). A DEFECT FOUND AND REPAIRED ON THE WAY: `unsigned long` is 32 bits on this host's compiler, so the instrument's link hash shifted to zero and every probe walked from slot 0 — records a–c are correct and were slow (the known answer of record a in 256 s, now 1 s). KNOWN ANSWERS: the plaquette census exact to ten faces, 12 and 180 closed sets through a link (the certificate's per-site 4 and 60, three times); record b's nine classes at its own threshold to the digit; the exact census to five faces in ONE process (429,822 leaves, 6.283994·10⁻⁴) equal to the merged parts' five-face part — the root decomposition read two ways; the census polynomial of `cp_mixgas_lhs.awk` equal to the instrument's weighted sum to the digit. THE CENSUS: 12,377,661 Z2-minimal closed sets of plaquettes, rectangles, chairs and parallelograms through a link with at most six faces — 621 of three faces, 15,696 of four, 413,505 of five, 11,947,839 of six, over 75 compositions (the coupling-independent table, in record d: the mixed certificate's left side is a polynomial in the four face weights at ANY couplings); the six-hexagon sets alone 3,381,028. THE READING at the fit's central line (α = 0.002, κ = 0.004311 per face): the leaf sum to six faces 6.2878·10⁻⁴ (by face count 5.891·10⁻⁴, 3.643·10⁻⁵, 2.876·10⁻⁶, 3.80·10⁻⁷ — the series falls by about 0.07 a face), the measured tail beyond six faces 9.743·10⁻⁵ [CORRECTED 21:29: 5.924·10⁻⁴] (the global antichain bound W⁷/(1 − W) = 7.52·10⁻⁴ its cruder form), so LHS = 6.288·10⁻⁴ + 4.2 × 9.743·10⁻⁵ = 1.038·10⁻³ against α = 0.002: PASS, ratio 0.52 [CORRECTED 21:29: the measured tail is 5.924·10⁻⁴, so LHS = 3.117·10⁻³, FAIL, ratio 1.56]. On the antichain tail, valid at every α: PASS from α = 0.006 to beyond 0.025, the best ratio 0.61 near α = 0.015. At the one-sigma corner of the four spin-½ couplings (every |t| raised by its fit error: 0.12941, −0.00119, 0.00598, 0.00628): the same 12,377,661 leaves, the census being a count on the lattice, the leaf sum 7.043·10⁻⁴ (the census polynomial on run d's table to the digit), the measured tail 1.727·10⁻⁴, LHS 1.430·10⁻³: PASS, ratio 0.72 (record e) [CORRECTED 21:29: the measured tail 8.457·10⁻⁴, LHS 4.256·10⁻³, FAIL, ratio 2.13; on the antichain tail PASS for α from 0.010 to 0.020, best 0.845]; on the antichain tail PASS from α = 0.010 to 0.020, ratio 0.85–0.91. The control on the same road: the plaquette gas alone at 0.12845 reads 5.9·10⁻⁵, ratio 0.03 — the certificate's four orders, which the long loops spend down to a factor of two [CORRECTED 21:29: to 1.6, at α ≈ 0.015]. WHAT IT SAYS: on the fitted two-step-blocked action at a_c = 4a the exit's own criterion — Theorem 8.9's surface-gas certificate with the fitted long loops entered as faces — is MET through a link by a factor of about two [CORRECTED 21:29: at α ≈ 0.015, by 1.6 in the sum and about seven per cent in the activities; not at α = 0.002], at the scale where the single-site Dobrushin row fails (1.095): on this reading the reach–contraction gap of RESULTS 1–9 is a gap of the criterion, not of the theory, and the two-step scale is where the exit's criterion is met. WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY (R1; the side note of 19:0x, "a promising reading, not the gap closed"): it is a reading of a twelve-term fit — the blocked action's omitted terms (longer loops, each a face of a further kind adding to W; the long loops' spin-1 and spin-3/2 faces entering the walls) are bounded by nothing here, and the margin, a factor of two at W = 0.34 [CORRECTED 21:29: seven per cent in the activities], would not survive the long-loop part of W doubling; the face dressing is the plaquette gas's κ_U; 2^χ ≤ 4 is the plaquette gas's lemma assumed for mixed sets; double arithmetic. It is not the gap and not a step of the proof. WHAT CLOSES THE EXIT, SHARPENED: (1) THE FLOW BOUND — the two-step-blocked weight's character coefficients bounded above along the flow from β = 2.2, every loop family and the tail of families together; (2) THE MIXED CERTIFICATE in interval arithmetic — this census table (exact and coupling-independent: its half of (2) is in hand to six faces), the closing tree's tail, the dressing for mixed faces and the pairing lemma for mixed sets — run on the bounds of (1).

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM II — THE TAIL MISREAD AND CORRECTED: AT α = 0.002 THE MIXED GAS FAILS THE CERTIFICATE ON THE FIT (RATIO 1.56); IT PASSES AT THE RATE α ≈ 0.015 BY A FACTOR 1.6 IN THE SUM AND ABOUT SEVEN PER CENT IN THE FACE ACTIVITIES — THINNER THAN THE ADDENDUM SAID, A READING OF THE FIT STILL (2026-11-03 21:29 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; records `cp_mixgas.2026-11-03d.txt` and `e` with dated corrections appended, `f` (the dressing tenfold) and `g` (the activities scaled), landed with this note; the addendum above bracketed in place).** THE DEFECT: the merge that summed the 28 root ranges' tails read each value at offset 10 behind the 9-character prefix "(1 - W)) ", so it dropped the leading digit of every part's tail (root 0's 5.384576·10⁻⁵ became 3.85·10⁻⁶) and printed a merged "measured tail" of 9.743·10⁻⁵ where the parts sum to 5.924·10⁻⁴. The instrument's own output was right; the census, its 12,377,661 leaves and every leaf sum stand; the antichain-tail lines, computed by `cp_mixgas_lhs.awk` from a closed form, stand. THE CORRECTED READINGS, all at six faces on the fit's n = 4 FULL spin-½ line: at α = 0.002 with the measured tail, LHS = 6.288·10⁻⁴ + 4.2 × 5.924·10⁻⁴ = 3.117·10⁻³ — FAIL, ratio 1.56; at the one-sigma corner 7.043·10⁻⁴ + 4.2 × 8.457·10⁻⁴ = 4.256·10⁻³, FAIL, ratio 2.13; with the face dressing tenfold (κ = 0.05, record f) 4.233·10⁻³, FAIL, 2.12; with the four activities scaled by 1.15 (record g) 8.125·10⁻³, FAIL, 4.06. On the closing tree's antichain tail 4.2 W⁷/(1 − W), valid at every α: the central line PASSES for α from 0.006 to 0.025, best ratio 0.607 at α = 0.015; the corner PASSES for α from 0.010 to 0.020, best 0.845; the tenfold dressing PASSES for α from 0.010 to 0.020, best 0.845; the four activities scaled together PASS to λ = 1.07 (best ratios 0.751, 0.864, 0.992, 1.217 at λ = 1.03, 1.05, 1.07, 1.10, each at α = 0.015). WHAT IT SAYS NOW: on the fitted two-step-blocked action the exit's own criterion — the surface-gas certificate with the long loops as faces — is met at the rate α ≈ 0.015, with a factor 1.6 in the sum and about seven per cent in the face activities, at the scale where the single-site row fails; it survives the fit's one-sigma corner and a tenfold face dressing (ratio 0.85), and it does not survive a ten per cent rise of all four activities together. The tail carries most of the sum at α = 0.015 (the leaf 9.2·10⁻⁴ against the tail's 8.2·10⁻³), so the margin is the six-face census's, and a deeper census or a sharper tail bound — the true measured tail at α = 0.015, or the exact census to seven faces — is what would widen it; the leaf sum alone would allow the activities to grow by a factor of about 2.5. WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY: R1 as before — a reading of a twelve-term fit, the omitted loops unbounded (a seven per cent margin in the activities does not survive the omitted loops adding a tenth to the weight through a link), the face dressing the plaquette gas's, 2^χ ≤ 4 for mixed sets resting on the floor's pairing lemma (S12; its elimination form in the S1 page's NOTE III [CORRECTED 23:17: the elimination theorem stands only as an implication, and S12's remainder is the BOUND at cycle rank ≥ 2 outside the unicyclic and budget classes — NOTE III ADDENDUM IV, edefdda93, the S1 seat's dated withdrawal]). **§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM IV — THE EIGHT-LINK FACES BREAK THE CLOSING-TREE TAIL: ON THE SEVENTEEN-TERM FIT THE CENSUS'S PASS DOES NOT SURVIVE, SO "MET AT α ≈ 0.015" IS A PROPERTY OF THE TWELVE-TERM TRUNCATION, NOT OF THE BLOCKED ACTION (2026-11-03 22:23 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_polycount.c` and its record `cp_polycount.2026-11-03a.txt`, landed with this note; the seventeen-term fit is agent AD's of 11-02, `STDLIB/build/aperanton/agents/2026-11-02/math/AD/rec/su2/su2_b2.2_N24_L8.fit.txt`, its §9.2).** THE COUNT: the self-avoiding polygons through a link of Z⁴ are 6, 114, 2,868, 81,540 and 2,533,296 at lengths 4, 6, 8, 10 and 12 (`cp_polycount.c`, its known answers the census's own 6 and 114; the first draft halved every count and read three plaquettes, caught by that known answer) — AD's `loops8.py` builds the same 2,868 by a second road, by extent class 24 (1×3), 60 (2×2), 48 (cube), 1,440 (box) and 1,296 (tesseract). THE FIT: AD fitted those five classes beside the twelve terms on the same 400 coarse configurations (model L1+8: plaquette 0.12839, rectangle −0.00045, chair 0.00554, parallelogram 0.00545, the plaquette and six-link couplings moved under two per cent; the eight-link couplings −0.00007(90), −0.00049(41), +0.00053(56), +0.00007(10), +0.00016(9), each within 1.8 of its error of zero). THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE CENSUS: as faces, the eight-link loops add to the closing tree's per-step weight through a link 0.116 at α = 0.015 at the central values (0.031 to 0.223 with every |t| moved one error; 0.095 at α = 0.002), the box and tesseract classes carrying most of it by their count — against an allowance of about 0.0015 for faces outside the census at α = 0.015 (the paths through such a face charged W a step from depth three, the tail at W + ΔW). So with the eight-link classes as faces the certificate in its closing-tree form FAILS, by more than an order of magnitude even at the low end of their errors; the census's PASS of ADDENDA II–III is a property of the twelve-term truncation. THE FAILURE IS THE TREE'S, NOT THE LEAVES': an eight-link face closes only with a disc of further faces — two rectangles under a 2×2, three plaquettes under a 1×3, chairs and parallelograms under the non-planar classes — so each such leaf weighs 10⁻⁶ to 10⁻⁹ and the eight-link leaf sum is small beside the six-link one; it is the closing tree that charges every face through a link at every step, and the polygon entropy (growing 25-, 28-, 31-fold per two links toward μ² ≈ 46) outruns the coupling decay the fits resolve (plaquette to six-link about 27-fold; the chairs and parallelograms ≈ t_plaq²/3, second order in the plaquette coupling, the rectangles suppressed). The single-site row rose with the same classes (AD §9.2, 1.16 → 1.46). WHAT IT SAYS: no certificate of the closing-tree form decides the exit at 4a on the blocked action; what decides it is (i) A TAIL BOUND THAT CHARGES EACH OPEN CONFIGURATION ITS CLOSING COST — a long face costs its disc, so a potential in the odd links (a Peierls bound on the completion sum, G(T) ≤ Aρ^{|∂T|}) in place of the tree's uniform W — or (ii) the decay of the blocked couplings with loop length, which no fit here resolves past six links (the eight-link couplings are within two errors of zero). Both are the work; the census's exact six-face leaf table (record d) is the part of the certificate in hand, the leaves being what such a bound keeps. THE PROOF's Step 35 carries the qualification; TASKS carries the two objects.

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM V — THE PEIERLS TAIL: A FINITE CONDITION THAT BOUNDS THE COMPLETION OF EVERY OPEN CONFIGURATION BY ITS BOUNDARY, AND THE CONDITION THE EXIT AT 4a ACTUALLY NEEDS (2026-11-03 23:17 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_mixgas.c` with its PEIERLS and PTAIL modes, `cp_peierls8.c`, and the records `cp_mixgas.2026-11-03j.txt` and `cp_peierls8.2026-11-03a.txt`, landed with this note).** ADDENDUM IV named the missing object: a tail bound charging each open configuration its closing cost. HERE IT IS, IN FINITE FORM. THE LEMMA: the closing tree adds to a state T a face through its smallest odd link l, so every odd link of T a face contains is ordered at or after l; with k_max(f) the number of the face's links at or after l, the local sum Σ_{f∋l, f∉T} w_f ρ^{|f| − 2k_f} over the children of T is at most B(ρ) := Σ_{f∋l} w_f ρ^{|f| − 2k_max(f)}, a finite sum fixed by the gas and the link order alone. If B(ρ) ≤ 1 at each of the four link directions, the completion sum of EVERY state — the weight of the Z₂-minimal closed sets reached from T — is at most ρ^{|∂T|}: by induction on the depth, a face changes |∂T| by |f| − 2k_f and a closed child completes to 1 = ρ⁰. A census cut node v is then charged m(v) ρ^{|∂v|}, the cost of closing its open boundary, in place of the tree's uniform W a step (each node takes the smaller of the two valid bounds). W = 0 in technique — a Peierls potential in the Kotecký–Preiss manner; the earning is the finite form through k_max, and its numbers. THE CHECKS (each in double arithmetic beside the certificate's interval road; the refusal fires by name below the threshold — ρ = 0.60, B = 2.147): the plaquette gas at 0.12845, B(0.70) = 0.563; the twelve-term mixed gas, B(0.70) = 1.085 (fails), B(0.75) = 0.823 at α = 0.002 and 0.938 at α = 0.015; with agent AD's five eight-link classes as faces (2,868 through a link, the class counts 24, 60, 48, 1,440, 1,296 met by `cp_peierls8.c`'s own walk, a second road to AD's `loops8`), B(0.75) = 1.212 (fails), B(0.80) = 0.909, B(0.85) = 0.714 at the central couplings, and B(0.85) = 0.884 with every |t₈| raised one error, at α = 0.002 — at α = 0.015 the thresholds move to ρ = 0.85 (B = 0.830) and ρ = 0.90 (B = 0.835) (record `cp_peierls8.2026-11-03a.txt`); with every eight-link coupling zero the four-family value returns unchanged (the known answer). THE CENSUS WITH THE PEIERLS TAIL (record j): at five faces the tail falls from 1.844·10⁻³ (the closing tree) and 1.497·10⁻³ (the need-aware tree) to 4.450·10⁻⁴ at ρ = 0.75; at six faces and α = 0.002 the Peierls tail is 8.748·10⁻⁵ at ρ = 0.73 (B = 0.915) and 1.226·10⁻⁴ at ρ = 0.75, against the closing tree's measured 5.924·10⁻⁴ — so LHS = 6.288·10⁻⁴ + 4.2 × 8.748·10⁻⁵ = 9.962·10⁻⁴: THE TWELVE-TERM TRUNCATION PASSES AT α = 0.002, ratio 0.498, its tail now a theorem about that gas (ADDENDUM II's FAIL there was the closing tree's, not the gas's); at α = 0.015, the Peierls tail at rho = 0.75 is 2.213231e-04 and LHS 1.848133e-03 against 0.015, ratio 0.123. WHAT THE PEIERLS BOUND DOES NOT DO FOR THE EIGHT-LINK FACES: the condition holds with them, but the bound is weak at small boundaries by its nature (ρ^{|∂T|} at |∂T| = 8 is 0.17 at ρ = 0.80, where an eight-link loop's real completion needs a disc of two to four further faces, of weight 10⁻⁵ or less), so charging the eight-link roots by it gives Σ w ρ⁸ ≈ 0.016 — eight times α. Their own closed surfaces must be counted exactly to a depth where the bound is sharp (an eight-link face and up to three or four more), the Peierls tail beyond: the instrument is `cp_mixgas.c` with the eight-link templates (1,434 per site, 2,868 through a link), not yet built; until it runs, the seventeen-term fit's verdict is open, its closing-tree verdict (ADDENDUM IV) superseded as the weaker tool's. THE CONDITION THE EXIT AT 4a ACTUALLY NEEDS: B carries, for the loops of n links, Σ_{n-link faces} w_n ρ^{n − 2k_max}; the polygons through a link grow 19-, 25-, 28- and 31-fold per two links (`cp_polycount`, toward μ² ≈ 46), while the couplings the fits resolve fall about 23- to 27-fold per two links (plaquette to six-link; the chairs and parallelograms ≈ t_plaq²/3, the rectangles suppressed). So on the resolved trend the loops beyond eight links make B grow without bound at every ρ < 1, and NO loop-expansion certificate — closing tree or Peierls — can certify the blocked action at 4a unless its couplings fall faster with loop length than the polygon entropy grows. If the longer loops are generated at successive orders of the plaquette coupling (a fall of about 3/t_plaq per two links), that asks t_plaq ≲ 3/μ² ≈ 0.065: the two-step scale (0.128) is outside it, the three-step scale (0.006 ± 0.004 on the coarser record's fit) far inside. THE QUANTITY THAT DECIDES is the blocked couplings' decay in loop length — and the plaquette-to-six-link ratio is not that decay: both loops sit inside one coarse cell, so their ratio is a local one, while the decay of a blocked action's couplings with loop size is set by the blocked theory's correlation length, which an eight-link loop at 4a (eight to twelve fine lattice units across) spans several times over at β = 2.2, so a much faster fall is what the physics leads one to expect — expected, not measured [REFUTED IN ITS MECHANISM — 2026-11-05 02:06 EST, ADDENDUM VI: fibre clustering bounds a blocked coupling by its loop’s diameter, not its length; 1,344 of the 2,868 eight-link loops through a link lie inside one unit cell, and on 2,000 configurations the per-length activity falls by 0.685 then 0.570]; the eight-link couplings are within two errors of zero on 400 configurations, consistent with it, and 1,600 more of the same 24⁴ record (AD's sampler, a new seed, β = 2.2) are being sampled tonight to halve their errors. WHAT IT SAYS: the mixed certificate's tail is closed in finite form for any face gas whose B is at most one; the exit's open content at 4a is exactly the decay of the blocked couplings with loop length, and the exit scale may be 8a — where the fit is undetermined and the decay is the generated loops', not the noise floor's. R1 throughout: every coupling a reading of a fit.

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI — THE BLOCKED LINK'S CONDITIONAL LAW READ WITH NO FIT (FLAT IN THE LOCAL ORDER AT 4a, STIFFENING AT 2a), THE SEVENTEEN-TERM FIT ON 2,000 CONFIGURATIONS, AND ADDENDUM V'S EXPECTED FAST FALL REFUTED IN ITS MECHANISM (2026-11-05 02:06 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; instruments `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_order.c`, `cp_order_run.sh`, `cp_order_pool.awk`, `cp_fit.c`, `cp_peierls8.c`, `cp_stats_concat.sh`; records `cp_order.2026-11-05a.txt`, `cp_order.2026-11-05b.txt`, `cp_fit.2026-11-04a.txt`, `cp_peierls8.2026-11-04a.txt`, `ymc_n24_s2/`, `ymc_n24_c2/`; landed edd4f8459 and this landing).** THE FIT ON 2,000 CONFIGURATIONS (a reading of a fit, R1; one-sigma jackknife errors): AD's 400 and 1,600 of a second chain of the 24⁴, β = 2.2 record at n = 4 give, in the seventeen-term model's L1+8 line, plaquette +0.12869(49), rectangle +0.00112(46), chair +0.00555(17), parallelogram +0.00454(37), and the five eight-link classes 1×3 +0.00044(38), 2×2 −0.00008(28), cube +0.00077(25), box +0.00002(4), tesseract +0.00017(5). The two chains fitted apart agree on every coupling but the rectangle (+0.00151(40) against −0.00045(70), 2.4 errors apart). Two eight-link classes are resolved at three errors, the cube and the tesseract class, and they are the two whose loops lie inside one unit cell. THE REFUTATION OF ADDENDUM V'S EXPECTATION, UNSOFTENED: that addendum wrote that an eight-link loop at 4a spans the blocked theory's correlation length several times over, so that a much faster fall of the couplings with loop length is what one expects. The mechanism is wrong. Clustering of the fibre bounds a blocked coupling by its loop's DIAMETER, not its length, and length is not diameter: of the 114 six-link loops through a link 96 lie inside one unit cell; of the 2,868 eight-link loops 1,344 do (the 48 of the cube class and the 1,296 of the tesseract class) and 2,844 lie within two units; of the 81,540 ten-link loops 5,280 lie inside one cell and 77,040 within two. The count of loops no wider than a plaquette's own cell grows with the length, and nothing in the correlation length suppresses them. MEASURED on the 2,000 line: the first-order activity through a link is 0.1930 for the plaquettes, 0.1322 for the six-link loops and 0.0754 ± 0.022 for the eight-link loops (0.0643 of it from the two resolved classes) — ratios 0.685 and 0.570, a slow fall; the mean coupling per loop falls 27.7-fold then 44-fold while the number of loops grows 19-fold then 25-fold. In powers of the plaquette coupling t_p the compact classes read chair 0.335 t_p², parallelogram 0.274 t_p², rectangle 0.068 t_p², cube 0.36 t_p³, tesseract 0.080 t_p³: numbers of order one times t_p to the area the loop spans, the pattern of loops generated by the plaquette term, which decays in area and not in length. THE PEIERLS SUM BY LENGTH on the 2,000 line (α = 0.002, central couplings; a reading of a fit): at ρ = 0.80 the plaquettes give 0.339, the six-link faces 0.299, the eight-link faces 0.224, sum 0.862; at 0.85: 0.287, 0.233, 0.159, sum 0.680; at 0.90: 0.248, 0.189, 0.120, sum 0.557; at 0.95: 0.219, 0.158, 0.094, sum 0.472; at the one-sigma corner the sums are 0.994 at 0.80 and 0.775 at 0.85. The successive ratios are 0.883 and 0.749 at ρ = 0.80, 0.813 and 0.683 at 0.85, 0.760 and 0.634 at 0.90, 0.720 and 0.598 at 0.95. BY THE RULE that every further length repeats the last measured ratio — a rule, not a measurement — the whole sum is 1.53 at ρ = 0.80, 1.02 at 0.85, 0.76 at 0.90 and 0.61 at 0.95: the condition B ≤ 1 then holds from ρ ≈ 0.86 up and fails below. What decides it is the ten-link faces' weight; the closing census `cp_closing.c` (the desktop seat's, record `cp_closing.2026-11-04b.txt`, landed 45e5b227a) reads their closing coefficient per unit weight at 1.448 through five faces against 1.075 through four — not converged in depth — beside 1.206 for the eight-link faces; its depth series is running. THE FIT-FREE READING. Every number above is a reading of an action fitted on Gibbs-typical configurations, and a fit weighs the rare ordered configurations by their rarity: it cannot say what the blocked action does there. `cp_order.c` reads the blocked measure itself. For each coarse link c let Σ_c be the sum of its six staples, n_c = |Σ_c|/6 ∈ [0, 1] the local order its eighteen neighbours present, and a_c = ½ tr(V_c Σ_c)/|Σ_c| its alignment. Under any plaquette action exp(t Σ_p ½ tr V_p) the law of V_c given the rest is the von Mises–Fisher law of concentration 6 t n_c, so E[a | rest] = I₂/I₁(6 t n) and the spin-1 moment E[(4a² − 1)/3 | rest] = I₃/I₁(6 t n). The instrument bins the links by n and reads the mean alignment, the concentration κ_eff a von Mises–Fisher law of that mean would have, the EFFECTIVE COUPLING t_eff(n) = κ_eff/(6n), the spin-1 moment beside its von Mises–Fisher value, the tail P(n > K) and its clustering on parallel links; and the PLAQUETTE PSEUDO-LIKELIHOOD COUPLING t_PL, the root of Σ_c n_c (a_c − I₂/I₁(6 t n_c)) = 0. LEMMA N (PROVED; for every measure on the coarse links): E[n_c²] = 1/6 + (W_rect + 4 W_chair)/6, where W_rect and W_chair are the mean ½-traces of the 1×2 rectangle and of the chair, averaged over the 3 rectangles and the 12 chairs that are the products of two staples of c. Proof: |Σ_c|² = Σ_{i,j} ½ tr(S_i S_j⁻¹); the six diagonal terms are 1; a forward and a backward staple in one plane close the rectangle (3 pairs), two staples in different planes close a chair (12 pairs); divide by 36. So the second moment of the local order is the six-link loops' expectation, with no fit. KNOWN ANSWERS, all met before any reading (the runner refuses otherwise): the plaquette mean from the staples equals the plaquette-by-plaquette mean to 10⁻¹⁴ on every configuration read; Haar links read E[n²] = 0.16658 against 1/6 and t_PL = +0.0001 ± 0.0011; the Wilson action at block size 1 reads t_PL = 2.1985 ± 0.0014 at β = 2.2 and 0.13013 ± 0.00048 at β = 0.13, with t_eff flat in n (χ² 18.8 over 29 bins, 34.5 over 33), the spin-1 moment on its von Mises–Fisher value (−0.00001 ± 0.00024, +0.00010 ± 0.00011) and, at 0.13, E[n²] = 0.167575 against Lemma N's 1/6 + 5u²/6 = 0.167546. THE READINGS (Monte Carlo, with jackknife errors; none a bound). At 4a, 2,000 configurations, 10,368,000 links: t_PL = 0.13114 ± 0.00049 (the two chains 0.13078 ± 0.00093 and 0.13123 ± 0.00050) — the one-parameter plaquette projection of the whole single-link law, a different functional of the measure from the seventeen-term fit's plaquette coefficient (0.0024 ± 0.0007 above it) and not a disagreement with it; t_eff(n) is FLAT in the local order, χ² = 27.2 over the 33 bins of at least 1,000 links against t_PL, the extremes −1.7 and +1.9 errors, from n = 0.03 to n = 0.85; the tail of the local order is within 8 % of the plaquette action's at β = 0.13 and within 14 % of Haar's up to n > 0.8 (P(n > 0.7) = 1.107·10⁻² against 1.067·10⁻² and 1.027·10⁻²; P(n > 0.8) = 7.93·10⁻⁴ against 7.35·10⁻⁴ and 6.99·10⁻⁴), with 75 links above 0.9 where the plaquette action's rate gives 57, and ordered links do not cluster (P(both)/P² = 1.00 to 1.02 at neighbouring parallel links up to n > 0.7); the spin-1 moment sits above its von Mises–Fisher value by 0.00057 ± 0.00010, 13 % of it, 5.5 errors, where every control reads zero — the part of the conditional law that is not a spin-½ plaquette term, read with no fit; and W_rect + 4 W_chair = 0.01177 ± 0.00020, 2.19 times the 0.00537 of the plaquette action at t_PL and within 7 % of the fit's first-order 0.01100. At 2a (100 configurations of the same theory at n = 2, 8,294,400 links, sampled for this reading): t_PL = 1.2678 ± 0.0009, and the law STIFFENS on ordered data — t_eff rises from 1.19 at n ≈ 0.2 to 1.34 at n ≈ 0.85 (χ² = 6,418 over 35 bins against a constant; −24.7 and +27.1 errors at the extremes), the spin-1 excess is +0.0073 ± 0.0001, and ordered links cluster (P(both)/P² = 1.36 at n > 0.7, 1.73 at n > 0.8, 2.02 at n > 0.9 on neighbouring parallel links). The instrument sees stiffening and clustering where they exist, one block step below, and reads neither at 4a. At 8a (AD's 150 configurations of 32⁴ at n = 8, a 4⁴ coarse lattice, 153,600 links): t_PL = 0.0058 ± 0.0044, every reading zero within 2.5 errors. WHAT THE READING IS: the conditional law's MEAN over the environments of a given local order, on n ≤ 0.9 — it removes the specific possibility that rare ordered data overturn the small plaquette coupling the fit reads at 4a, to the resolution of 10⁷ links; it is not a supremum over environments, so it bounds nothing and the loop certificate at 4a stays a reading. THE TYPICAL-DATA CRITERION, CLOSED AT THE DESK (W = 0: Dobrushin–Pecherski, Lecture Notes in Mathematics 1021 (1983) 97–110; Conache–Kondratiev–Kozitsky–Pasurek, arXiv:1501.00673): uniqueness and exponential decay follow from Dobrushin's contraction on a good set of boundary data alone, given a Lyapunov inequality for the rest, for one-site specifications of finite range. At 4a the single-site row is above one on TYPICAL data by the plaquette term alone (1.094 at first order, `ds_lower_bound.2026-11-04a.txt`) and the ordered set carries none of it (t_eff flat, tails Haar's), so relaxing the contraction on a rare set certifies nothing there; it is the shape of a criterion the 8a readings would meet were they bounds, which no reading is. WHY A FIT IS NOT A POTENTIAL (W = 0): the potential of a block-averaged measure exists with exponentially decaying interactions when the fine system constrained to the coarse datum is in the Dobrushin–Shlosman regime uniformly in the datum (van Enter–Fernández–Sokal, J. Stat. Phys. 72 (1993), §4; Bertini–Cirillo–Olivieri, J. Stat. Phys. 97 (1999)), and only almost everywhere when the good data are typical and not all (Bricmont–Kupiainen–Lefevere, Commun. Math. Phys. 194 (1998); Bertini–Cirillo–Olivieri, Commun. Math. Phys. 261 (2006)); that is Theorem X's clause (ii), and the 2a reading says where its uniformity is in question: ordered coarse data. THE WALL AT ITS ADDRESS, AND THE ATTACK MADE ON IT AT THE DESK IN THIS PASS. The wall is (F2) of §21: a bound on the blocked action as a function on every coarse configuration through the crossover. Five routes were taken to the desk and each stops at a named place. (a) Dobrushin's single-site condition on the fine lattice at β = 2.2, and Dobrushin–Shlosman's on blocks, in the worst case: one reversed staple against five cancelling ones moves a link's law by a total variation of order one on each of eighteen neighbours, so the fine row is far above one, and the block condition's worst datum has no bound (the block readings on record are of another conditioning). (b) Correlation inequalities: Ginibre's are theorems for abelian groups and no analogue is known for SU(2). (c) Reflection positivity, chessboard and Migdal–Kadanoff bounds: each holds verbatim for U(1), whose four-dimensional theory has no gap at weak coupling (Guth 1980; Fröhlich–Spencer 1982), so none carries the step. (d) Clustering of the fibre: it bounds a coupling by its loop's diameter (the refutation above) and gives nothing per length. (e) The loop certificate at 4a: its small parameter is a per-link bound on the blocked action, the step's own conclusion. The step therefore stands OPEN, at clause (ii) of Theorem X uniformly in the coarse datum, with M7b's Lemma 9.1 (the blocked action's mixed second derivative is a fibre covariance of two local forces) the identity that turns a uniform fibre bound into (F2). THE PLACE ON THE ROAD AND THE NEXT STAGE, EACH RUNNING: at 4a the plaquette coupling is inside the pure-plaquette certificate 0.426 by a factor 3.3, the conditional law is flat in the local order, and what stands between the readings and the mixed certificate is the weight of the ten-link and longer faces — decided by the closing census's depth series and by `cp_mixgas8` on the 2,000 line at the smallest admissible ρ, central and corner (its record k stopped at six parts at the 11-04 sign-out; root 0 read B = 0.5957 + 0.2339 = 0.8296 at α = 0.015, ρ = 0.85 on AD's line); at 8a every reading is zero within its error on a 4⁴ coarse lattice, and a 48⁴ record at block 8 (a 6⁴ coarse lattice, 300 configurations, AD's sampler, seed 481105, launched 02:00 on 2026-11-05, `ymc_n48_c8/`) is being sampled so that `cp_order` reads t_PL(8a), its spin-1 excess and W_rect + 4 W_chair on ten times the links. Every coupling a reading of a fit; every Monte Carlo number a reading with its error.

WHAT THE NUMBER IS MADE OF: 75 % of the left side is the eight-link TAIL, a bound (4.2 × 1.7495·10⁻³ = 7.348·10⁻³); the eight-link weight counted exactly is 0.7 % of it; without any eight-link term the same census at this ρ reads 9.10·10⁻⁴ + 4.2 × 3.41·10⁻⁴ = 2.34·10⁻³. RUNNING: the one-sigma corner at α = 0.015 (ρ = 0.84, B = 0.947615; launched 02:08), then AD’s central line at α = 0.015, ρ = 0.82 (B = 0.955868; ρ = 0.81 fails there at 1.005522).

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-b — THE CONTACT-LEAF BOUND IS FALSE, AND WHAT THAT DOES TO THE EXIT’S READINGS: THE MIXED CERTIFICATE CHARGES ITS TAIL THE CONSTANT 4.2 OF THAT BOUND (2026-11-05 03:02 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; the S1 page’s entry (q) of the same hour; `cp_mixgas.c` line 210; the seat iii-bb’s record `cp_mixgas8.2026-11-05d.txt`, on disk).** A Z₂-minimal contact leaf of 46 plaquettes reads S = 20/3 where the staked half of Lemma S12 allows 4, on four roads, and the word systems of k blocks read 4(5/3)^k (S1 (q)); the strong-coupling theorem is restated at β ≤ 0.3650 under four inputs, with 0.4764 and every threshold above 0.3650 withdrawn as readings of a certificate whose tail bound is false (THE PROOF e2ce30b). THREE CONSEQUENCES HERE. (1) The radius this page has used for the pure plaquette action, “the certified 0.426”, is the mixed-gas instrument’s own radius and carries the same tail charge [CORRECTED 2026-11-05 04:00 EST: it is not the mixed-gas instrument’s — it is the flux-surface gas certificate’s own β* = 0.4266 at γ = 6, F0 = 20 (S1 §14; the known answer in the header of `aperanton_kp_cert.awk`), which that instrument’s header quotes; that certificate charges every census leaf and the whole tail the bound 4σ(F)u^F, so it carries the false constant in its tail as said, and it charges BOUNDS, never exact weights, which is why the gluing input is an L² statement on the bound (THE PROOF Step 17)]; the radius free of the contact-leaf bound is 0.3650, so the fitted 4a plaquette coupling 0.12869 lies inside it by a factor 2.8, not 3.3 (ADDENDUM VI’s sentence is read so). (2) Every PASS of the mixed certificate — the twelve-term truncation at α = 0.015, the Peierls tail’s at α = 0.002 on that truncation, and ADDENDUM VI-a’s 0.6503 at α = 0.015 through the eight-link faces — charges each closed set of its tail 4σ ≤ 4.2, the contact-leaf bound, which is a theorem for branch-free closed surfaces and for the proved classes of S12 and is false for contact leaves from 46 plaquettes on. The exactly counted sets (at most six faces) are untouched; the tail is not: each PASS stands as a reading only until its tail is charged by a proved bound. The proved bound on the plaquette gas is the all-spin bound, 4(C₁K₁^F + C_IK_I^F) with K₁ ≥ √2 per plaquette; its form for the mixed faces is not yet written. If it carries with the same K per face, the Peierls condition has to hold for face weights multiplied by √2, and the sums of ADDENDUM VI say at which ρ it can: B(ρ)·√2 ≤ 1 needs B ≤ 0.707, which the 2,000 line meets from ρ ≈ 0.84 at α = 0.002 (0.680 at 0.85) and from ρ ≈ 0.88 at α = 0.015 (0.788 at 0.85, 0.644 at 0.90) through eight links — before the ten-link faces, and as a reading of a fit. (3) Record d of `cp_mixgas8` (the 2,000 line, α = 0.002, ρ = 0.78, B = 0.959498; the same 23,574,078 closed sets as record b): leaf 6.631973·10⁻⁴, tree tail 7.058207·10⁻⁴ (four-family cuts 1.123357·10⁻⁴, eight-link tail 5.934850·10⁻⁴), left side 3.627644·10⁻³ against α = 0.002, ratio 1.8138: FAIL, by the eight-link tail’s bound (without it 1.135·10⁻³, 57 % of α; the verdict would turn at an eight-link tail 2.9 times smaller). THE NEXT STAGE, ITS INSTRUMENT NAMED AND ASKED FOR THIS HOUR: `cp_mixgas8` with its tail charged by the all-spin bound in place of 4.2, on the 2,000 line, central and corner; and, on the strong-coupling side, the bound 4θ^F on the contact leaves of the tail (S1 (q)), whose θ decides both the theorem’s threshold above 0.3650 and the exit’s tail.

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-f — THE 8a READING ON A 6⁴ COARSE LATTICE: THE BLOCKED MEASURE IS HAAR TO THE RESOLUTION OF 5.6·10⁵ LINKS (2026-11-05 03:52 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; instrument `cp_order.c`, record `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_order.2026-11-05c.txt`, the coarse record `ymc_n48_c8/su2_b2.2_N48_c8.first108.coarse8` with its provenance).** 108 configurations of the 48⁴, β = 2.2 theory at block size 8 (the sampler of ADDENDUM VI was asked for 300 and was stopped at 108 by the session’s harness when the host ran critically short of memory; it was not started again; the 108 are whole, the plaquette mean from the staples meeting the plaquette-by-plaquette mean to 6·10⁻¹⁷ on each), 559,872 coarse links, 3.6 times those of the 4⁴ record. MEASURED, with jackknife errors over 18 blocks: the plaquette pseudo-likelihood coupling t_PL = +0.0011 ± 0.0017 (the 4⁴ record read 0.0058 ± 0.0044; the two agree, and both with zero); W_rect + 4 W_chair = 6(E[n²] − 1/6) = −0.0002 ± 0.0009; the spin-1 moment minus its von Mises–Fisher value −0.0001 ± 0.0004; t_eff against t_PL χ² = 31.7 over 27 bins, extremes −2.6 and +1.8 errors; the tail of the local order equal to Haar’s at every threshold (P(n > 0.5) = 0.19923 against 0.19905, P(n > 0.7) = 1.012·10⁻² against 1.027·10⁻², P(n > 0.8) = 7.07·10⁻⁴ against 6.99·10⁻⁴); no clustering. So at 8a every fit-free reading is zero within its error and t_PL is below 0.0045 at two errors — where the plaquette law read at 4a, 0.131, would send it by the area law of a plaquette gas (u⁴ ≈ 10⁻⁶). A reading of means, not a bound on the blocked action: it says the 8a blocked measure has no structure a sample of this size can resolve, which is what an exit there would look like and is not a certificate of one. THE NEXT STAGE AND ITS INSTRUMENTS: the bound 4Cθ^F on contact leaves (S1 (q) and its NOTE I), on which both the exit’s certificate and any coupling above 0.3650 wait; record h of `cp_mixgas8` at the floor rate (running); and, for 8a, a longer sample when the host’s memory allows — the remaining 192 configurations are not being sampled now.

Two things decide it, and no reading supplies either: the rate θ of a proved bound 4Cθ^F on contact leaves, between 1.0107 and √2, and the fit’s error region. **§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-d — WHAT THE CLOSING CENSUS CHARGES, AND WHAT (q) DOES TO IT: NOTHING AT SPIN ½ — EVERY CLOSED SET OF AT MOST FIVE FACES HAS A CONTACT GRAPH OF CYCLE RANK AT MOST ONE, WHERE THE CONSTANT 4 IS A THEOREM (2026-11-05 05:15 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat db5968f8, in the letter the trunk kept free for this line; the instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_closing.c` with its reader K-7, landed ec9128ca9; the run of record `cp_closing.2026-11-05a.txt`).** WHAT IT CHARGES. `cp_closing.c` weighs each closed set S of its census — at most five faces, an eight-link face among them, or a ten-link face completed by plaquettes and six-link faces — at the mixed certificate's leaf charge 4σ(S)·Π_f w_f, σ(S) = 1 + 9·ΠR1_f + 16·ΠR3_f, re-implemented from `cp_mixgas.c`. ITS RECORD PRINTS NO VERDICT: it reads sums of those charges ((B) 4.333178·10⁻⁵ through a link at AD's centre, the ten-link column's closing coefficient 1.448 per unit w₁₀ through five faces against 1.075 through four), and every sentence above that uses them is a reading at fitted couplings (R1). WHAT (q) DOES TO IT. Lemma S12's staked half fell at 46 plaquettes on a leaf whose contact graph has cycle rank 2; what stands by the reduction and the one-chord exhaustion (S1 (q) NOTE I) is |S| ≤ 4 at spin ½ on every closed set with links of multiplicity two and four and a contact graph of cycle rank at most one, and that reduction reads nothing of the faces but that they are discs. THE FIVE-FACE LEMMA (proved): a closed set of at most five polygon faces — of length at most eight, or one of length ten with the rest of length at most six — is Z₂-minimal (the instrument's header), has links of multiplicity two and four only, and a contact graph G of cycle rank at most one. Four faces: a link of multiplicity four lies in every face, so G lies in every face and holds no cycle. Five faces: each contact link is missing from exactly one face; suppose b₁(G) ≥ 2. (i) Two faces that are cycles of G: each misses exactly the rest of G, so the other three faces contain the symmetric difference of the two, which holds a cycle, and a polygon holding a cycle is that cycle — three faces equal. (ii) One face C that is a cycle of G: the rest of G lies in each of the other four faces, so it holds no cycle, and b₁(G) ≥ 2 then gives a vertex of C of degree three in G; each of the other four faces must miss one of C's two links there, and only two can. (iii) No such face: every face misses at least b₁(G) ≥ 2 contact links, so G has at least ten, and the five faces must hold 4|G| ≥ 40 contact link-places where they have at most Σ(|f| − 1) ≤ 35 (29 with a ten-link face). THE READER, TWO-SIDED (K-7, every set the census records): the twelve-term gas, 626,360 records (each set once per face), rank 0 on every one, 143,112 with a contact link; the eight-link census, 4,110,450 sets, rank 0 on 4,110,354 (984,084 with a contact link) and rank 1 on 96 — all of five faces with a cube-class anchor, the plaquette whose four links are wrapped three times by three chairs and the strip of three plaquettes, the configuration the desk derived before the run, where the system is (a)(a³) on two discs and |S| = 4, the charge attained; the ten-link column to five faces, 20,316,588 sets, rank 0 on every one (3,856,824 with a contact link); no set of rank two or more and none with a link of another multiplicity. Every other line of the record equals run b's (`cp_closing.2026-11-04b.txt`) byte for byte. SO: on the closing census's population, and on every census row of at most five faces in `cp_mixgas.c` and `cp_mixgas8`, the 4 of the spin-½ term is a bound and not an input; the false constant enters the mixed certificate only through its TAIL (six faces and more), as ADDENDUM VI-b says of it. WHAT K-7 DOES NOT READ: σ's 9 and 16 on a set with a contact cycle, and the mixed spin assignments a set of two pieces admits (on the 96, the plaquette at spin 3/2 inside wrappers at spin ½ integrates to a nonzero weight, ∫χ_{3/2}(a)χ_{½}(a³) = 1) — the dressing's input (3) of THE PROOF's Step 17.

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-i — WHERE THE LEVER IS: A UNIFORM RATE CHARGES EVERY FACE FOR CONTACTS THAT 99.4 % OF THE WEIGHT DOES NOT HAVE; THE CHARGE GRADED BY EACH SET’S OWN CONTACT RANK, STATED WITH WHAT IS PROVED OF IT AND WHAT IS NOT (2026-11-05 05:50 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; on the desktop seat ce191afc’s S1 (q) NOTE III, b1b8bea22).** NOTE III proves the count of contact cycle rank against size, r ≤ (75F + 167)/153 on every Z₂-minimal set with four-fold contact links (r ≤ (3F + 3)/5 in four lines), and reads what it buys: under the bound 4·2^r of S1’s NOTE IV the uniform rate is 2^{75/153} = 1.4046 a plaquette (1.404636; NOTE III prints 1.4047) against the all-spin √2 = 1.4142, 0.7 %; even the pair ceiling 5/3 at every rank, which is proved to rank 3 only, would read (5/3)^{75/306} = 1.1334 with the constant 4 × 1.32, above the turning rate the steering runs estimate (between 1.09 and 1.10 at C = 1). So no bound of the uniform form 4Cθ^F reached through a count of rank against size certifies the mixed census at this fit: the lever is elsewhere. WHERE: the bound is graded before it is made uniform — |S(X)| ≤ 4κ^{r(X)} with r(X) the contact cycle rank of that set — and the uniform rate spends it on every face of every set, while in record b the sets with a four-fold link at all carry 0.58 % of the counted weight. PROVED, two lines each: (a) r(X) ≤ n₄(X), the number of four-fold links of X, a cycle rank being at most the number of edges; (b) n₄ is additive along the closing tree: a link’s multiplicity rises by one with each face added through it, so a four-fold link is created exactly when a face is added over a link of multiplicity three, and n₄ of a leaf is the number of such incidences in its construction. IMPLIED by (a), (b) and a per-rank bound 4κ^r on closed sets of polygon faces with links of multiplicity two and four: every counted set may be charged 4σκ^{n₄} exactly, and the factor κ enters the tree only at a face laid over a link of multiplicity three. OPEN, and it is the whole difficulty of the tail: the completion bound of a cut state knows the size of its boundary and not which of its odd links have multiplicity three, so the graded charge needs a Peierls condition with two kinds of odd link, whose worst case over states is not yet bounded; and the per-rank bound’s hypotheses for polygon faces (κ = 2 from NOTE IV as NOTE III uses it; six-fold links outside it) are being confirmed with its author. AN EXPECTATION, written as one and to be refuted or met by the instrument, two of this seat’s expectations having been refuted this night: if contacts form in the tail as rarely as in the window, the graded tail stays within a few per cent of the plain one and the centre of the fit reads near 0.65 to 0.67 of α at κ = 2. THE INSTRUMENT ASKED FOR: `cp_mixgas8` printing the counted weight by n₄, then the charge 4σκ^{n₄} on the counted sets and the two-kind condition on the tail.

THE GRADED CHARGE OF VI-i, FIRST VERDICT. The counted sets can be charged exactly by their own rank (an engine change the seat iii-bb will make; the six-face sets weigh 7.5·10⁻⁷ in all on record b). The tail cannot, through the only lemma in hand: the completion of a cut state creates further links of multiplicity three, which only a potential can charge — ρ for an odd link of multiplicity one, κρ for one of multiplicity three — and its finite condition, the worst case over the multiplicities of a state, is B_g(ρ) = Σ_{f∋l} w_f ρ⁻¹ max(ρ⁻¹, κρ)^{k_max − 1} (κρ)^{|f| − k_max} ≤ 1, in which κ is paid PER FOUR-FOLD LINK and not per unit of rank, a potential being blind to whether a new contact link closes a cycle. The proved constants are per unit of rank (2 on every minimal set of disc faces, any sheet; 1, 1, 5/3, 5/3 exactly to rank three); the tail’s bound spends them per four-fold link. WHERE THE LEVER NOW IS, each a named lemma and none proved: a local charge of the rank — that a new four-fold link raises the contact cycle rank only where a finite local pattern is present, with that pattern’s own weight — or the minimal sets’ structure in a bound of rank by four-fold links (r ≤ 3N₄/4 is proved, S1 (p) NOTE IV ADDENDUM III; a bound with coefficient 1/4 would do and is false as stated, two squares of contact links sharing one having r = 2 on seven links); and, short of either, the exact window deepened, which lowers the bounded part of the tail directly. **§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-m — THE RANK CENSUS AT SIX FACES, AND TWO ELEMENTARY LEMMAS ON PIECES: NO Z₂-MINIMAL SET OF CONTACT RANK TWO THROUGH SIX FACES; A Z₂-MINIMAL SET HAS AT MOST r + 1 PIECES; A SET OF ONE PIECE IS BOUNDED BY THE SPHERE'S TERM AT EVERY SPIN; WHAT THE COUNTED SETS THEN STILL NEED (2026-11-05 09:50 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the desktop seat db5968f8; instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_closing.c`, mode `rank 6 1`, record `cp_closing_rank.2026-11-05a.txt`; the meeting with the seat iii-bb's `cp_mixgas8` record b, landed cd4f09303).**

THE INSTRUMENT. Mode `rank K` of the closing reader: the same closing search as K-7, with no table of sets (each tried face is forbidden to the faces tried after it at the same node, so every closed set through the anchor is read once), weights not computed. For every closed set it reads the contact graph (the links of multiplicity four OR MORE), its cycle rank r, Z₂-minimality by the rank of the faces' incidence with the links, whether a link has multiplicity six, whether the six-fold links hold a cycle among themselves, and the PIECES — the classes of faces joined through links of multiplicity two. Two populations: the twelve-term gas (plaquettes and six-link faces), where a set is read once for each of its faces, and the sets holding exactly one eight-link face completed by plaquettes and six-link faces. KNOWN ANSWERS, all met on the run of record: the twelve-term gas's Z₂-minimal counts divisible by the faces (K-8a); 621, 15,696, 413,505 sets through a link at three, four, five faces, the census of record's (K-8c); 4,110,450 sets with an eight-link face through five faces, 96 of them of rank one, the table search's (K-8d); and the theorem of Lemma P below on every row (K-8e). The run of record was taken on the build before the word `ext` was added to the mode; the five-face census without that word is byte for byte the same on that build and on the landed source.

THE CENSUS (Z₂-minimal sets; "through a link" counts a class once for each of its distinct links in one direction):

| population, faces | rank 0, links 2 and 4 | rank 0, a six-fold link | rank 1 | rank 1, a six-fold link | rank ≥ 2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| twelve-term gas, 3 | 300 classes, 621 through a link | — | — | — | — |
| twelve-term gas, 4 | 5,710 / 15,696 | — | — | — | — |
| twelve-term gas, 5 | 120,524 / 413,505 | — | — | — | — |
| twelve-term gas, 6 | 2,752,144 / 11,339,907 | 5,872 / 21,256 | 648 / 2,016 | — | none |
| one eight-link face, 3 | 4,260 / 10,152 | — | — | — | — |
| one eight-link face, 4 | 133,344 / 409,458 | — | — | — | — |
| one eight-link face, 5 | 3,972,750 / 14,879,994 | — | 96 / 264 | — | — |
| one eight-link face, 6 | 115,687,644 / 510,825,618 | 248,736 / 966,216 | 24,528 / 85,488 | 288 / 864 | none |

EVERY rank-0 row is ONE piece and EVERY rank-1 row is TWO pieces; no set has a cycle made of six-fold links alone. The twelve-term gas's six-face Z₂-minimal total through a link, 11,363,179, and its six-fold count 21,256 meet `cp_mixgas8`'s record b, whose own six-face total 11,947,839 is larger by 584,660 sets with a closed part (below). The 264 is the number the seat iii-bb's rank column is to meet at five faces.

LEMMA P (pieces; proved). Let X be a closed set of polygon faces, every link of even multiplicity, C its contact graph (multiplicity four or more), r the cycle rank of C. The boundary of a piece is a union of closed walks on C, so its class mod 2 lies in the cycle space of C, of dimension r. A family of pieces is a closed subset of X exactly when its classes sum to zero; all the pieces together do. If X is Z₂-minimal no proper subfamily does, so the m pieces have exactly one relation: m − 1 ≤ r. On a FOREST a Z₂-minimal set is one piece, whatever the multiplicities.

LEMMA O (one piece; proved). A link of multiplicity two forces the same spin on its two faces, so the faces of a piece P carry one spin j, and integrating P's two-fold links leaves d_j^{χ(P)} times the product over P's boundary circles of χ_j of their holonomies (χ(P) = interior vertices − interior links + faces). Since |χ_j| ≤ d_j, for ANY spin assignment on ANY closed set, |S| ≤ Π over the pieces of d_{j(P)}^{χ(P) + circles(P)} ≤ Π d_{j(P)}², the capped piece being a connected closed surface. A set of one piece is therefore bounded by the sphere's term d_j² at every spin, whatever its contact rank and multiplicities.

WHAT FOLLOWS FOR THE COUNTED SETS. (1) At spin ½ every Z₂-minimal set of both populations through six faces is at most 4. The forest rows, six-fold links included, are one piece: Lemma O at j = ½. The rank-one rows: take a four-fold link of the cycle as the chord (there is no cycle of six-fold links) and fix the gauge on the other contact links, a tree; the two pieces' classes are both odd on the chord, so one piece holds one of its four passes and the other three, the circles read tr a against tr a³, or tr a² tr a, or (tr a)³, with integrals −1 or 1, 0 or 2, 2 and Euler characteristics at most 1, 0, −1 for the second piece: 2·2·1, 2·1·2, 2·½·2, never above 4 — the constant K₁ = 1 of S1 (q) NOTES I and IV, here with the other links' multiplicities free. So ADDENDUM VI-d's sentence — the 4 of the spin-½ term is a bound and not an input — extends from five faces to six on these two populations. (2) At the higher uniform spins every forest row, the six-fold ones included, is one piece and is bounded by the sphere's d_j². (3) WHAT IS NOT YET CHARGED, found by the seat iii-bb on its own engine: σ = 1 + 9ΠR₁ + 16ΠR₃ charges the three UNIFORM assignments only, and a rank-one set has two pieces, which may carry two spins. Lemma O prices every mixed assignment at d_{s₁}² d_{s₂}²; the charge that closes it is 4K_r Π|a_½| + [Π over the pieces of (4 + 9ΠR₁ + 16ΠR₃) − 4^m] Π|a_½|, the products inside the bracket over each piece's own faces. On the 2,000 line's plaquette (R₃ = 3.448·10⁻⁴) the bracket's leading term is 2.2·10⁻² of the 4, on a population of weight about 10⁻¹¹ a link: a hole in the bound, invisible in the reading. **§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-n — THE CLOSED SETS WITH A CLOSED PART ARE THE TREE'S, AND THE CERTIFICATE CHARGES THEM: THE 4 OF α̃ = 4α IS S1 §14'S LOAD α + T + 2T, FORCED AND NOT A MARGIN; THE CENTRE'S CENSUS ADMITS A GLUING COST UP TO 6.086 A NEIGHBOUR WHERE THE TREE'S OWN ARE 1 AND 2; THE COUNTED SETS SPLIT BY CONTACT RANK AND CONTACT LINKS (2026-11-05 10:05 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; on the seat iii-bb's messages of 09:36 and 09:37 and its record `cp_mixgas8.2026-11-05l.txt` — complete over the 2,988 roots, not yet landed, its merged lines read here in its work directory and every sum re-done — and on ADDENDUM VI-m's item (4); one clause of RESULTS 10 and one of VI-m bracketed in place).**

THE QUESTION. The engine's search returns at the first closed state (`cp_mixgas8.c`, `dfs`: no odd link, count, return — read here), and a polymer is any closed link-connected set with an admissible spin assignment (S1 §14, Definitions). The seat iii-bb asked where a polymer Y ∪ R with R closed is charged, and whether the factor 4 in α̃ = 4α is a margin; VI-m's item (4) says that population "is the DRESSING of S1" and that the product inequality that would price it is false at c = 1. Both are answered by S1 §14 as it stands.

WHAT THE SEARCH REACHES. (a) Every Z₂-minimal closed set through the link: inside one no state closes early (the closing-tree lemma, S1 §14 item 1, face-shape independent). (b) Of the closed sets holding a proper closed subset, those on which the rule does not close early — 584,660 of the six-face sets without an eight-link face (record b's 11,947,839 less VI-m's Z₂-minimal 11,363,179), where the canonical count of that population in the twelve-term gas is 2,038,656 through a link, every one with d = 2 (the desktop seat db5968f8's `cp_closing rank 6 0 ext`, reported to the trunk this hour; its record a later entry). That the search cannot reach all of them is proved by the seat iii-bb: in a counted three-face set Y with largest link l*, a partial set has at least three odd links and l* is never the smallest, so no face through l* is added before Y closes, and Y ∪ Z — Z a closed three meeting Y in l* alone and missing the root link — is never a state. So RESULTS 10's clause "every closed connected face set reached" is FALSE as written and is bracketed; what the leaf census holds is every Z₂-minimal set and a part of the others, the part a surplus (below).

WHERE THE OTHERS ARE CHARGED. S1 §14 has three parts and they are three populations: the LEAVES (item 1: the Z₂-minimal sets, counted to F₀ and bounded beyond by the closing tree), the DRESSING (item 2: every decoration of a leaf "at integer spins", e^{κ|X|}), and THE TREE (item 3): "forest gluings exact (S3), cycle gluings at cost 2 with ≤ 2|X| sites (S4′, orientable); per link the load is α + T + 2T, so the composite sum converges if the leaf-and-dressing sum evaluated at 4α is ≤ α (κ_T = 4)". The closed sets with a closed part at spin ½ are the tree's composites, and the engine's α̃ = 4α is that load. So the 4 is FORCED, it is exactly the charge for this population, and VI-m's sentence is bracketed: its example — a box surface and a cube through the four links of one plaquette, 32 against 4 · 4 — is S4′'s own equality case, the F = 16 waist of S1 §10, a cycle gluing at its cost 2.

THE TREE AS ONE LEMMA (the seat iii-bb's statement of 09:36, here with the root piece taken Z₂-minimal, so that the root's charge is a minimal set's). Let Ψ bound the polymer weights, T(a) the supremum over links of Σ Ψ(Y) e^{a|L(Y)|} over the Z₂-minimal closed sets Y through the link, S(a) the same over ALL polymers, and suppose (M): Ψ(Y ∪ R₁ ∪ ⋯ ∪ R_k) ≤ Ψ(Y) Π_i c Ψ(R_i) whenever Y is Z₂-minimal and the R_i are polymers, pairwise without a common link, each sharing a link with Y. IF T(a + cλ) ≤ λ THEN S(a) ≤ λ. Proof. In a polymer X through the link let f be its least face through the link and Y a closed subset of X minimal among those holding f; Y is Z₂-minimal (a proper closed part Z of Y either holds f, or leaves Y∖Z closed and holding f). X∖Y is closed; its link-connected parts R_i share no link with one another, so each is closed, and each shares a link with Y because X is link-connected; X ↦ (Y, {R_i}) is injective and L(X) = L(Y) ∪ ⋃ L(R_i). With S_N the sum over the polymers of at most N faces, S_{N+1}(a) ≤ Σ_Y Ψ(Y) e^{a|L(Y)|} Σ_k (c|L(Y)|S_N)^k/k! = T(a + cS_N) ≤ T(a + cλ) ≤ λ, by induction from S₀ = 0. ∎ The search counts Y at its canonical root f. THE SURPLUS: the non-minimal first closures the search also counts are not roots of this decomposition, so their line in the leaf is an over-count, and their constant 4 — below K_r 4^d, as VI-m says — under-charges nothing.

THE ENGINE'S TEST IN THAT FORM. The engine weighs a face e^{2α_e|f|} and every link of a closed set lies in two faces or more, so its left side is at least T(4α_e). Put a + cλ = 4α_e and λ = LHS(α_e): Kotecký–Preiss at the rate a holds when λ ≤ a, that is when LHS(α_e) ≤ 4α_e/(1 + c). At c = 3 — S1's 1 for the forest neighbours and 2 for the cycle neighbours, added — this is the landed test LHS(α) ≤ α. The seat iii-bb's reading "LHS(α(1 + c/4)) ≤ α" therefore charges the composites twice: its c is the cost in excess of the tree's 3, and its figure 0.7056 "at c = 1" is the ratio at a cost of 4 a neighbour. Record i (the centre of the 2,000 line, ρ 0.8004, LHS(0.015) = 8.467068·10⁻³), the ratio LHS·(1 + c)/0.06 at every cost: c = 1, 0.2822; 2, 0.4234; 3, 0.5645 (the landed reading, unchanged); 4, 0.7056; 5, 0.8467; 6, 0.9878; 7, 1.1289; the centre turns at c = 0.06/LHS − 1 = 6.086. The same slack read as a rate: at c = 3 the census certifies the rate a = 0.06 − 3 × 8.467068·10⁻³ = 0.0346, the sum through a link at most 0.00847. AD's line (record f, ρ 0.82, 0.9309) turns at c = 3.297; the 2,000 line's one-sigma corner (1.8020) holds only below c = 1.22 and fails at the tree's costs, as it failed. Every one a reading of a fit (R1), under C = K = 1 on the tail and the constant 4. No reading of ADDENDA VI-a to VI-k changes: each was taken at the tree's load.

WHAT IS PROVED OF (M) IN THE MIXED GAS. (a) A FOREST NEIGHBOUR COSTS EXACTLY 1: if L(R_i) ∩ L(Y) holds no cycle, W(Y ∪ ⋃ R_i) = W(Y) Π W(R_i) — Lemma S3, whose proof (a gauge transformation at R_i's vertices moves its integrand off the integrated links; a forest is gauged to the identity) reads nothing of the faces' lengths or spins. Every one of the 2,038,656 is such a set: on a closed set of three polygon faces every link lies in exactly two, the links are two boundaries meeting in one path (a second path or a touching vertex would break the third boundary), so every cycle of its links bounds a subset of its faces, and d = 2 says no cycle bounds on both sides.
