WHAT STANDS: every one weighs at most 16Πw at spin ½ — a forest pair the product of its parts' own weights, 4 · 4, 4 · 2 or 2 · 2, a projective plane weighing 2; the 12,144 by K_r 4^d at r = 1, d = 2 (S1 (q) NOTES I and IV), their exact weights not read — so the bound 9·T₃² and the comparison with the load stand, and a neighbour costs at most 1 on all of them under the engine's charge. WHAT DOES NOT: "every one … is such a set" and "their weight is the product, 16Πw a set".] Their weight is the product, 16Πw a set at spin ½, and together they weigh at most 9·T₃² = 6.48·10⁻⁶ through a link (T₃ = 8.485153·10⁻⁴ the twelve-term gas's 621 closed threes at the engine's weights, record i; nine the links of a three at most), where the load the test puts on those threes alone is at least (1 − e^{−0.045·7})·T₃ = 2.29·10⁻⁴: at the centre the tree is charged thirty-five times what its first composites weigh. (b) A NEIGHBOUR ON ONE CYCLE COSTS 2: S4′ on branch-free plaquette surfaces, attained; for constituents of disc faces with four-fold contact links, the gluing theorem of S1 (q) in its exhausted region. (c) Two or more cycles glued at once, contact rank beyond that region, six-fold links: the gluing input the strong-coupling theorem itself carries (THE PROOF, Step 17). The mixed certificate inherits that input and adds none.

WHAT THE MIXED CERTIFICATE DOES NOT YET PRICE, restated after this examination: (1) the tail's constant on contact leaves (ADDENDA VI-b to VI-k: a proved bound 4Cθ^F); (2) THE DRESSING PROPER — κ = 0.004311 is the plaquette gas's root (the counting constants of its lemma are plaquette constants), and the integer-spin decorations of a mixed leaf by six- and eight-link faces have their own activities and their own count. WHAT IS COUNTED. At five faces or fewer every closed set of the twelve-term gas is Z₂-minimal, so a six-face closed link-connected set with a closed part is two closed threes on disjoint faces sharing a link, in one way only (two splittings would leave a closed set of two or four faces).

TWO ROADS, sharing the face templates and no step of a search. (2) The mode `pairs`: the closed threes through a link built with no search — a face through the link, a face through one of its links, the third their sum mod 2 when that is a face: 621, the census's number (met) — each paired with the closed threes on other faces through any of its links: 2,222,454 ordered pairs, 367,596 with the partner through the root link too, so 2,222,454 − 367,596 / 2 sets.

THE READING, both roads: 2,038,656 through a link (532,436 translation classes); d = 2 on every one. By the cycle rank of the links the two parts share — the set's contact rank, a closed three having no contact link of its own — 2,026,512 at rank 0 (two pieces) and 12,144 at rank 1 (2,208 of two pieces, 9,936 of three), nothing above; the two roads agree on the split (K-9d, met). With the 11,363,179 Z₂-minimal sets the twelve-term gas has 13,401,835 six-face polymers through a link; `cp_mixgas8`'s tree counts 584,660 of the 2,038,656, a surplus by VI-n.

THE 12,144, AND THE CLAUSE OF VI-n (a) THEY REFUTE. VI-n (a) says every one of the 2,038,656 is a forest gluing, because on a closed set of three polygon faces every cycle of its links bounds a subset of its faces. That holds on 549 of the 621 closed threes through a link, the spheres, and fails on 72, which are PROJECTIVE PLANES: three skew hexagons at one corner b of a cube — b, its three neighbours and the three face-diagonal vertices; each hexagon holds two of the three links at b and four of the six outer links; nine links, each in two hexagons; 7 − 9 + 3 = 1. Two of its faces meet in two paths, the second run in the opposite sense, and their sum is still one polygon. Its links hold the boundaries of the three plaquettes at b, cycles that bound no subset of its faces; a second closed three through such a cycle shares a cycle with it that does not bound on both sides: contact rank 1 with d = 2. WHAT STANDS: the bound. Each of the 2,038,656 is at most 16 at spin ½ by K_r 4^d with r ≤ 1 and d = 2 (S1 (q) NOTES I and IV, polygon faces), so VI-n's 9·T₃² is a bound and its comparison with the load is unmoved. WHAT DOES NOT: "their weight is the product, 16Πw". On the 2,026,512 forest pairs the weight is the product of the parts' own, 4·4, 4·2 or 2·2 — a projective plane weighs 2 at spin ½, one piece of Euler characteristic 1 (Lemma O's bound, here an equality); on the 12,144 it is a cycle gluing in the mixed gas, at six faces already, and its exact weights are not read here. For the leaves: the constant 4 over-charges the 72 projective-plane threes through a link by a factor two.

STILL RUNNING: the same with one eight-link face (`rank 6 1 ext`), where the contact rank and d of the closed-part rows say whether a shared cycle bounds on both sides; its record is a later entry.

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-p — THE EXACT WEIGHTS OF THE 2,038,656 AT SPIN ½: 16, 8, 4 ON THE FOREST PAIRS, 0 ON THE 9,936 AND 8 ON THE 2,208 — A CYCLE ONE-SIDED IN BOTH PARTS COSTS 2 WITH NO CYCLE BOUNDING ON BOTH SIDES, ON NON-ORIENTABLE PARTS (2026-11-05 10:23 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the desktop seat db5968f8; record `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_closing_ext.2026-11-05c.txt`, landed b75e1f1ac; it answers the question the trunk put after ADDENDUM VI-o: what a cycle shared without bounding on both sides costs).**

READ (the pairs road, each pair by the cycle rank of its shared links and by its two closed threes' Euler characteristics; counts through a link). Shared links a forest: two spheres 1,594,056; a sphere and a projective plane 407,688; two projective planes 24,768. Shared links holding a cycle: two spheres NONE; a sphere and a projective plane 9,936; two projective planes 2,208. The 9,936 and the 2,208 are exactly the extension's rank-one rows of three pieces and of two pieces (ADDENDUM VI-o): two counts by two roads that share no step.

DERIVED FROM THAT (exact; the tools are Lemma S3 and the pieces, ADDENDUM VI-m). A closed three is one closed surface with no contact link, so it weighs 2^χ in modulus: 4 a sphere, 2 a projective plane. FOREST PAIRS: the product of the parts' own weights — 16 on 1,594,056, 8 on 407,688, 4 on 24,768. PAIRS SHARING A CYCLE γ: fix the gauge on the other shared links, a forest, and cut each part along γ; a is the holonomy of γ. A sphere falls into two discs, each bounded by γ once: 2·2·(tr a)². A projective plane in which γ is one-sided is cut open into ONE disc whose boundary runs twice round γ: 2 tr(a²). The piece counts say which case holds: three pieces is a sphere cut in two and a projective plane cut open, two pieces is two projective planes cut open; γ never bounds in both (d = 2). Hence
- a sphere and a projective plane: ∫ 4 (tr a)² · 2 tr(a²) = 8 ∫ (1 + χ₁)(χ₁ − 1) = 0 — the 9,936 weigh NOTHING;
- two projective planes: ∫ 2 tr(a²) · 2 tr(a²) = 4 ∫ (χ₁ − 1)² = 8, against the parts' 2 · 2 — the 2,208 weigh TWICE the product.

WHAT IT SAYS. (1) The cost 2 of a cycle gluing is not confined to a cycle that bounds on both sides: it is attained at six faces of the twelve-term gas with d = 2, on a cycle one-sided in both parts. S4′ is stated for orientable surfaces and does not cover these; the form that does is the class-function gluing of S1 (q) NOTES II and IV — along a two-fold cycle a part's amplitude is γ₀ + γ₁χ₁, the composite is γ₀γ₀′ + γ₁γ₁′ — with γ₁/γ₀ = −1 for a projective plane on a one-sided cycle and +1 for a sphere on any cycle: 1 + 1 for two spheres or two projective planes, 1 − 1 for one of each. (2) The tree's load, 1 for a forest neighbour and 2 for a cycle neighbour, covers every one of the 2,038,656: the largest cost is 2. (3) At spin ½ the exact weights are at most 16 everywhere, as K_r 4^d says, and the constants summed through a link, each set once and unweighted by its faces' activities, are 16 · 1,594,056 + 8 · 407,688 + 4 · 24,768 + 8 · 2,208 = 28,883,136, where 16 a set gives 32,618,496. These weights are derived from the kinds and the piece counts, not read by an integral over each set; an evaluator of S on polygon faces (`latobj` reads plaquettes only) is the instrument that would read them.

**§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-q — THE DRESSING'S COUNT FOR A LEAF OF POLYGON FACES, PER LINK: A LEMMA THAT READS OF A FACE ONLY ITS LINKS, PROVED AS A COUNT; ITS WEIGHTS ARE INPUTS; THE ROOT AT THE CENSUSES' RATE IS 1.8 TO 2.6 TIMES THE ENGINE'S κ ON A PLAQUETTE AND 3.5 TO 5.3 TIMES ON AN EIGHT-LINK FACE (2026-11-05 10:32 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; asked for by the seat iii-bb at 10:19 for the response table of its record m, running; the desk values by an awk series for the Bessel ratios, the plaquette case met by its closed form).**

WHY. ADDENDUM VI-n named the dressing proper as the mixed certificate's unpriced part. The engine dresses every face by one e^{κ}, κ = 0.004311: the strong-coupling certificate's root at the plaquette coupling, computed at α = 0.002 and held at every α and every face length (RESULTS 10: "κ = 0.004311 held at every α"). S1 §14 item 2's root is per plaquette, and its counting constants — two wall sites a plaquette, eight piece roots, five choices a step — are plaquette geometry. For a leaf of polygon faces the count is stated here per LINK and proved.

THE OBJECTS. U a closed set of faces. A DECORATION Δ of U is a finite set of faces, none of U, such that (P) every link of every face of Δ lies on L(U) or in two faces or more of Δ, and (C) U ∪ Δ is link-connected: (C) is what makes U ∪ Δ one polymer, and without (P) the weight is zero when Δ carries integer spins (a link through one such face alone integrates to zero). WALLS AND CORE: scan Δ from its greatest face down and remove a face when every link of it lies on L(U) or in two OTHER faces still present, and what is left stays link-connected with U; the faces removed are the walls W, the rest the core Δ′. A wall weighs y₁(g), a core face y_p(g). Put ζ := sup over links of Σ_{g ∋ ℓ} y₁(g)/|g| and B(κ) := sup over links of Σ_{g ∋ ℓ} y_p(g) e^{κ|g|/2}.

LEMMA (the count). (i) Every link of every wall lies on L(U ∪ Δ′). (ii) Δ′ has (P) and (C). (iii) If κ′ ≥ 0 and b := B(ζ + κ′) < 1 satisfy b²/(1 − b) ≤ 1 − e^{−κ′}, then Σ over the decorations of U of Π_W y₁ Π_{Δ′} y_p ≤ e^{(ζ + κ′)|L(U)|} ≤ Π_{f ∈ U} e^{(ζ + κ′)|f|/2}.

PROOF. (i), (ii): a removal at a link leaves two faces there, and every later removal at that link leaves two again; a link where nothing was removed keeps what (P) gave it; connectedness is the rule's second clause. (iii), the walls: for a fixed core the sets of walls are subsets of the faces off U ∪ Δ′ with every link on L(U ∪ Δ′), so they weigh at most exp(Σ y₁) over those faces, and such a face is counted at each of its |g| links: at most exp(ζ|L(U ∪ Δ′)|). The links of Δ′ off L(U) lie in two core faces each, so |L(U ∪ Δ′)| ≤ |L(U)| + Σ_{g ∈ Δ′} |g|/2, and Σ_Δ ≤ e^{ζ|L(U)|} Σ_{Δ′} Π_{g ∈ Δ′} y_p(g) e^{ζ|g|/2}. The cores, by exploration: keep a queue of base links, at first L(U). At the head link take the least remaining core face through it and run the closing rule with the base's links exempt — at each step a remaining face through the least link that is off the base and in exactly one face taken so far — until no such link is left; the faces taken are a PIECE, its links off the base lie in two of its faces each and join the queue; repeat at the same link while a core face through it remains, then drop the link. By (C) the core is exhausted. A piece has two faces or more: one face alone would have every link on the base, that is on L(U) or on a link held by two other core faces, and removing it disconnects nothing, so the scan would have removed it as a wall. [ADDED 2026-11-05 11:04, the step spelled out: the base's links are those of U and of the pieces already taken; a link brought by an earlier piece lies in two faces of that piece, core faces the scan never removed, so at this face's turn in the scan both were present and the scan's first clause held; and every face sharing a link with it shares that link with a face of U or with one of those core faces, which reach U through the pieces taken before it, so a path through it is rerouted and the second clause held too. **§35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-s — THE COUNT LEMMA OF ADDENDUM VI-q READ BY A SECOND SEAT: SIGNED AS A COUNT, WITH THREE REMARKS AND NO HOLE; READ AT THE DESK STEP BY STEP AND BY MACHINE ON EVERY DECORATION OF AT MOST SIXTEEN FACES OF THREE CLOSED SETS (2026-11-05 11:22 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the desktop seat db5968f8, at the trunk seat's request; instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/decocheck.c`, record `decocheck.2026-11-05a.txt`, landed 737b49af0).**

WHAT IS SIGNED: clauses (i), (ii) and (iii) of the lemma as VI-q states them, for any family of faces and any fixed orders of the scan and of the exploration. WHAT IS NOT: that a wall and a core face of a real decoration weigh at most y₁(g) and y_p(g) — the lemma's inputs — and the engine's root and the desk values computed from them.

READ AT THE DESK. (i) A face is removed at a link off L(U) only when two OTHER faces are present there, so a removal needs three and leaves two, and the last removal at a link leaves two faces that are never removed: a wall's link off L(U) lies in TWO core faces, more than (i) says and what (ii) uses. (ii) (P) of the core: a link of a core face off L(U) where nothing was removed keeps the two faces (P) gave it; where something was, the last removal left two. (C) is the scan's second clause at every removal. (iii), THE WALLS — the place the trunk said to look first, the count by a per-link share. For a fixed core Δ′ the wall sets are distinct subsets of A = the faces off U ∪ Δ′ with every link on L(U ∪ Δ′), so they weigh at most Π_A (1 + y₁) ≤ exp Σ_A y₁; and Σ_{g ∈ A} y₁(g) = Σ_{g ∈ A} Σ_{ℓ ∈ g} y₁(g)/|g| = Σ_{ℓ ∈ L(U ∪ Δ′)} Σ_{g ∈ A, g ∋ ℓ} y₁(g)/|g| ≤ ζ |L(U ∪ Δ′)|, an identity before the last step because EVERY link of a face of A is on L(U ∪ Δ′): the share 1/|g| is collected at each of the |g| links, once each. The count by links is exact where a count by sites was not. |L(U ∪ Δ′)| ≤ |L(U)| + Σ_{Δ′} |g|/2 by (P) of the core. THE CORES. (b) A piece of one face g has every link on the base. A base link is on L(U) or was brought by an earlier piece, where it lies in two faces of that piece: core faces, other than g, present at every moment of the scan. So at g's turn the scan's first clause held. Every face that shares a link with g shares a base link, hence a link with a face of U or with those two core faces; and every face of an earlier piece reaches U through the faces of the pieces up to its own (the first face of a piece through its head link, a base link; each next through a link of its piece). So removing g cuts nothing off and the second clause held: the scan would have removed g. Hence no piece of one face, and the series starts at b². (c) At each step of a piece the link is determined by the base and the faces taken, and the faces through one link weigh at most b in sum, so the pieces of s faces at a head link weigh at most b^s whatever the base; a piece's new base links lie in two of its faces, at most Σ |g|/2 of them, which the factor e^{κ′|g|/2} of each face pays; the pieces at one head link come in a determined order, so k of them weigh at most (b²/(1 − b))^k. With H ≤ e^{κ′q} for fewer faces, or the same faces and a shorter queue (k = 0 drops the head link alone, so the induction is on the pair): H(q) ≤ e^{κ′(q − 1)} / (1 − b²/(1 − b)) ≤ e^{κ′q}. (d) A link leaves the queue only when no core face through it remains, so no face taken later holds a dropped link; every link of U and of every piece is queued; by (C) every core face is reached.

THREE REMARKS, none a hole. (1) "A remaining face through the least link" is to be read as a fixed choice, the least remaining core face; the bound sums over every face through the link, so any fixed choice serves, and by (P) of the core a remaining one exists (the link is off the base, so no earlier piece holds a face through it). (2) The cores summed over are the sets that ARE the core of some decoration: (b) is proved for them through the scan of the decoration they came from, its walls present. A set with (P) and (C) need not be its own core, and the lemma does not need it to be. (3) The word OTHER in the scan's first clause is what (b) and (ii) stand on: the machine's control below reads the lemma false without it.

READ BY MACHINE. `decocheck.c` enumerates every decoration of a closed set — every set of faces with (P) and (C), each once — runs the scan and the exploration as VI-q states them and checks (i), (ii), (b), that the closing rule is never short of a face and leaves each link of a piece off the base in two of its faces, and (d). Plaquettes in three dimensions, a box of eight vertices a side; U the unit cube, the surface of two cubes, an L of three; decorations of at most sixteen faces: 6,227, 16,293 and 36,175; the two orders ascending, descending, and each turned round alone: twelve runs, NO CLAUSE FAILING ON ANY DECORATION. Up to five walls beside cores of up to sixteen faces; pieces of two faces occur (11 on the two-cube box, 38 on the L), so the lemma's "two or more" is attained. Known answers: the unit cube to five faces has six decorations, the cubes glued on its faces (met); THE SIGHTED CONTROL: with the scan's first clause read without OTHER the clauses fail on the two-cube box to ten faces — (ii) on 288 decorations, a piece of one face on 126 (met). The partial sums of y₁^walls y_p^core lie far under the bound (1.04 against 18.3 on the L at y₁ = 0.02, y_p = 0.05): they could have refuted (iii) and did not; they prove nothing of it. The landing commit's message says "walls up to four" and "pieces of four to sixteen faces"; the record reads five and two.

THE STATUS LINE OF VI-q IS ANSWERED HERE: the lemma is signed by this seat as a count.

THE STATEMENTS. Let S be a closed link-connected set of polygon faces at spin ½ — plaquettes and the six- and eight-link classes of the fitted action, each face a disc whose boundary is a closed self-avoiding walk — with every link of S in exactly two faces of S (branch-free), and let w be a polygon face not in S whose links all lie in S, its boundary meeting no other wall. S7′: |W(S ∪ w)| = 3a₁(t_w) · W(S) exactly, only spin 1 on w contributing, a₁(t_w) = I₃(t_w)/I₁(t_w) at the wall's own coupling. S8′: for lone walls w₁, …, w_k on S whose boundary cycles are pairwise link-disjoint, W(S ∪ {w_i}) = Π_i 3a₁(t_{w_i}) · W(S) · Π_v c(D_v), with c(D_v) the chord-diagram number of S8 at each vertex of S where two or more boundaries meet, |c(D_v)| ≤ 1 for every diagram; on a non-orientable S the entries' signs may change and the bound |c| ≤ 1 does not (§15 (n), (ii)).

THE PROOFS, READ. S7's five steps: (1) gauge-fix all links of ∂w but one — ∂w is a cycle, so n − 1 of its n links are a forest and the chord carries the wall's holonomy z; (2) cut S along ∂w — a cycle in S with each of its links in exactly two faces of S, so its two sides give two boundary curves each going round once, or one going round twice (one-sided); (3)–(4) the pieces integrate to d^{χ−n} Π_b χ_½(z_b) with both boundary holonomies z^{±1} (S4′'s piece formula, whose proof glues faces across interior links by ∫ χ_½(AU) χ_½(U⁻¹B) dU = ½ χ_½(AB) and reads of a face only that its character is χ_½ of the product of its links around the disc — the rule is Migdal's and is face-shape free), so S's integral is ∝ χ_½(z)² or ∝ χ_½(z²) = χ₁(z) − 1, and ∫ χ_½² χ_j / ∫ χ_½² = δ_{j1}, the one-sided ratio −δ_{j1}; (5) the wall's own weight is Σ_j d_j a_j(t_w) χ_j(z), so |W(S ∪ w)| = 3a₁(t_w) W(S). Nothing in the five steps counts the wall's links or S's faces' lengths. S8's proof: (i) locality — after S's links off T = ⋃∂w_i are integrated, every T-link carries exactly three strands, the two boundary traversals and the wall's spin-1 strand, so its Haar integral is the rank-one projector onto Inv(½ ⊗ ½* ⊗ 1), and the integral over T is a product over vertices of contractions of the projectors' halves along the corner pairings — the corners of S's faces at the vertex, which a polygon face has at each of its vertices exactly as a plaquette has; (ii) the local values — the per-link projector (1/6)(σ_μ)_{αβ}(σ_ν)_{β′α′} against ½δδ, a wall's adjoint index between two consecutive links of the wall shared by two insertions at ONE vertex, so the sum factorises over vertices; a wall of n links has n vertices with two spokes at each, and at a vertex with one wall the trace Σ_ρ tr(σ_ρσ_ρ) = 6 against the no-wall loop's 2 pays the link factor 1/3 back exactly: (1/3)^n · 3^n = 1 whatever n, which is S7′ again; at a vertex with several walls the chord diagram D_v is the cyclic order of the walls' spokes in S's vertex cycle, the same object for polygon faces, and |c(D_v)| ≤ 1 because the Pauli product is unitary on ℂ². The non-orientable case: §15 (n)'s ε-insertions at orientation-reversing links pair off around every contractible index loop and leave a trace of a product of 2 × 2 unitaries, face-shape free.

WHAT IT CLOSES. In ADDENDUM VI-q's root the walls' activity y₁(c) was entered as e^{α̃ℓ_c/2}(3|a₁| + 5|a₂| + 7|a₃|)(t_c), an upper side with every integer spin: on a wall whose links lie in two rest faces each, only spin 1 contributes and the weight is exactly 3a₁(t_c) times a number of modulus at most one — S7′ and S8′, the first of the dressing's two weight inputs, PROVED for faces of any length when the rest is branch-free at the wall's links. The desk values stand to their printed digits: 5a₂ + 7a₃ is a part in three thousand of 3a₁ on every class (7.1·10⁻⁷ against 2.07·10⁻³ on the plaquette of the 2,000 line), below the fourth digit of ζ and of every root.

WHAT IT LEAVES. (1) A wall of VI-q's scan may have a link that is a junction of the rest — a link of U where a core piece attaches, in three or more faces of U ∪ core — and there the three-strand projector is not the link's integral; that wall is priced with the junction, not by S7′. (2) The core faces' rate γ: S1's junction theorem and local theorem (§21, §24) price a piece's face at 3θ⁴ a₁ from the exact maxima of plaquette star integrals; for a piece holding six- and eight-link faces the stars are other objects, unread. So the dressing's weight input narrows to the junction walls and the core rate; the count is signed (VI-s), the walls on two-face links are now priced exactly, and the root's values stand.
